    *   The AMD framework is well-designed and demonstrates strong performance.
    *   The paper provides thorough experimental evaluations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper focuses primarily on facial manipulation and may not generalize well to other types of multimedia manipulation. While they have a category for pure text generation, this is generally to accompany facial edits.
    *   The paper relies heavily on the assumption that MLLM-generated text will maintain semantic consistency with the manipulated image. While this is a reasonable assumption, it may not always hold true, particularly as MLLMs continue to evolve. The paper provides analysis of MLLM generalization but perhaps a more targeted analysis of dataset artifacts and prompt engineering would be valuable.
    *   The 'broad impact and limitation' section identifies that the data mainly focuses on "high-risk manipulations of celebrity faces in multimodal news". This limits generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence research in multimedia manipulation detection. The MDSM dataset and AMD framework could become standard tools for evaluating and developing new detection methods. The paper's emphasis on MLLM-driven disinformation could also inspire further research into the development of more robust and adaptable detection systems.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper's novelty and significance are substantial, and it addresses a timely and important problem. The MDSM dataset and AMD framework represent valuable contributions to the field. However, the paper's limitations, particularly its narrow focus on facial manipulation and its reliance on certain assumptions about MLLM behavior, prevent it from achieving a higher score. Furthermore, the dataset's focus on celebrities limits generalizability somewhat. Although it represents a novel improvement, there is a substantial amount of similar work. A higher score would require significantly broader generalizability.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reverse-Speech-Finder: A Neural Network Backtracking Architecture for Generating Alzheimer's Disease Speech Samples and Improving Diagnosis Performance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17477v1)
- **Authors**: Victor OK Li, Yang Han, Jacqueline CK Lam, Lawrence YL Cheung
- **Abstract**: This study introduces Reverse-Speech-Finder (RSF), a groundbreaking neural network backtracking architecture designed to enhance Alzheimer's Disease (AD) diagnosis through speech analysis. Leveraging the power of pre-trained large language models, RSF identifies and utilizes the most probable AD-specific speech markers, addressing both the scarcity of real AD speech samples and the challenge of limited interpretability in existing models. RSF's unique approach consists of three core innovations: Firstly, it exploits the observation that speech markers most probable of predicting AD, defined as the most probable speech-markers (MPMs), must have the highest probability of activating those neurons (in the neural network) with the highest probability of predicting AD, defined as the most probable neurons (MPNs). Secondly, it utilizes a speech token representation at the input layer, allowing backtracking from MPNs to identify the most probable speech-tokens (MPTs) of AD. Lastly, it develops an innovative backtracking method to track backwards from the MPNs to the input layer, identifying the MPTs and the corresponding MPMs, and ingeniously uncovering novel speech markers for AD detection. Experimental results demonstrate RSF's superiority over traditional methods such as SHAP and Integrated Gradients, achieving a 3.5% improvement in accuracy and a 3.2% boost in F1-score. By generating speech data that encapsulates novel markers, RSF not only mitigates the limitations of real data scarcity but also significantly enhances the robustness and accuracy of AD diagnostic models. These findings underscore RSF's potential as a transformative tool in speech-based AD detection, offering new insights into AD-related linguistic deficits and paving the way for more effective non-invasive early intervention strategies.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reverse-Speech-Finder (RSF), a novel neural network architecture for improving Alzheimer's Disease (AD) diagnosis through speech analysis. RSF leverages pre-trained large language models (LLMs) to identify and utilize the most probable AD-specific speech markers. The method involves three key innovations: (1) exploiting the observation that speech markers most probable of predicting AD must have the highest probability of activating neurons with the highest probability of predicting AD; (2) utilizing speech token representation at the input layer to allow backtracking from these neurons to identify the most probable speech tokens (MPTs) of AD; (3) developing an innovative backtracking method to identify MPTs and their corresponding most probable markers (MPMs). Experiments demonstrate that RSF outperforms traditional methods in accuracy and F1-score, and generates synthetic speech data that mitigates the limitations of real data scarcity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper presents a genuinely novel architecture for AD diagnosis using speech, particularly in its reverse engineering approach. The backtracking method from MPNs to identify MPTs is a distinct contribution. Prior works mostly focus on forward approaches or static analysis of speech markers, while RSF attempts to actively discover and generate data based on influential markers. Generating synthetic data enriched with discovered novel markers is also a significant advancement, addressing the key problem of limited real-world AD speech data. However, the individual components, like LLMs for feature extraction and data generation, are not novel on their own, but the *integration* of these components in a reverse-engineering framework is new.

*Significance:*

The significance stems from several factors.  Firstly, the improved accuracy and F1-score (3.5% and 3.2%, respectively) are meaningful improvements, particularly in medical diagnostic contexts where even small gains can significantly impact patient outcomes. Secondly, identifying novel speech markers contributes valuable insights into AD-related linguistic deficits and opens new avenues for research.  The ability to generate synthetic speech data is crucial for overcoming data scarcity and improving the robustness and generalizability of AD diagnostic models. Lastly, the interpretable nature of RSF makes it more appealing to clinicians and researchers compared to black-box deep learning models.

*Strengths:*

*   **Novel Architecture:** The reverse engineering approach is a creative departure from traditional methods.
*   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in accuracy and F1-score.
*   **Interpretability:** The system provides insights into relevant speech markers.
*   **Data Augmentation:**  The method addresses the problem of data scarcity by generating synthetic speech samples.
*   **Thorough Experimentation:** It compares with other established LLM-enhanced speech data augmentation methodologies.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Dataset Dependency:**  The system's performance is likely dependent on the quality and representativeness of the initial training dataset (Pitt Corpus). The generalizability of the identified markers across diverse populations and linguistic backgrounds may be limited. The paper acknowledges this limitation, however, this is a major point of concern.
*   **Synthetic Data Quality:**  While the generated data helps, it's still synthetic, and the generated data cannot fully replace the value of real human speech data.  The potential for biases or artifacts introduced during data generation must be carefully considered.
*   **Limited novelty in certain components:** While RSF presents a novel architecture, it does rely on existing techniques like LLMs and causal tracing.

*Potential Influence:*

RSF can influence speech-based AD detection research by promoting the use of reverse engineering approaches, encouraging the development of more interpretable models, and facilitating data augmentation techniques to address the data scarcity problem. It may also impact the development of early detection tools for AD, potentially enabling earlier intervention and improved patient outcomes.

*Justification of Score:*

The paper is innovative and presents statistically significant results and addresses a critical limitation within the field. It offers a novel method to improve upon existing practices, showing a genuine improvement over baseline approaches. This, while tempered by some limitations, is a positive contribution that moves the field forward.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Simultaneous Modeling of Protein Conformation and Dynamics via Autoregression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17478v1)
- **Authors**: Yuning Shen, Lihao Wang, Huizhuo Yuan, Yan Wang, Bangji Yang, Quanquan Gu
- **Abstract**: Understanding protein dynamics is critical for elucidating their biological functions. The increasing availability of molecular dynamics (MD) data enables the training of deep generative models to efficiently explore the conformational space of proteins. However, existing approaches either fail to explicitly capture the temporal dependencies between conformations or do not support direct generation of time-independent samples. To address these limitations, we introduce ConfRover, an autoregressive model that simultaneously learns protein conformation and dynamics from MD trajectories, supporting both time-dependent and time-independent sampling. At the core of our model is a modular architecture comprising: (i) an encoding layer, adapted from protein folding models, that embeds protein-specific information and conformation at each time frame into a latent space; (ii) a temporal module, a sequence model that captures conformational dynamics across frames; and (iii) an SE(3) diffusion model as the structure decoder, generating conformations in continuous space. Experiments on ATLAS, a large-scale protein MD dataset of diverse structures, demonstrate the effectiveness of our model in learning conformational dynamics and supporting a wide range of downstream tasks. ConfRover is the first model to sample both protein conformations and trajectories within a single framework, offering a novel and flexible approach for learning from protein MD data.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and novelty/significance score for the provided paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CONFROVER, an autoregressive deep generative model designed for simultaneously learning protein conformation and dynamics from molecular dynamics (MD) trajectory data. Unlike existing methods that either fail to capture temporal dependencies or don't support direct time-independent sampling, CONFROVER can generate both protein conformations *and* trajectories within a single framework. The model uses a modular architecture consisting of: 1) an encoding layer (adapted from protein folding models) to embed protein-specific information and conformation; 2) a temporal module (a sequence model) to capture dynamics across frames; and 3) an SE(3) diffusion model as the structure decoder to generate conformations in continuous space. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of CONFROVER on the ATLAS dataset, showcasing its ability to perform trajectory simulation, time-independent conformation sampling, and conformation interpolation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Framework:** The primary strength of the paper lies in its unification of three key protein structure and dynamics tasks (trajectory simulation, time-independent sampling, and conformation interpolation) within a single autoregressive framework. This is a significant step forward, as prior work typically treated these as separate problems requiring distinct models.
    *   **Explicit Temporal Modeling:** The use of a sequence model (Llama) in the latent space to capture temporal dependencies is well-motivated and likely contributes to the model's ability to generate realistic trajectories. Addressing the non-Markovian nature of MD simulations is a key advantage.
    *   **Continuous Space Conformation Generation:** The adoption of an SE(3) diffusion model avoids the need for discretizing the protein conformational space, which can lead to quantization errors. This is crucial for accurately representing protein structures.
    *   **Empirical Results:** The experiments on the large-scale ATLAS dataset are comprehensive and demonstrate CONFROVER's effectiveness in various tasks. The comparison against state-of-the-art baselines, especially in trajectory simulation, is convincing. The results demonstrate strong capabilities, including outperforming MDGEN and matching Alphaflow and CONFDIFF in time-dependent generation.
    *   **Interpolation Capability:** The ability to perform conformation interpolation, and improvement upon the initial model by incorporating explicit training in that direction, highlights the versatility of their architecture.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Baselines for Interpolation:** The lack of strong baselines for conformation interpolation makes it difficult to fully assess the novelty and performance of CONFROVER in this task. A more detailed analysis, potentially involving visual inspection or metrics related to functional relevance, would strengthen this section.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper provides information on training and inference costs, it is still substantial (using 8 NVIDIA H100 GPUs). More detailed comparisons of computational efficiency against traditional MD simulation, or other generative models, would be helpful. This impacts its accessibility.
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Components:** The model relies on a pre-trained OpenFold model for encoding and an initialized CONFDIFF diffusion decoder. While leveraging these components is practical, it also somewhat limits the novelty of the approach. The integration is well-executed, but it reduces the "from-scratch" contribution.
    *   **Marginal Performance in Time-Independent Sampling:** While the results are competitive in time-independent sampling, the model does not drastically outperform existing specialized methods. This indicates a potential area for future improvement.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the authors perform a reasonable evaluation, certain evaluation metrics are somewhat preliminary, such as in the interpolation task. The relatively small time-scales of the MD simulations and the focus on local dynamics might not fully capture the long-term, large-scale conformational changes relevant to protein function. This is largely a dataset limitation, but it should be acknowledged.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The unified approach for learning protein conformation and dynamics, incorporating explicit temporal modeling and a continuous space representation, is a novel contribution.
    *   The use of a latent causal modeling framework coupled with SE(3) diffusion, demonstrates that transformers, usually used on tokens, can be effective for protein dynamics, which is not immediately obvious and valuable to the field.
    *   The demonstration of conformation interpolation within this framework, though not heavily validated, is potentially impactful.
    *   While some components are adapted from existing models, the overall architecture and training strategy are original and well-integrated.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of protein structure and dynamics modeling by unifying multiple tasks within a single, autoregressive framework. The model's ability to explicitly capture temporal dependencies and generate conformations in continuous space is a notable strength. While the reliance on pre-trained components and the limited baselines for interpolation somewhat reduce the overall novelty, the comprehensiveness of the experiments and the competitive performance on the ATLAS dataset make this a solid contribution. The limitations mentioned above prevent it from scoring higher. CONFROVER offers a compelling approach that is likely to influence future research in this area. Therefore, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Twin-2K-500: A dataset for building digital twins of over 2,000 people based on their answers to over 500 questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17479v1)
- **Authors**: Olivier Toubia, George Z. Gui, Tianyi Peng, Daniel J. Merlau, Ang Li, Haozhe Chen
- **Abstract**: LLM-based digital twin simulation, where large language models are used to emulate individual human behavior, holds great promise for research in AI, social science, and digital experimentation. However, progress in this area has been hindered by the scarcity of real, individual-level datasets that are both large and publicly available. This lack of high-quality ground truth limits both the development and validation of digital twin methodologies. To address this gap, we introduce a large-scale, public dataset designed to capture a rich and holistic view of individual human behavior. We survey a representative sample of $N = 2,058$ participants (average 2.42 hours per person) in the US across four waves with 500 questions in total, covering a comprehensive battery of demographic, psychological, economic, personality, and cognitive measures, as well as replications of behavioral economics experiments and a pricing survey. The final wave repeats tasks from earlier waves to establish a test-retest accuracy baseline. Initial analyses suggest the data are of high quality and show promise for constructing digital twins that predict human behavior well at the individual and aggregate levels. By making the full dataset publicly available, we aim to establish a valuable testbed for the development and benchmarking of LLM-based persona simulations. Beyond LLM applications, due to its unique breadth and scale the dataset also enables broad social science research, including studies of cross-construct correlations and heterogeneous treatment effects.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Twin-2K-500: A dataset for building digital twins of over 2,000 people based on their answers to over 500 questions" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Twin-2K-500, a large-scale, publicly available dataset designed to facilitate the development and validation of LLM-based digital twins.  The dataset comprises survey responses from over 2,000 participants across four waves, covering a wide range of demographic, psychological, cognitive, economic, and behavioral measures (including replications of classic behavioral economics experiments).  The fourth wave of data repeats tasks from prior waves to provide a test-retest accuracy benchmark.  The authors perform initial analyses suggesting data quality and demonstrate promising results in constructing digital twins capable of predicting individual and aggregate behavior. They release the dataset publicly to encourage further research in LLM-based persona simulation and broader social science investigations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary contribution lies in the *creation and public release* of a uniquely comprehensive dataset. Datasets combining psychological profiles, behavioral economics experiments, and demographics at this scale are rare, especially with public availability. While using LLMs for digital twins isn't inherently new, the *availability of ground truth data to train and validate these twins is a significant barrier*.  The inclusion of a test-retest wave is also a valuable asset, enabling researchers to benchmark the performance of digital twins against human consistency. The effort the authors put into assembling such a rich and diverse dataset is commendable.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact is substantial, primarily because the dataset lowers the entry barrier for researchers wanting to explore LLM-based persona simulations. It provides:

    *   A common ground for benchmarking different digital twin methodologies.
    *   A foundation for studying the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in emulating human behavior across diverse domains.
    *   A valuable resource for social scientists interested in cross-construct correlations and heterogeneous treatment effects, beyond the scope of digital twins. The breadth and depth of the data open possibilities for novel analyses within social science.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scale:** The dataset's large sample size allows for statistically robust analyses.
    *   **Breadth:** The variety of measures provides a holistic view of individual behavior, enabling researchers to build more complex and realistic digital twins.
    *   **Public Availability:** This promotes transparency, reproducibility, and collaborative research.
    *   **Test-Retest Data:** Provides a benchmark for evaluating the accuracy of digital twins.
    *   **Replication of Classic Experiments:** Allows for validation of the data and provides a direct comparison of human and LLM-simulated behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Initial Analysis:** The paper presents preliminary results, but a more in-depth analysis of digital twin performance across different tasks and subpopulations would strengthen the findings. The initial approach to DT creation also seems simple and further experiments could refine DT creation methods.
    *   **US-Centric:** The data is limited to a US sample, which may limit the generalizability of findings to other cultures or populations. It also focuses heavily on social science-based topics that can lead to different results from other topic categories.
    *   **Potential Biases:** As the authors acknowledge, LLMs can exhibit biases, and the dataset reflects potential limitations (e.g., overly trusting of medical professionals, difficulty representing conservative viewpoints). Further work is required to address the divergence from humans when it comes to replicating views across the medical and political domains.

*   **Potential Influence:** The dataset has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in the field of LLM-based simulation and social science research. It can facilitate the development of more accurate and reliable digital twins, leading to advances in areas like personalized recommendations, behavioral modeling, and experimental design.

While the paper itself presents limited empirical results, the *primary contribution is the dataset*. The dataset is valuable to the field because it enables more research to be conducted and because the dataset has unique characteristics that separate itself from previous datasets.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper receives a score of 7.5 because of the high value of the public dataset they are releasing, enabling other researchers to explore diverse digital twin creation techniques and improve the general methodology as a whole. The dataset itself has unique characteristics that are not available in previously made datasets, such as the breadth of collected questions, size of sample population, and the wave that measures test-retest accuracy. The dataset is a great resource, which enables many researchers to develop digital twins more efficiently and more accurately. In the future, it may be important to expand the scope of this research outside of the US and to diversify the question categories to be more robust.

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### MARCO: Meta-Reflection with Cross-Referencing for Code Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17481v1)
- **Authors**: Yusheng Zhao, Xiao Luo, Weizhi Zhang, Wei Ju, Zhiping Xiao, Philip S. Yu, Ming Zhang
- **Abstract**: The ability to reason is one of the most fundamental capabilities of large language models (LLMs), enabling a wide range of downstream tasks through sophisticated problem-solving. A critical aspect of this is code reasoning, which involves logical reasoning with formal languages (i.e., programming code). In this paper, we enhance this capability of LLMs by exploring the following question: how can an LLM agent become progressively smarter in code reasoning with each solution it proposes, thereby achieving substantial cumulative improvement? Most existing research takes a static perspective, focusing on isolated problem-solving using frozen LLMs. In contrast, we adopt a cognitive-evolving perspective and propose a novel framework named Meta-Reflection with Cross-Referencing (MARCO) that enables the LLM to evolve dynamically during inference through self-improvement. From the perspective of human cognitive development, we leverage both knowledge accumulation and lesson sharing. In particular, to accumulate knowledge during problem-solving, we propose meta-reflection that reflects on the reasoning paths of the current problem to obtain knowledge and experience for future consideration. Moreover, to effectively utilize the lessons from other agents, we propose cross-referencing that incorporates the solution and feedback from other agents into the current problem-solving process. We conduct experiments across various datasets in code reasoning, and the results demonstrate the effectiveness of MARCO.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces MARCO (Meta-Reflection with Cross-Referencing), a novel framework designed to enhance the code reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). MARCO takes a cognitive-evolving perspective, enabling LLMs to dynamically improve their performance during inference through self-improvement. The framework integrates two key components:

1.  **Meta-Reflection:**  This allows the LLM to reflect on its reasoning paths for a given problem, extract knowledge, and accumulate experience for future problems.
2.  **Cross-Referencing:** This enables the LLM to incorporate solutions and feedback from other agents into its problem-solving process, effectively learning from their mistakes and successes.

The authors conduct extensive experiments across various code reasoning datasets and tasks (induction, deduction, abduction), demonstrating MARCO's effectiveness compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a novel approach by focusing on the cognitive evolution of LLMs during inference for code reasoning.  Most prior work takes a static perspective. The specific combination of meta-reflection and cross-referencing to achieve this is also a unique contribution. The connection of LLM behavior to cognitive development concepts (knowledge accumulation and lesson sharing) is a nice framing.
*   **Significance:**  Enhancing the reasoning abilities of LLMs is a critical area of research. Code reasoning, in particular, is a challenging task with broad applications. MARCO represents a significant step towards creating more robust and adaptable LLMs for code-related tasks. The experimental results convincingly show the benefits of the approach. The framework is well-motivated, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of each component.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The cognitive-evolving perspective is a valuable contribution to the field.
    *   The MARCO framework is well-designed and integrates two complementary techniques effectively.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough and comprehensive, covering multiple datasets and tasks.
    *   The ablation studies provide evidence for the importance of each component of MARCO.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the concepts and methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The implementation details of the summarization and knowledge condensation components within meta-reflection could be further elaborated.
    *   While the experimental results are strong, the computational cost of MARCO compared to static approaches could be more thoroughly analyzed and discussed.
    *   The analysis of meta-reflection results, while helpful, could provide more specific examples and deeper insights into the types of knowledge the LLM is learning and how it's being applied.
    *   The potential limitations of the approach, such as its dependence on the quality of feedback from the code interpreter or the representativeness of the training data, are not discussed in detail.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on LLM reasoning and code generation.  The cognitive-evolving perspective and the specific techniques introduced in MARCO could inspire new approaches to improving the adaptability and robustness of LLMs in various domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM reasoning, particularly within the context of code. The cognitive-evolving perspective is a welcome departure from static approaches, and the MARCO framework is well-designed and thoroughly evaluated. The paper's clear writing style and comprehensive experimental results further strengthen its value. While there are some weaknesses, particularly regarding implementation details and computational cost analysis, the overall impact of the work warrants a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### PD$^3$: A Project Duplication Detection Framework via Adapted Multi-Agent Debate
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17492v1)
- **Authors**: Dezheng Bao, Yueci Yang, Xin Chen, Zhengxuan Jiang, Zeguo Fei, Daoze Zhang, Xuanwen Huang, Junru Chen, Chutian Yu, Xiang Yuan, Yang Yang
- **Abstract**: Project duplication detection is critical for project quality assessment, as it improves resource utilization efficiency by preventing investing in newly proposed project that have already been studied. It requires the ability to understand high-level semantics and generate constructive and valuable feedback. Existing detection methods rely on basic word- or sentence-level comparison or solely apply large language models, lacking valuable insights for experts and in-depth comprehension of project content and review criteria. To tackle this issue, we propose PD$^3$, a Project Duplication Detection framework via adapted multi-agent Debate. Inspired by real-world expert debates, it employs a fair competition format to guide multi-agent debate to retrieve relevant projects. For feedback, it incorporates both qualitative and quantitative analysis to improve its practicality. Over 800 real-world power project data spanning more than 20 specialized fields are used to evaluate the framework, demonstrating that our method outperforms existing approaches by 7.43% and 8.00% in two downstream tasks. Furthermore, we establish an online platform, Review Dingdang, to assist power experts, saving 5.73 million USD in initial detection on more than 100 newly proposed projects.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a concise summary, critical evaluation, and novelty/significance score for the paper "PD³: A Project Duplication Detection Framework via Adapted Multi-Agent Debate":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PD³, a framework for detecting project duplication using an adapted multi-agent debate approach. It aims to improve resource utilization by preventing investments in projects that overlap with existing research. PD³ employs a round-robin competition format among LLM-based agents to retrieve relevant projects. The framework also provides both qualitative and quantitative feedback to experts, enhancing its practicality and assisting in project refinement. Experiments using real-world power project data demonstrate the framework's superiority over existing methods in two downstream tasks. Furthermore, the authors have deployed PD³ as an online platform ("Review Dingdang") and claim substantial cost savings through duplicate project prevention.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Approach:** The use of a multi-agent debate system for project duplication detection is a genuinely innovative contribution. Adapting the MAD paradigm specifically for this retrieval problem, and especially with the round-robin approach, is well-motivated and potentially valuable.
    *   **Practical Feedback:** The inclusion of both qualitative (summaries, text comparisons) and quantitative feedback significantly enhances the framework's utility. This addresses a key weakness of many existing methods that only provide numerical similarity scores.
    *   **Real-World Validation:** The evaluation on a large, real-world dataset of power projects is a significant strength. This demonstrates the practical applicability of the framework in a specific domain with demonstrable savings for power experts
    *   **System Deployment**: Showing that the framework has been deployed and is actively being used provides a high degree of evidence for the utility of the approach. The reported savings in potential duplicate project funding further strengths the findings

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scope/Generality Concerns:** While the focus on power projects allows for domain-specific optimization, it may limit the generalizability of the framework to other fields. The review criteria and agent prompts are likely tailored to this domain.
    *   **Dependence on LLMs:** The framework heavily relies on the capabilities of LLMs. The performance and cost of the framework are therefore tied to the evolution and expense of these models. Specific to deployment, reliance on external APIs can pose risks, although the use of open-source LLMs mitigate some risk.
    *   **Evaluation Metric Bias:** The evaluation metrics, although standard, could potentially favor certain approaches (e.g., LLM-based methods). A more diverse set of metrics, including metrics focused on the quality of feedback provided, could provide a more comprehensive evaluation.
    *   **Clarity about Agent Design/Prompt Engineering:** The paper would benefit from a more detailed discussion of the specific prompts used for the expert agents and the senior judge. The prompt templates provided in the appendix are helpful, but additional insights into the design choices would be valuable.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper introduces a significantly novel approach to project duplication detection by combining the benefits of LLMs and multi-agent debate.
    *   The focus on practical feedback and the real-world validation make it a valuable contribution to the field of automated project assessment.
    *   If proven generalizable, the approach could have a substantial impact on research funding allocation and resource utilization in various domains.

**Score: 8/10**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid and innovative solution to a practical problem. The strengths of the approach – particularly the novel combination of MAD, the focus on human-centered feedback, and real-world validation – outweigh the weaknesses. The score reflects the significant contribution to the field and the potential for substantial impact. While the scope/generality and reliance on LLMs are limitations, they do not diminish the overall value of the research. A higher score would require a stronger demonstration of generalizability, more detail on agent design, and potentially more diverse evaluation metrics.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### ProxySPEX: Inference-Efficient Interpretability via Sparse Feature Interactions in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17495v1)
- **Authors**: Landon Butler, Abhineet Agarwal, Justin Singh Kang, Yigit Efe Erginbas, Bin Yu, Kannan Ramchandran
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable performance by capturing complex interactions between input features. To identify these interactions, most existing approaches require enumerating all possible combinations of features up to a given order, causing them to scale poorly with the number of inputs $n$. Recently, Kang et al. (2025) proposed SPEX, an information-theoretic approach that uses interaction sparsity to scale to $n \approx 10^3$ features. SPEX greatly improves upon prior methods but requires tens of thousands of model inferences, which can be prohibitive for large models. In this paper, we observe that LLM feature interactions are often hierarchical -- higher-order interactions are accompanied by their lower-order subsets -- which enables more efficient discovery. To exploit this hierarchy, we propose ProxySPEX, an interaction attribution algorithm that first fits gradient boosted trees to masked LLM outputs and then extracts the important interactions. Experiments across four challenging high-dimensional datasets show that ProxySPEX more faithfully reconstructs LLM outputs by 20% over marginal attribution approaches while using $10\times$ fewer inferences than SPEX. By accounting for interactions, ProxySPEX identifies features that influence model output over 20% more than those selected by marginal approaches. Further, we apply ProxySPEX to two interpretability tasks. Data attribution, where we identify interactions among CIFAR-10 training samples that influence test predictions, and mechanistic interpretability, where we uncover interactions between attention heads, both within and across layers, on a question-answering task. ProxySPEX identifies interactions that enable more aggressive pruning of heads than marginal approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PROXYSPEX, an algorithm designed to improve the inference efficiency of interpreting large language models (LLMs) by identifying sparse feature interactions. It builds upon the existing SPEX method, which scales to a larger number of features than previous interaction attribution methods but still requires a substantial number of model inferences. PROXYSPEX leverages the observation that LLM feature interactions are often hierarchical (high-order interactions include their lower-order subsets). The algorithm first fits gradient boosted trees (GBTs) to masked LLM outputs as a proxy model. It then extracts important interactions from this GBT proxy, significantly reducing the number of expensive LLM inferences needed. Experiments on high-dimensional datasets show PROXYSPEX achieves comparable or better faithfulness to SPEX with fewer inferences. The paper also demonstrates PROXYSPEX's utility in data attribution (identifying influential training samples) and mechanistic interpretability (uncovering interactions between attention heads).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in exploiting the hierarchical structure of LLM feature interactions to improve inference efficiency. Using GBTs as a proxy model to capture these hierarchical relationships is a clever approach. While proxy models aren't entirely new in interpretability, the specific application to capture hierarchical *interactions* within LLMs for *inference efficiency* appears novel. It builds directly on SPEX [4], and directly addresses one of its main limitations (the amount of model queries).
*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from addressing a critical bottleneck in LLM interpretability: the computational cost of identifying feature interactions. The reduced inference requirements of PROXYSPEX could make interaction-based interpretability more accessible for larger and more complex LLMs, a space where marginal methods begin to fail. The applications to data attribution and attention head analysis demonstrate the practical value of the approach. Further, the empirical finding that interactions have a hierarchical form is, in and of itself, significant for future research on LLM interpretability.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents thorough experiments across multiple datasets, showing improvements in faithfulness and inference efficiency compared to baselines.
    *   **Well-Defined Algorithm:** PROXYSPEX is clearly described, with details on each step of the process.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The case studies demonstrate the algorithm's utility in addressing real-world interpretability challenges.
    *   **Clear Motivation:** The problem and motivation are well-articulated.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Hierarchy:** The method's effectiveness hinges on the hierarchical structure of interactions. The paper acknowledges that performance may degrade if this assumption is violated, and more effort may need to be spent on selecting the appropriate surrogate function. While evidence is provided, it is possible that certain LLMs or tasks exhibit different interaction patterns, which could be a limitation.
    *   **Interpretability of Interactions:** The paper acknowledges the inherent difficulty in interpreting higher-order interactions compared to marginal attributions. The results could benefit from more in-depth visualizations/examples showcasing the qualitative insights that the discovered interactions provide. Visualizing a GBT can be difficult, especially as its depth increases.
    *   **Scalability to Even Larger Models:** While the paper demonstrates efficiency gains over SPEX, it would be beneficial to see how PROXYSPEX scales with the very largest LLMs (e.g., models with billions or trillions of parameters). The scalability of PROXYSPEX is partially dependent on the size of the input sequence (the "n"), as the initial GBT construction would scale with this parameter.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

PROXYSPEX makes a significant contribution by improving the inference efficiency of interaction-based LLM interpretability. The algorithm is well-motivated, clearly described, and empirically validated.  The key strength is in providing a practical method that reduces inference cost by capitalizing on hierarchical interactions, a novel observation in this space. The weaknesses lie in the algorithm's reliance on the assumed hierarchy and scalability limitations for *extreme scale LLMs*, where constructing the proxy model might still be costly. Moreover, the difficulty of interpreting interactions is only partially addressed; improvements here would bolster the argument for the overall utility of the approach. As such, while it doesn't break any theoretical ground, it provides a solid advance in a practical area of LLM research, with the potential to influence further work in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Analyzing Mitigation Strategies for Catastrophic Forgetting in End-to-End Training of Spoken Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17496v1)
- **Authors**: Chi-Yuan Hsiao, Ke-Han Lu, Kai-Wei Chang, Chih-Kai Yang, Wei-Chih Chen, Hung-yi Lee
- **Abstract**: End-to-end training of Spoken Language Models (SLMs) commonly involves adapting pre-trained text-based Large Language Models (LLMs) to the speech modality through multi-stage training on diverse tasks such as ASR, TTS and spoken question answering (SQA). Although this multi-stage continual learning equips LLMs with both speech understanding and generation capabilities, the substantial differences in task and data distributions across stages can lead to catastrophic forgetting, where previously acquired knowledge is lost. This paper investigates catastrophic forgetting and evaluates three mitigation strategies-model merging, discounting the LoRA scaling factor, and experience replay to balance knowledge retention with new learning. Results show that experience replay is the most effective, with further gains achieved by combining it with other methods. These findings provide insights for developing more robust and efficient SLM training pipelines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the problem of catastrophic forgetting in end-to-end training of Spoken Language Models (SLMs). SLMs are often created by adapting pre-trained text-based Large Language Models (LLMs) through multi-stage training involving tasks like ASR, TTS, and spoken question answering (SQA). The paper explores three mitigation strategies: model merging, discounting the LoRA scaling factor, and experience replay. The experiments indicate that experience replay is the most effective strategy, and combining it with other methods can further improve performance. The study systematically analyzes the effect of each stage on the final model performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focused analysis of catastrophic forgetting specifically within the context of end-to-end SLM training with a contemporary set of training tasks (ASR, TTS, SQA). While the mitigation strategies themselves (model merging, LoRA scaling discount, experience replay) are not novel in the broader machine learning literature, their application and comparative analysis within the context of SLMs constitutes a useful contribution. The specific nuances of speech processing and the way LLMs are adapted for speech create a distinct setting worth exploring. It is also novel that the authors analyze performance using both question answering and instruction following.

**Significance:** The significance of this work is moderate. SLMs are gaining importance, and understanding how to train them effectively while retaining previous knowledge is crucial. The empirical evaluation of different mitigation strategies provides practical guidance for SLM developers. The finding that experience replay is particularly effective is valuable, as it suggests a relatively simple yet potent technique for addressing catastrophic forgetting in this domain.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of catastrophic forgetting in SLM training.
*   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper provides a systematic evaluation of several popular mitigation strategies.
*   **Practical Insights:** The results offer practical guidance for developing SLM training pipelines.
*   **Well-Defined Experiments:** The experiments are described in sufficient detail for reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses on a specific SLM architecture (based on LLaMA) and a limited set of tasks (ASR, TTS, SQA). The generalizability of the findings to other SLM architectures or tasks may be limited.
*   **Incremental Contribution:** While the application of existing mitigation strategies to SLMs is valuable, the paper does not introduce fundamentally new techniques or theoretical insights.
*   **Dataset Dependence:** The conclusions are heavily dependent on the datasets used in the study (LibriSpeech, Magpie-Air, IFEval). Other datasets might produce different results.
*   **Limited Novelty in Techniques:** The core mitigation techniques are well-established in the general machine learning literature, making the novelty more about the application and evaluation within the SLM domain.

**Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the way SLMs are trained, particularly with respect to continual learning. The findings may encourage researchers and practitioners to incorporate experience replay into their SLM training pipelines to mitigate catastrophic forgetting. It also provides a baseline for future research on more advanced mitigation techniques tailored specifically for SLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the analysis, a score of 6 is assigned. The paper presents a clear problem statement, and systematic experiments and insights into existing methods. While the mitigation strategies themselves are not novel, their application and comparison in SLM training, which has its own specific challenges (speech processing), add a degree of novelty and practical value to the contribution, making it a reasonably useful addition to the field. The limited scope and dataset dependence prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### CReSt: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Retrieval-Augmented Generation with Complex Reasoning over Structured Documents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17503v1)
- **Authors**: Minsoo Khang, Sangjun Park, Teakgyu Hong, Dawoon Jung
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have made substantial progress in recent years, yet evaluating their capabilities in practical Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) scenarios remains challenging. In practical applications, LLMs must demonstrate complex reasoning, refuse to answer appropriately, provide precise citations, and effectively understand document layout. These capabilities are crucial for advanced task handling, uncertainty awareness, maintaining reliability, and structural understanding. While some of the prior works address these aspects individually, there is a need for a unified framework that evaluates them collectively in practical RAG scenarios. To address this, we present CReSt (A Comprehensive Benchmark for Retrieval-Augmented Generation with Complex Reasoning over Structured Documents), a benchmark designed to assess these key dimensions holistically. CReSt comprises 2,245 human-annotated examples in English and Korean, designed to capture practical RAG scenarios that require complex reasoning over structured documents. It also introduces a tailored evaluation methodology to comprehensively assess model performance in these critical areas. Our evaluation shows that even advanced LLMs struggle to perform consistently across these dimensions, underscoring key areas for improvement. We release CReSt to support further research and the development of more robust RAG systems. The dataset and code are available at: https://github.com/UpstageAI/CReSt.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CReSt (A Comprehensive Benchmark for Retrieval-Augmented Generation with Complex Reasoning over Structured Documents), a new benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in practical RAG scenarios.  CReSt focuses on complex reasoning, appropriate refusal, accurate citation, and understanding structured documents (HTML). The benchmark consists of 2,245 human-annotated examples in both English and Korean, derived from realistic source documents. The authors provide a tailored evaluation methodology to comprehensively assess model performance.  The results show that even advanced LLMs struggle across these dimensions consistently, highlighting areas for improvement. The authors release the dataset and code to encourage further research and development of more robust RAG systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies in its holistic approach to evaluating RAG systems, particularly focusing on real-world scenarios involving structured documents and the integration of refusal capabilities alongside other important dimensions.  Many existing benchmarks tend to focus on a subset of these criteria or rely on simplified document formats. The use of both English and Korean is also a valuable contribution, addressing the need for multilingual evaluations.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the growing importance of RAG in practical LLM applications. CReSt addresses a gap in the existing evaluation landscape by providing a more comprehensive and realistic assessment of LLM performance in these scenarios.  By highlighting the challenges LLMs face with complex reasoning, structured data, and refusal, the benchmark can guide future research toward developing more robust and reliable RAG systems.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  CReSt considers several critical aspects (complex reasoning, refusal, citation, structured documents) in a single benchmark.
    *   **Realistic Data:**  The dataset is constructed from realistic source documents, making it more representative of real-world RAG applications than benchmarks using curated or simplified data.
    *   **Multilingual Support:** Inclusion of both English and Korean expands the scope of evaluation and supports the development of multilingual RAG systems.
    *   **Well-Defined Evaluation Methodology:** The authors provide a clear and well-reasoned evaluation methodology, including metrics and a LLM-as-a-judge approach for answer correctness.
    *   **Thorough Experimentation:** A detailed set of experiments are provided, showcasing the strengths of the data set.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper mentions limitations related to end-to-end RAG pipeline evaluation (focusing on LLM rather than retrieval or ranking) and a limited exploration of prompt engineering strategies. These represent avenues for future work.
    *   **Complexity of Task:** As the paper highlights the level of quality of even SOTA LLMs on this task is limited, there could have been more evaluation done on more simple LLMs to evaluate the difficulty of the task and if it is perhaps too comprehensive to be easily used in general.
*   **Impact:** CReSt has the potential to become a valuable resource for the RAG research community. It can drive advancements in LLM reasoning capabilities, document understanding, and reliability, leading to more effective and trustworthy RAG systems. It pushes the field towards holistically evaluating LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

CReSt represents a significant step forward in evaluating LLMs for RAG applications.  While it acknowledges its limitations, the benchmark's comprehensive nature, realistic data, and multilingual support make it a valuable contribution. The experiments provide concrete evidence of the challenges LLMs face and highlight areas for future research.  While not a groundbreaking theoretical advance, its practical impact and potential to guide future development warrant a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### L-MTP: Leap Multi-Token Prediction Beyond Adjacent Context for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17505v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaohao Liu, Xiaobo Xia, Weixiang Zhao, Manyi Zhang, Xianzhi Yu, Xiu Su, Shuo Yang, See-Kiong Ng, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved notable progress. Despite their success, next-token prediction (NTP), the dominant method for LLM training and inference, is constrained in both contextual coverage and inference efficiency due to its inherently sequential process. To overcome these challenges, we propose leap multi-token prediction~(L-MTP), an innovative token prediction method that extends the capabilities of multi-token prediction (MTP) by introducing a leap-based mechanism. Unlike conventional MTP, which generates multiple tokens at adjacent positions, L-MTP strategically skips over intermediate tokens, predicting non-sequential ones in a single forward pass. This structured leap not only enhances the model's ability to capture long-range dependencies but also enables a decoding strategy specially optimized for non-sequential leap token generation, effectively accelerating inference. We theoretically demonstrate the benefit of L-MTP in improving inference efficiency. Experiments across diverse benchmarks validate its merit in boosting both LLM performance and inference speed. The source code will be publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Leap Multi-Token Prediction (L-MTP), a novel token prediction method for Large Language Models (LLMs).  L-MTP extends multi-token prediction (MTP) by strategically skipping intermediate tokens and predicting non-sequential tokens in a single forward pass. This "leap" mechanism aims to enhance the model's ability to capture long-range dependencies and accelerate inference. The authors theoretically demonstrate the potential efficiency gains of L-MTP and validate its performance and speed improvements through experiments on diverse benchmarks using several LLMs such as Qwen, Llama and Gemma.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of skipping tokens during training and inference to improve efficiency and capture long-range dependencies has some intuitive appeal, but the overall novelty of L-MTP is moderate. Multi-token prediction itself is not new, and while the "leap" concept is a distinct modification, it builds upon existing work.  The concept is similar to ideas of model compression or abstraction.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its potential to improve the inference speed of LLMs without significantly compromising performance.  The experimental results suggest L-MTP can offer performance improvements over standard NTP/MTP approaches, particularly on math and code tasks. The ability to accelerate inference while maintaining accuracy is valuable, making LLMs more practical for real-world applications. However, the results also indicate that the compensation for the "leap" strategy can sometimes be limited, and carefully choosing the quality of training data is beneficial. This somewhat limits the general applicability and impact of the research. The theoretical analyses are reasonably well-formulated and provide insights, even though the assumptions made may not perfectly hold in practice. The discussion about relating attenuation to the number of heads and speedup offers some useful directions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper addresses a relevant problem: improving LLM inference efficiency.
    *   The proposed method is well-motivated with both theoretical and practical considerations.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of L-MTP across diverse benchmarks and model families.
    *   The analyses show a clear relation between accuracy and acceleration.
    *   The implementation details and pseudo-code enhance reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The improvement may not be seen in every instance.
    *   Some benefits observed may originate from specifically-selected data.
    *   The leap strategy requires more careful data selection for training.
    *   The theoretical analysis relies on assumptions which are probably not satisfied strictly.
    *   The results show that the benefit is not linear for general data.

*   **Impact:** The work has the potential to influence the development of more efficient LLM training and inference techniques. If widely adopted, it could contribute to more accessible and sustainable AI systems.  However, further research is needed to fully understand the limitations and optimize the method for different model architectures and tasks. The research seems promising but more analysis is required to demonstrate its broad applicability.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** While the paper presents a solid contribution to the field, the overall novelty and the limited improvement (in some instances) justify a slightly lower score. The idea of L-MTP builds upon existing work and offers incremental improvements. The limitations need to be studied in more detail to completely determine how and when L-MTP can improve existing LLMs. More comprehensive real-world results would strongly improve the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### On the Design of KL-Regularized Policy Gradient Algorithms for LLM Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17508v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Zhang, Yifeng Liu, Huizhuo Yuan, Yang Yuan, Quanquan Gu, Andrew C Yao
- **Abstract**: Policy gradient algorithms have been successfully applied to enhance the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Despite the widespread use of Kullback-Leibler (KL) regularization in policy gradient algorithms to stabilize training, the systematic exploration of how different KL divergence formulations can be estimated and integrated into surrogate loss functions for online reinforcement learning (RL) presents a nuanced and systematically explorable design space. In this paper, we propose regularized policy gradient (RPG), a systematic framework for deriving and analyzing KL-regularized policy gradient methods in the online RL setting. We derive policy gradients and corresponding surrogate loss functions for objectives regularized by both forward and reverse KL divergences, considering both normalized and unnormalized policy distributions. Furthermore, we present derivations for fully differentiable loss functions as well as REINFORCE-style gradient estimators, accommodating diverse algorithmic needs. We conduct extensive experiments on RL for LLM reasoning using these methods, showing improved or competitive results in terms of training stability and performance compared to strong baselines such as GRPO, REINFORCE++, and DAPO. The code is available at https://github.com/complex-reasoning/RPG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "On the Design of KL-Regularized Policy Gradient Algorithms for LLM Reasoning" introduces Regularized Policy Gradient (RPG), a framework for deriving and analyzing KL-regularized policy gradient methods in online reinforcement learning (RL) for large language models (LLMs). It systematically derives policy gradients and corresponding surrogate loss functions for objectives regularized by forward and reverse KL divergences, considering both normalized and unnormalized policy distributions. It also presents derivations for both fully differentiable loss functions and REINFORCE-style gradient estimators. The authors conduct experiments on RL for LLM reasoning, showing improved or competitive results compared to baselines like GRPO, REINFORCE++, and DAPO.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides a systematic treatment of KL regularization within policy gradient methods for LLMs, which is a practical contribution. The systematic derivation of both fully differentiable and REINFORCE-style estimators for forward and reverse KL divergences, including both normalized and unnormalized variants, is a valuable contribution. The detailed connection between the k3 estimator and unnormalized KL, as well as the theoretical inconsistency identified in the GRPO objective, are insightful. However, each element may not be *entirely* novel as some aspects have been touched upon in prior work, but their integration within a single framework is a strength.

*   **Significance:** LLMs are increasingly used for reasoning tasks, and RL is a promising technique for improving their reasoning capabilities. KL regularization is a key ingredient for stabilizing policy gradient methods. The paper provides a systematic framework that can help researchers and practitioners design and analyze new KL-regularized policy gradient algorithms. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods on LLM reasoning tasks, showing improved or competitive performance against existing baselines.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Framework:** The RPG framework provides a structured approach to KL regularization in policy gradient methods, facilitating analysis and algorithm design.
    *   **Comprehensive Derivations:** The detailed derivations of policy gradients, surrogate loss functions, and gradient estimators are valuable for both theoretical understanding and practical implementation.
    *   **Practical Insights:** The theoretical inconsistency identified in GRPO and the analysis of REINFORCE++ provide practical insights for improving existing algorithms.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods on LLM reasoning tasks.
    *   Clear exposition and relatively accessible math.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the RPG framework provides a valuable contribution, some of its components (e.g., forward/reverse KL, normalized/unnormalized forms, REINFORCE-style estimators) have been explored in previous research.  The "novelty" primarily lies in the systematic unification and comprehensive treatment.
    *   **Experimental Scope:** The experimental evaluation is focused on mathematical reasoning tasks. It would be helpful to evaluate the proposed methods on other LLM reasoning tasks, such as commonsense reasoning, logical reasoning, or knowledge-based reasoning. More extensive comparison to PPO might also have been useful.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the Appendix provides details, a deeper analysis of the impact of different hyperparameters (e.g., KL coefficient) on performance would be helpful.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The RPG framework could influence the development of new policy gradient algorithms for LLM reasoning. The practical insights from the theoretical analysis could improve the performance of existing algorithms.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper offers a solid contribution with practical value for researchers and practitioners working on LLM reasoning. While it builds on existing techniques, the systematic treatment and comprehensive derivations are valuable. The identification of inconsistencies in existing methods is also a strength. The experimental results are promising, but the scope could be broader. As such, the novelty is of practical value.

**Score: 7**

The paper is not groundbreaking, but represents a significant and well-executed engineering contribution that consolidates various ideas and provides tangible benefits. This level demonstrates solid advancement but not disruptive impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models Do Multi-Label Classification Differently
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17510v1)
- **Authors**: Marcus Ma, Georgios Chochlakis, Niyantha Maruthu Pandiyan, Jesse Thomason, Shrikanth Narayanan
- **Abstract**: Multi-label classification is prevalent in real-world settings, but the behavior of Large Language Models (LLMs) in this setting is understudied. We investigate how autoregressive LLMs perform multi-label classification, with a focus on subjective tasks, by analyzing the output distributions of the models in each generation step. We find that their predictive behavior reflects the multiple steps in the underlying language modeling required to generate all relevant labels as they tend to suppress all but one label at each step. We further observe that as model scale increases, their token distributions exhibit lower entropy, yet the internal ranking of the labels improves. Finetuning methods such as supervised finetuning and reinforcement learning amplify this phenomenon. To further study this issue, we introduce the task of distribution alignment for multi-label settings: aligning LLM-derived label distributions with empirical distributions estimated from annotator responses in subjective tasks. We propose both zero-shot and supervised methods which improve both alignment and predictive performance over existing approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how Large Language Models (LLMs) perform multi-label classification, specifically on subjective tasks.  The authors find that LLMs tend to favor a single label at each generation step, leading to "spiky" probability distributions rather than holistic multi-label reasoning. They frame the problem as a "distribution alignment" task, aiming to align LLM-derived label distributions with human-annotation-derived empirical distributions. They propose and evaluate zero-shot and supervised approaches for this alignment, demonstrating improvements over standard baselines. The key finding is that LLMs treat multi-label classification as a sequence of single-label decisions influenced by the language modeling objective.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper provides a valuable empirical analysis of LLM behavior in the multi-label setting, which is a relatively under-explored area compared to single-label tasks.  The formal analysis of the "spikiness" of LLM distributions and the investigation of how language modeling biases interfere with classification are novel and insightful.  Framing the problem as distribution alignment is also a useful contribution.  However, the proposed alignment methods themselves, while showing improvements, build on existing techniques and might not be considered groundbreaking in isolation. A particularly appealing angle is the exploration of calibrating against annotator degrees of belief rather than a strict 1-hot encoding for labels.
* **Significance:** Multi-label classification is prevalent in real-world applications, particularly in areas like sentiment analysis, emotion recognition, and topic modeling.  Understanding how LLMs handle these tasks and developing methods to improve their performance is important. The paper's findings highlight the limitations of directly applying pre-trained LLMs to multi-label problems and motivate the need for specialized techniques.  The emphasis on subjective tasks and the attempt to capture the nuances of human annotation are particularly relevant.  This work can inspire further research into better multi-label classification approaches with LLMs by providing strong empirical validation.
* **Strengths:**
    * Thorough empirical analysis:  The paper presents a comprehensive analysis of LLM output distributions across various datasets and model sizes.
    * Clear problem formulation:  The framing of multi-label classification as a distribution alignment task is well-defined and facilitates the development of targeted solutions.
    * Useful insights: The paper identifies key challenges in multi-label classification with LLMs, such as the "spikiness" of distributions and the influence of language modeling biases.
    * The paper investigates various datasets and model families.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited scope of solutions:  The proposed distribution alignment methods, while effective, are not particularly innovative from a methodological perspective.
    * Lack of theoretical analysis: The paper provides empirical evidence but lacks a deeper theoretical explanation for the observed phenomena.  A formal model of how language modeling biases affect multi-label classification would strengthen the work.
    * Focus on one model family: Primarily analyzes Llama models; extending analysis to other architectures could reveal generalizable insights or model-specific behaviors.
    * The experimental setup for distribution alignment scores is a bit obscure, particularly related to the uniform label distribution and it's implications for NLL.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    * Multi-label classification with LLMs: It can inspire new approaches that address the identified challenges, such as developing methods to encourage holistic multi-label reasoning and reduce the impact of language modeling biases.
    * Subjective language understanding:  It highlights the importance of capturing the nuances of human annotation and developing models that can represent degrees of belief.
    * LLM calibration: It provides insights into the calibration of LLMs in a multi-label setting, which can inform the development of better calibration techniques.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the understanding of LLMs in the context of multi-label classification. The empirical analysis and the framing of the problem as distribution alignment are valuable.  While the proposed solutions are incremental, the paper's insights and findings are significant and can motivate further research. However, the relatively narrow scope of proposed solutions prevents a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Probe by Gaming: A Game-based Benchmark for Assessing Conceptual Knowledge in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17512v1)
- **Authors**: Shuhang Xu, Weijian Deng, Yixuan Zhou, Fangwei Zhong
- **Abstract**: Concepts represent generalized abstractions that enable humans to categorize and reason efficiently, yet it is unclear to what extent Large Language Models (LLMs) comprehend these semantic relationships. Existing benchmarks typically focus on factual recall and isolated tasks, failing to evaluate the ability of LLMs to understand conceptual boundaries. To address this gap, we introduce CK-Arena, a multi-agent interaction game built upon the Undercover game, designed to evaluate the capacity of LLMs to reason with concepts in interactive settings. CK-Arena challenges models to describe, differentiate, and infer conceptual boundaries based on partial information, encouraging models to explore commonalities and distinctions between closely related concepts. By simulating real-world interaction, CK-Arena provides a scalable and realistic benchmark for assessing conceptual reasoning in dynamic environments. Experimental results show that LLMs' understanding of conceptual knowledge varies significantly across different categories and is not strictly aligned with parameter size or general model capabilities. The data and code are available at the project homepage: https://ck-arena.site.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "Probe by Gaming: A Game-based Benchmark for Assessing Conceptual Knowledge in LLMs":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces CK-Arena, a novel game-based benchmark designed to assess the conceptual knowledge and reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). CK-Arena utilizes a multi-agent interaction game inspired by the "Undercover" game, where LLMs take on the roles of players (civilians or undercover agents) and judges. The game challenges models to describe, differentiate, and infer conceptual boundaries based on partial information and semantic relationships.  Unlike traditional benchmarks that focus on factual recall, CK-Arena emphasizes conceptual understanding within interactive, dynamic environments. The paper evaluates several popular LLMs using CK-Arena, revealing varying levels of conceptual understanding across different categories and highlighting that parameter size does not guarantee superior performance in this domain. The authors provide a detailed description of the benchmark's design, implementation, and evaluation metrics, along with comprehensive results and analyses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to evaluating conceptual knowledge in LLMs by moving beyond static question-answering tasks to an interactive, game-based setting. CK-Arena effectively mimics real-world interaction scenarios, demanding more sophisticated reasoning skills than simple factual recall. The use of the "Undercover" game as a framework is clever, as it naturally forces models to reason about shared attributes and distinctive features of related concepts. This aspect is a significant strength.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in current LLM evaluation methods.  The ability to understand and utilize conceptual knowledge is crucial for LLMs to perform complex reasoning tasks and interact effectively in dynamic environments. By introducing CK-Arena, the authors offer a valuable tool for assessing this aspect of LLM intelligence, with significant implications for the development of more robust and human-like AI systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Evaluation Framework:** The game-based approach provides a more realistic and challenging assessment of conceptual knowledge compared to traditional benchmarks.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Metrics:**  The paper defines clear and informative metrics for evaluating both statement-level and player-level performance, offering a nuanced understanding of LLM capabilities.
    *   **Detailed Implementation:** The paper provides comprehensive details about the design, implementation, and data preparation for CK-Arena, including the prompt design for various agents and the criteria for human review, enhancing reproducibility.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation of LLMs:** The evaluation of several popular LLMs provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses in conceptual reasoning.
    *   **Interesting Findings:** The observation that parameter size doesn't guarantee superior performance is significant and challenges common assumptions about model scaling.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Concept Types:** The current implementation of CK-Arena is primarily effective for evaluating noun-based concepts, as mentioned in the Limitations section.  Extending the framework to evaluate verbs, abstract concepts, or more complex relationships would enhance its versatility. While acknowledged, this is a limitation in scope.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The automated evaluation process relies on LLMs as judges, which raises potential concerns about bias or inconsistencies in scoring.  The human review process mitigates this, but a more objective or alternative evaluation method would be desirable. The use of strong models for judging is likely important here.
    *   **English Language Bias:** The benchmark is currently limited to the English language, which may introduce language-specific biases and limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Computational Resources:** The resource intensity of the multi-agent interaction design is another limitation, potentially restricting accessibility for researchers with limited computing power.
    *   **Potential for Exploitation**: The prompts, while detailed, could still be potentially exploitable, where LLMs might simply 'play the game' rather than demonstrate true conceptual understanding. The human evaluation attempts to mediate this, but prompt engineering and 'gaming' can be difficult to disentangle.

*   **Potential Influence on the Field:** CK-Arena has the potential to become a valuable resource for the LLM research community, enabling more targeted evaluation and development of models with enhanced conceptual reasoning abilities. It could also influence the design of future benchmarks and evaluation methods in the field.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the novel approach to LLM evaluation, the significance of the research question, the comprehensive experimental design and analysis, and its weaknesses the paper makes a strong contribution to the field. The game-based setup is a significant leap forward, and the limitations are recognized, offering clear directions for future work.
**Score: 8**
