### Spacetime Geometry of Denoising in Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17517v1)
- **Authors**: Rafał Karczewski, Markus Heinonen, Alison Pouplin, Søren Hauberg, Vikas Garg
- **Abstract**: We present a novel perspective on diffusion models using the framework of information geometry. We show that the set of noisy samples, taken across all noise levels simultaneously, forms a statistical manifold -- a family of denoising probability distributions. Interpreting the noise level as a temporal parameter, we refer to this manifold as spacetime. This manifold naturally carries a Fisher-Rao metric, which defines geodesics -- shortest paths between noisy points. Notably, this family of distributions is exponential, enabling efficient geodesic computation even in high-dimensional settings without retraining or fine-tuning. We demonstrate the practical value of this geometric viewpoint in transition path sampling, where spacetime geodesics define smooth sequences of Boltzmann distributions, enabling the generation of continuous trajectories between low-energy metastable states. Code is available at: https://github.com/Aalto-QuML/diffusion-spacetime-geometry.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Spacetime Geometry of Denoising in Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel perspective on diffusion models by framing the denoising process as a statistical manifold equipped with a Fisher-Rao metric. This manifold, dubbed "spacetime," represents the set of noisy samples across all noise levels. The authors demonstrate that the denoising distributions form an exponential family, which allows for efficient geodesic computation (shortest paths between noisy points) *without retraining or fine-tuning*.  The geometric viewpoint is then applied to transition path sampling, enabling the generation of smooth trajectories between low-energy metastable states.  Code is provided for reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of treating the denoising process as a spacetime manifold with a Fisher-Rao metric is genuinely novel. Previous works have explored latent space geometry using pullback metrics from the decoder or investigated the relationship between latent variables. However, this paper directly addresses the *entire denoising distribution* and uses information geometry in a principled way. The realization that denoising distributions in diffusion models fall within the exponential family is a significant and surprising finding. Leveraging this structure to efficiently compute geodesics adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from several aspects:
    *   **Theoretical Insight:** The framework provides a new way to *understand* the denoising process in diffusion models, moving beyond a black-box view of the denoiser. The connection to information geometry provides a rich set of tools for analysis.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The efficient geodesic computation, made possible by the exponential family structure, is a major advantage.  It avoids the computational burden of traditional pullback methods.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The transition path sampling application is a compelling demonstration of the framework's utility. Generating smooth transitions between states has direct applications in molecular dynamics and potentially other fields.
    *   **Potential for Future Work:** The framework opens up avenues for future research, such as improved sampling strategies and better understanding of information flow in diffusion models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and well-structured presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and explains the concepts clearly. The background section is helpful for understanding the context.
    *   **Solid theoretical foundation:** The paper builds upon established concepts from information geometry and diffusion models.
    *   **Practical validation:** The experimental results, while not exhaustive, provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the approach. The code release enhances reproducibility and allows others to build upon the work.
    *   **The connection to an exponential family is highly valuable and allows for tractable estimation of geodesics.**

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Experimental Scope:**  While the transition path sampling example is promising, more extensive experimentation is needed to fully demonstrate the advantages of the proposed method across a wider range of applications and datasets.  The differences between geodesic and PF-ODE sampling trajectories in ImageNet are described as "minor"; a more detailed analysis of perceptual differences would be valuable.
    *   **Practical challenges near t ≈ 0 :** They solved from only until t = tmin = 0.1 (as opposed to t = 0), because for t ≈ 0, the denoising distributions p(xoxt) become closer to Dirac delta distributions δx0, which makes the energies very large. This limitation needs to be explicitly stated.
    *   **Approximations and Assumptions:** The analysis relies on approximations and assumptions (e.g., using a denoiser network to approximate the denoising mean). The impact of these approximations on the results should be discussed more thoroughly.
    *   **Limited Discussion on limitations with nearly clean data** The extreme scale in optimizing nearly clean data due to the KL divergence is a limitation of their results.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence research in diffusion models by providing a new geometric perspective and efficient tools for analysis and application. It could lead to improved sampling strategies, better understanding of information flow, and new applications in areas like molecular dynamics and generative modeling.
    *This work demonstrates that denoising distributions form an exponential family!*

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper presents a genuinely novel and significant contribution to the field of diffusion models. The idea of treating the denoising process as a statistical manifold equipped with the Fisher-Rao metric and the discovery of the exponential family structure are both highly valuable. The efficient geodesic computation and the application to transition path sampling are compelling demonstrations of the framework's utility. While the experimental scope could be broader and some limitations exist, the paper's theoretical insights and potential impact warrant a high score. The score reflects a strong contribution with the potential to significantly influence future research in diffusion models and related areas.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Chain-of-Lure: A Synthetic Narrative-Driven Approach to Compromise Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17519v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhan Chang, Tianqing Zhu, Yu Zhao, Shuangyong Song, Ping Xiong, Wanlei Zhou, Yongxiang Li
- **Abstract**: In the era of rapid generative AI development, interactions between humans and large language models face significant misusing risks. Previous research has primarily focused on black-box scenarios using human-guided prompts and white-box scenarios leveraging gradient-based LLM generation methods, neglecting the possibility that LLMs can act not only as victim models, but also as attacker models to harm other models. We proposes a novel jailbreaking method inspired by the Chain-of-Thought mechanism, where the attacker model uses mission transfer to conceal harmful user intent in dialogue and generates chained narrative lures to stimulate the reasoning capabilities of victim models, leading to successful jailbreaking. To enhance the attack success rate, we introduce a helper model that performs random narrative optimization on the narrative lures during multi-turn dialogues while ensuring alignment with the original intent, enabling the optimized lures to bypass the safety barriers of victim models effectively. Our experiments reveal that models with weaker safety mechanisms exhibit stronger attack capabilities, demonstrating that models can not only be exploited, but also help harm others. By incorporating toxicity scores, we employ third-party models to evaluate the harmfulness of victim models' responses to jailbreaking attempts. The study shows that using refusal keywords as an evaluation metric for attack success rates is significantly flawed because it does not assess whether the responses guide harmful questions, while toxicity scores measure the harm of generated content with more precision and its alignment with harmful questions. Our approach demonstrates outstanding performance, uncovering latent vulnerabilities in LLMs and providing data-driven feedback to optimize LLM safety mechanisms. We also discuss two defensive strategies to offer guidance on improving defense mechanisms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Chain-of-Lure: A Synthetic Narrative-Driven Approach to Compromise Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel jailbreaking attack called "Chain-of-Lure" against Large Language Models (LLMs). This method leverages an attacker LLM to generate a chain of narrative lures, carefully crafted scenarios embedding sensitive questions.  The goal is to stimulate the reasoning capabilities of victim LLMs, leading them to bypass safety barriers and reveal restricted content. To improve the attack success rate, the authors introduce a "helper model" that optimizes the narrative lures while maintaining alignment with the original harmful intent.  The paper shows that models with weaker safety mechanisms are more susceptible to this attack, highlighting the risk of LLMs being not just victims, but also attackers. It also introduces a new metric, the Toxicity Score (TS), for evaluating the harmfulness of LLM responses after jailbreaking, arguing that it's a more accurate metric than simply detecting refusal keywords.  The experiments demonstrate high attack success rates on both open-source and closed-source models in black-box API scenarios. The paper also discusses two potential defensive strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach by framing jailbreaking as a narrative generation and optimization problem. The "Chain-of-Lure" concept, using an LLM to guide another LLM toward unsafe outputs through carefully constructed scenarios, is a distinct contribution.  The idea of "mission transfer," embedding malicious intent in a seemingly harmless story, is also quite clever. While Chain-of-Thought (CoT) has been used previously to improve LLM reasoning *accuracy*, the paper's use of CoT-inspired techniques for jailbreaking is a creative twist. The explicit use of a "helper model" to optimize the narrative iteratively adds another layer of sophistication.

* **Significance:** The paper highlights a significant and growing security concern: LLMs can be exploited to attack other LLMs.  This "attacker model" scenario expands the threat landscape beyond simply preventing direct misuse of a single LLM. The demonstration of near-perfect attack success rates on various models, both open-source and closed-source, underscores the severity of the vulnerability. The introduction of the Toxicity Score as a more nuanced evaluation metric is valuable. The paper's findings have implications for developers of LLMs, emphasizing the need for more robust safety mechanisms that consider not only direct prompt attacks but also narrative-driven exploits.  Furthermore, the study’s discussion of defensive strategies provides a valuable starting point for future research on mitigating this class of attack. The fact that the method works in a black-box setting significantly increases its relevance to real-world scenarios.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Novelty:** The "Chain-of-Lure" concept is indeed a new approach to jailbreaking.
    * **Practicality:** Black-box setting, fewer computational demands, and clear defensive strategies.
    * **Evaluation Metric:** The introduction of Toxicity Score is a meaningful improvement.
    * **Thorough Experimentation:** Extensive experiments and benchmark against other state-of-the-art attack methods.
    * **Well-Written:** The paper is well-structured and generally clear.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on LLM Abilities:** The attack's success is heavily dependent on the text generation and narrative abilities of the attacker LLM. This might limit its effectiveness if the attacker LLM is weak.
    * **Defensive Strategies:** While two defensive strategies are mentioned, a deeper empirical validation of the effectiveness of these strategies would be beneficial.
    * **Ethical Considerations:** While acknowledging the potential for misuse is important, the paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of ethical considerations related to developing and publishing such an attack technique. What safeguards were put in place to prevent immediate misuse?

* **Potential Influence:**  This paper has the potential to influence the direction of LLM security research, motivating new defenses that address narrative-based attacks. It might also prompt a re-evaluation of current evaluation metrics for jailbreaking attacks, pushing for more semantically-aware measures like Toxicity Score.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant jailbreaking attack on LLMs, with strong empirical results. The new Toxicity Score metric is a meaningful contribution. While the defensive strategies require more validation and there's a lingering ethical concern (mitigated by the paper's acknowledgment), the overall impact of the work on the field is substantial. Therefore, the paper deserves a high score.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Optimizing Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Electrical Engineering: A Case Study on ABB Circuit Breakers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17520v1)
- **Authors**: Salahuddin Alawadhi, Noorhan Abbas
- **Abstract**: Integrating Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) with Large Language Models (LLMs) has shown the potential to provide precise, contextually relevant responses in knowledge intensive domains. This study investigates the ap-plication of RAG for ABB circuit breakers, focusing on accuracy, reliability, and contextual relevance in high-stakes engineering environments. By leveraging tailored datasets, advanced embedding models, and optimized chunking strategies, the research addresses challenges in data retrieval and contextual alignment unique to engineering documentation. Key contributions include the development of a domain-specific dataset for ABB circuit breakers and the evaluation of three RAG pipelines: OpenAI GPT4o, Cohere, and Anthropic Claude. Advanced chunking methods, such as paragraph-based and title-aware segmentation, are assessed for their impact on retrieval accuracy and response generation. Results demonstrate that while certain configurations achieve high precision and relevancy, limitations persist in ensuring factual faithfulness and completeness, critical in engineering contexts. This work underscores the need for iterative improvements in RAG systems to meet the stringent demands of electrical engineering tasks, including design, troubleshooting, and operational decision-making. The findings in this paper help advance research of AI in highly technical domains such as electrical engineering.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

This paper investigates the application of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems to the domain of electrical engineering, specifically using ABB circuit breaker documentation as a case study. The authors compare three RAG pipelines (OpenAI GPT-4o, Cohere, and Anthropic Claude) using various chunking strategies and RAGAS metrics to assess their performance in accuracy, reliability, and contextual relevance. The results highlight that while certain configurations achieve high precision and relevancy, ensuring factual faithfulness and completeness remains a challenge. The paper concludes that there is a need for iterative improvements in RAG systems to meet the stringent demands of electrical engineering tasks, and suggests future work in integrating knowledge graphs and multimodal data to further improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper explores the application of RAG in a niche domain (electrical engineering, specifically ABB circuit breakers), which adds a degree of novelty. While RAG systems themselves are not new, their systematic evaluation within such a specialized and safety-critical field is valuable. The comparative analysis of different LLMs (GPT-4o, Cohere, Claude) coupled with different chunking strategies tailored for engineering documentation contributes to a deeper understanding of their effectiveness in this specific context.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its identification of limitations in applying generic RAG systems to high-stakes engineering environments. The authors empirically demonstrate that even with optimized configurations, factual faithfulness and completeness remain critical concerns. This insight is crucial for researchers and practitioners looking to deploy LLMs in similar domains where accuracy is paramount. Furthermore, the suggested future directions (knowledge graph integration, multimodal data) point towards promising avenues for improving the reliability and robustness of RAG systems in these contexts.
However, the paper has some limitations that affect its overall significance. The case study focuses only on a single type of electrical equipment, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other engineering domains. While the evaluation metrics are standard (RAGAS), the lack of human evaluation and the relatively small dataset may weaken the strength of the conclusions. The comparison of LLMs is useful but fairly high level, and lacks an in-depth exploration of why each model performs differently.
The suggestion of integrating knowledge graphs and multimodal data, while promising, is relatively high-level and lacks specific implementation details. Furthermore, related work on similar topics could be more comprehensive.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper earns a score of 6/10. While the application of RAG to electrical engineering and the comparative analysis of LLMs are valuable contributions, the limitations mentioned above detract from its overall impact. The study's focus on a single type of electrical equipment (ABB circuit breakers) and the lack of deeper insight to why each performed the way it did hinders broad adoption. The suggestions for future work also need to be more concrete to establish true novelty.
The paper successfully highlights the challenges and opportunities for using RAG in a safety-critical domain, but further research is needed to fully realize its potential.

### Co-Reinforcement Learning for Unified Multimodal Understanding and Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17534v1)
- **Authors**: Jingjing Jiang, Chongjie Si, Jun Luo, Hanwang Zhang, Chao Ma
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a pioneering exploration of reinforcement learning (RL) via group relative policy optimization for unified multimodal large language models (ULMs), aimed at simultaneously reinforcing generation and understanding capabilities. Through systematic pilot studies, we uncover the significant potential of ULMs to enable the synergistic co-evolution of dual capabilities within a shared policy optimization framework. Building on this insight, we introduce \textbf{CoRL}, a co-reinforcement learning framework comprising a unified RL stage for joint optimization and a refined RL stage for task-specific enhancement. With the proposed CoRL, our resulting model, \textbf{ULM-R1}, achieves average improvements of \textbf{7%} on three text-to-image generation datasets and \textbf{23%} on nine multimodal understanding benchmarks. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of CoRL and highlight the substantial benefit of reinforcement learning in facilitating cross-task synergy and optimization for ULMs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance assessment and a score.

**Summary**

The paper "Co-Reinforcement Learning for Unified Multimodal Understanding and Generation" introduces CoRL, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to enhance both understanding and generation capabilities of Unified Multimodal Large Language Models (ULMs). CoRL employs a two-stage approach: first, a unified RL stage optimizing both capabilities jointly using a Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm and custom rewards (bidirectional cycle consistency and text-image matching), followed by a refined RL stage focusing on task-specific enhancement. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of CoRL by applying it to a baseline ULM (Janus-Pro), resulting in ULM-R1. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks show significant improvements in both text-to-image generation and multimodal understanding, especially in complex reasoning tasks. The paper highlights the potential of RL for cross-task synergy and optimization in ULMs.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *explicit* focus on *co-optimizing* both understanding and generation in ULMs using RL, particularly with carefully designed rewards to encourage semantic consistency in generated images and format adherence for understanding. While individual components like GRPO and text-image matching rewards exist, the holistic integration within a two-stage co-reinforcement learning framework, specifically tailored for ULMs' dual capabilities, is a relatively new contribution. The systematic pilot studies analyzing different RL paradigms (separate, cycle, unified) also add to the novelty. The application of GRPO to visual *generation* in ULM is itself valuable considering the existing use is mainly in understanding in single-modal tasks.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several factors:

    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** ULMs are increasingly relevant, and improving their capabilities without relying solely on massive supervised datasets is a key challenge. RL provides a promising path.
    *   **Data Efficiency:** RL, especially when combined with GRPO, offers a more data-efficient approach than supervised fine-tuning.
    *   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate significant performance gains on multiple benchmarks, suggesting that the CoRL framework is practically effective.
    *   **Insights into Cross-Task Synergy:** The pilot studies and the overall framework provide insights into how understanding and generation can mutually benefit from joint optimization, which is valuable for future research.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors include a detailed appendix on training data and hyperparameters for reproducibility, which enhances the paper's value to the community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation
    *   Novel CoRL framework design
    *   Systematic experiments and comprehensive evaluation
    *   Strong empirical results
    *   Clear writing and good organization
    *   Addresses cross-task synergy and data efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Rule-Based Rewards:** The verifiable rewards, while effective, are still rule-based. This may limit generalizability and could be seen as a form of engineering that's specific to the evaluated tasks. (Although that's the explicit target of the work, and a point made in the intro)
    *   **Complexity:** The two-stage framework is more complex than a simple end-to-end RL approach. However, they argue this is justified empirically.
    *   **Baseline Dependence:** The improvements are relative to a specific baseline (Janus-Pro). The absolute performance compared to other state-of-the-art ULMs, or completely different architectures, needs to be considered.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Reward Function Design:** The text-image matching reward is valuable, but there could be more exploration of different reward functions specifically geared towards reinforcing the synergistic effect between the two tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on RL for ULMs by:

    *   Providing a concrete framework for co-optimizing understanding and generation.
    *   Inspiring the development of new reward functions tailored for ULMs.
    *   Encouraging further investigation into the benefits of cross-task synergy.
    *   Serving as a benchmark for evaluating future RL-based ULM approaches.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed exploration of RL for ULMs. The CoRL framework, systematic experiments, and strong empirical results contribute meaningfully to the field. While the reliance on rule-based rewards and the framework complexity are minor limitations, the overall novelty and significance of the work justify a score of 7.5. The paper is likely to inspire and guide future research in this area, providing a practical and effective approach to improve the dual capabilities of ULMs. It represents a solid step forward in applying reinforcement learning to the increasingly important space of multimodal large language models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multimodal Conversation Structure Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17536v1)
- **Authors**: Kent K. Chang, Mackenzie Hanh Cramer, Anna Ho, Ti Ti Nguyen, Yilin Yuan, David Bamman
- **Abstract**: Conversations are usually structured by roles -- who is speaking, who's being addressed, and who's listening -- and unfold in threads that break with changes in speaker floor or topical focus. While large language models (LLMs) have shown incredible capabilities in dialogue and reasoning, their ability to understand fine-grained conversational structure, especially in multi-modal, multi-party settings, remains underexplored. To address this gap, we introduce a suite of tasks focused on conversational role attribution (speaker, addressees, side-participants) and conversation threading (utterance linking and clustering), drawing on conversation analysis and sociolinguistics. To support those tasks, we present a human annotated dataset of 4,398 annotations for speakers and reply-to relationship, 5,755 addressees, and 3,142 side-participants. We evaluate popular audio-visual LLMs and vision-language models on our dataset, and our experimental results suggest that multimodal conversational structure understanding remains challenging. The most performant audio-visual LLM outperforms all vision-language models across all metrics, especially in speaker and addressee recognition. However, its performance drops significantly when conversation participants are anonymized. The number of conversation participants in a clip is the strongest negative predictor of role-attribution performance, while acoustic clarity (measured by pitch and spectral centroid) and detected face coverage yield positive associations. We hope this work lays the groundwork for future evaluation and development of multimodal LLMs that can reason more effectively about conversation structure.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Multimodal Conversation Structure Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the underexplored area of fine-grained conversation structure understanding in multimodal, multi-party settings by language models (LLMs). It introduces a suite of tasks focused on conversational role attribution (speaker, addressees, side-participants) and conversation threading (utterance linking and clustering).  The authors create a human-annotated dataset (TV-MMPC) based on TVQA, comprising annotations for speakers, reply-to relationships, addressees, and side-participants. The paper evaluates popular audio-visual LLMs and vision-language models on this dataset, revealing that understanding multimodal conversational structure remains a challenge. While Gemini 2.0 Flash showed the best performance, its performance degrades when participants are anonymized, and its success correlates with visual and acoustic clarity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Problem Framing:** The paper tackles a crucial, yet often overlooked, aspect of multimodal understanding. Conversational structure is essential for human-like dialogue systems, and the paper's focus on this area is timely and relevant.
    *   **Well-Defined Tasks and Metrics:** The authors develop a clear set of tasks (role attribution, threading) and adapt existing metrics to evaluate performance effectively. The use of F1 score and normalized variation of information ensures a rigorous evaluation.
    *   **Dataset Creation:** The creation of the TV-MMPC dataset addresses the lack of suitable resources for evaluating this specific problem. The human annotation provides a high-quality benchmark for future research.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments cover a range of popular audio-visual and vision-language models. The analysis of factors such as anonymization, participant count, and modality contributions adds depth to the evaluation.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The finding that Gemini's performance degrades upon anonymization suggests the models' reliance on identity recognition and potential memorization, which is a vital insight. Further, its correlation with visual and acoustic quality guides future improvements in cross-modal dialogue understanding.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset limitations:** The dataset is limited by its source (TVQA), which may introduce biases due to the scripted nature of TV dialogues. The limited number of shows (50 clips, sampled from only four shows) represented may restrict the generality of the findings, and the dataset is restricted to English language and American culture.
    *   **Limited Task Simplification:** The conversion of TVQA for the conversational task required re-transcribing and inferring speaker labels. Speaker identification from face-tracking tools and the annotation stage might contain minor errors.
    *   **Zero-Shot Experiments:**  The experiments primarily focus on zero-shot performance which might not be fully representative of the capabilities of the LLMs with further tuning, and they do not explore or analyze how those datasets overlap with the training of these LLMs.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's primary novelty lies in its task framing and dataset creation for multimodal conversation structure understanding. While prior work exists on multi-party conversation understanding and video understanding, the integration of these domains with a focus on role attribution and threading in multimodal settings is a significant contribution. The rigorous evaluation of state-of-the-art models on this new benchmark is valuable for the community.
    *   The paper's significance comes from its ability to define a novel application area for multimodal LLMs and by giving practical insights to improve their role on cross-modal dialogue understanding.
    *   The analysis of factors influencing performance helps to guide future research towards addressing limitations of models in learning and understanding conversation structure.
*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The TV-MMPC dataset will likely become a standard benchmark for evaluating models on multimodal conversation structure understanding.
    *   The task definitions and metrics can inspire further research in this area, leading to the development of more sophisticated models.
    *   The findings on anonymization and modality contributions can inform the design of more robust and generalizable multimodal LLMs.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**
This paper offers a solid contribution by identifying a critical gap in multimodal LLMs' capabilities and by creating a valuable resource for future work in the field. Its originality is found in its problem framing and the integration of multiple modalities in its evaluations. While the limitations related to the dataset and the limited task simplification prevent a higher score, the paper's insightful analysis and comprehensive experiments make it a significant contribution to the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### How Knowledge Popularity Influences and Enhances LLM Knowledge Boundary Perception
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17537v1)
- **Authors**: Shiyu Ni, Keping Bi, Jiafeng Guo, Xueqi Cheng
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often fail to recognize their knowledge boundaries, producing confident yet incorrect answers. In this paper, we investigate how knowledge popularity affects LLMs' ability to perceive their knowledge boundaries. Focusing on entity-centric factual question answering (QA), we quantify knowledge popularity from three perspectives: the popularity of entities in the question, the popularity of entities in the answer, and relation popularity, defined as their co-occurrence frequency. Experiments on three representative datasets containing knowledge with varying popularity show that LLMs exhibit better QA performance, higher confidence, and more accurate perception on more popular knowledge, with relation popularity having the strongest correlation. Cause knowledge popularity shows strong correlation with LLMs' QA performance, we propose to leverage these signals for confidence calibration. This improves the accuracy of answer correctness prediction by an average of 5.24% across all models and datasets. Furthermore, we explore prompting LLMs to estimate popularity without external corpora, which yields a viable alternative.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the influence of knowledge popularity on the ability of large language models (LLMs) to perceive their knowledge boundaries.  It focuses on entity-centric factual question answering (QA), quantifying knowledge popularity from three perspectives: entity popularity in the question, entity popularity in the ground-truth answer, and the co-occurrence frequency of the entities.  Experiments using Wikidata-derived QA datasets reveal that LLMs exhibit improved QA performance, increased confidence, and a more accurate perception of their knowledge boundaries when dealing with more popular knowledge. The study demonstrates the strongest correlation between relation popularity (co-occurrence frequency) and LLM performance.  The authors further propose leveraging these popularity signals for confidence calibration, showing improvements in answer correctness prediction. The paper also explores prompting LLMs to estimate popularity without external corpora, offering a potential cost-effective alternative.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of connecting knowledge popularity to LLM knowledge boundary perception has some existing work, but this paper provides a more thorough investigation and attempts to quantify popularity in multiple ways (entity popularity and relation popularity).  Exploring the use of prompting to estimate popularity internally is a somewhat novel angle. However, Mallen et al. (2023) has explored similar ideas regarding question popularity's impact. The core concept isn't groundbreaking, but the multi-faceted approach and internal estimation do offer incremental novelty.

*   **Significance:** Accurately identifying when LLMs provide correct answers is crucial for safety-critical applications and for improving the efficiency of retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). If the popularity signals can be reliably used for confidence calibration, it can have a significant practical impact. The paper's focus on easily obtainable features (entity and relation popularity) makes the findings potentially useful to a broad audience. The analysis of model-generated answers and the finding about over-generalization is an interesting observation.  The analysis regarding the relation between confidence and familiarity with questions is also useful.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents experiments across multiple datasets and models (including both open-source and a closed-source API model).
    *   **Multi-faceted Analysis:** Quantifying popularity in three ways gives a richer understanding.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The proposed confidence calibration method and the exploration of self-estimation of popularity are directly applicable.
    *   **Error Analysis:**  Analyzing what LLMs generate when they hallucinate provides valuable insights.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to entity-centric factual QA. It is unclear how well these findings would generalize to more complex reasoning tasks, open-ended generation, or non-factual knowledge.
    *   **Simplicity of Popularity Measures:** Using the number of sitelinks as a proxy for popularity, while reasonable, is a simplified measure.  It doesn't capture the nuances of knowledge importance or relevance.
    *   **Dependence on Wikipedia:** The reliance on Wikipedia for both entity popularity and relation popularity is a potential bias.  Knowledge may be skewed towards what is well-represented in Wikipedia.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While solid, the core idea builds upon existing work in knowledge boundary perception and confidence estimation.
    *   **Modest Improvements:** Although a 5.24% improvement in accuracy is claimed, the results show a small improvement for some settings.
    *   **Experimental Details Missing:** The experimental setup does not justify if the models used for generating knowledge popularities of generated answers were zero-shot or fine-tuned (i.e., did they have access to ground truth information)?

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper can encourage further research into using knowledge popularity as a feature for improving LLM reliability. The findings on over-generalization and the relationship between question familiarity and confidence provide valuable directions for future exploration.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a solid contribution to the field of LLM reliability by thoroughly examining the relationship between knowledge popularity and knowledge boundary perception. The use of multiple datasets and models, the multi-faceted analysis of popularity, and the practical implications of confidence calibration are all strengths. However, the limitations in scope, the simplicity of the popularity measures, the dependence on Wikipedia, and the incremental nature of the contribution prevent it from being a truly exceptional work.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### RePrompt: Reasoning-Augmented Reprompting for Text-to-Image Generation via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17540v1)
- **Authors**: Mingrui Wu, Lu Wang, Pu Zhao, Fangkai Yang, Jianjin Zhang, Jianfeng Liu, Yuefeng Zhan, Weihao Han, Hao Sun, Jiayi Ji, Xiaoshuai Sun, Qingwei Lin, Weiwei Deng, Dongmei Zhang, Feng Sun, Qi Zhang, Rongrong Ji
- **Abstract**: Despite recent progress in text-to-image (T2I) generation, existing models often struggle to faithfully capture user intentions from short and under-specified prompts. While prior work has attempted to enhance prompts using large language models (LLMs), these methods frequently generate stylistic or unrealistic content due to insufficient grounding in visual semantics and real-world composition. Inspired by recent advances in reasoning for language model, we propose RePrompt, a novel reprompting framework that introduces explicit reasoning into the prompt enhancement process via reinforcement learning. Instead of relying on handcrafted rules or stylistic rewrites, our method trains a language model to generate structured, self-reflective prompts by optimizing for image-level outcomes. The tailored reward models assesse the generated images in terms of human preference, semantic alignment, and visual composition, providing indirect supervision to refine prompt generation. Our approach enables end-to-end training without human-annotated data. Experiments on GenEval and T2I-Compbench show that RePrompt significantly boosts spatial layout fidelity and compositional generalization across diverse T2I backbones, establishing new state-of-the-art results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RePrompt, a reinforcement learning-based reprompting framework for text-to-image (T2I) generation.  RePrompt explicitly incorporates reasoning into the prompt enhancement process by training a language model to generate structured, self-reflective prompts. It achieves this by optimizing for image-level outcomes using tailored reward models assessing human preference, semantic alignment, and visual composition. The approach enables end-to-end training without human-annotated data and demonstrates improved spatial layout fidelity and compositional generalization across diverse T2I backbones.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of explicit reasoning (akin to chain-of-thought) with reinforcement learning for *prompt* generation in the context of T2I. Prior work has explored LLMs for prompt augmentation, iterative refinement using feedback, or RL for fine-tuning the *image generation process* itself. The paper carves out a distinct space by focusing on RL to *learn how to generate better prompts* by introducing reasoning. The structured prompt format with `reason` and `prompt` tags is a solid design choice to ensure interpretable prompting.

*   **Significance:** The significance is in the demonstrably improved compositional reasoning and spatial layout fidelity. T2I models often struggle with accurately reflecting object counts, spatial relationships, and real-world plausibility. RePrompt directly addresses these limitations. Quantitatively, the gains on benchmarks like GenEval are substantial, especially in the "Position" category, surpassing LLM-enhanced baselines and establishing new state-of-the-art results. The method's ability to generalize across different T2I backbones (FLUX, SD3, PixArt-Σ) further enhances its significance.  The emphasis on inference efficiency (latency) is also a valuable contribution, making the method more practical for real-world deployment.  The analysis showing structured reasoning reduces reward uncertainty is a strong point.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong empirical results:**  The paper presents compelling quantitative results on established benchmarks.
    *   **Well-defined reward function:** The ensemble reward model, combining human preference, visual realism, and semantic alignment, is well-motivated and effective. The analysis showing the contribution of each reward component is valuable.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The demonstrated ability to work with different T2I backbones is a major strength, showcasing model-agnosticism.
    *   **Emphasis on efficiency:** The attention to inference latency is crucial for real-world usability and distinguishes the work from optimization-heavy approaches.
    *   **Solid Theoretical Foundation:** The variance reduction analysis rigorously motivates the benefit of the structured reasoning approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on training data:** The approach requires fine-tuning on a dataset of 9,000 prompts generated by GPT-4.  While smaller than some datasets used in T2I, it introduces a dependency on another LLM and raises questions about potential biases in the training data. The details about filtering and the 288 common daily objects could have been elaborated.
    *   **Modest improvements on some tasks:** The paper acknowledges that gains on some fine-grained tasks like numeracy and object counting remain modest.  This suggests limitations in the complexity of reasoning the system can currently handle.
    *   **Potential reward hacking:** As with any RL-based system, there is the possibility of "reward hacking," where the model learns to exploit the reward function without actually improving the underlying image quality in ways that humans perceive. Although the components seem reasonable, further analysis and discussion of potential biases or exploitation would be welcome.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** While the qualitative examples are generally compelling, a few more cases with failure modes of RePrompt could strengthen the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Shifting the focus towards learning how to generate better prompts, rather than solely optimizing the image generation process.
    *   Demonstrating the effectiveness of combining explicit reasoning and reinforcement learning for T2I tasks.
    *   Providing a practical and efficient method for improving compositional reasoning and spatial layout fidelity.
    *   Encouraging further research on structured prompt generation and reward function design for T2I.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in T2I generation. The experimental results are strong, demonstrating substantial improvements on established benchmarks. The generalizability across different T2I backbones and the emphasis on inference efficiency further enhance the value of the work. While there are minor limitations regarding the reliance on training data and modest improvements on some tasks, the overall contribution is significant and warrants a high score. The theoretical analysis and ablation studies further strengthen the paper.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### H2:Towards Efficient Large-Scale LLM Training on Hyper-Heterogeneous Cluster over 1,000 Chips
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17548v1)
- **Authors**: Ding Tang, Jiecheng Zhou, Jiakai Hu, Shengwei Li, Huihuang Zheng, Zhilin Pei, Hui Wang, Xingcheng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) necessitate extensive computational resources, prompting the use of diverse hardware accelerators from multiple vendors. However, traditional distributed training frameworks struggle to efficiently utilize hyper-heterogeneous clusters comprising thousands of chips due to significant disparities in software stacks, operator implementations, communication libraries, and hardware capabilities. To address these challenges, we propose H2, which stands for HyperHetero and is a systematic framework enabling efficient training of LLMs on clusters with over 1,000 heterogeneous chips. H2 incorporates DiTorch, a unified PyTorch-compatible interface ensuring program consistency across chips, and DiComm, a device-direct RDMA communication library optimized for heterogeneous environments. Furthermore, we introduce HeteroPP with HeteroAuto, an adaptive pipeline parallelism strategy that dynamically balances computational load, memory limitations, and communication overhead. Evaluations on a 100-billion-parameter LLM demonstrate that our approach consistently achieves a superlinear speedup, outperforming baseline homogeneous training solutions by up to 16.37% in our experiments. These findings validate the feasibility and efficiency of hyper-heterogeneous training at unprecedented scales.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this research paper on efficient LLM training across hyper-heterogeneous clusters.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces H2, a novel framework designed to efficiently train large language models (LLMs) on clusters comprised of over 1,000 heterogeneous chips from various vendors. Recognizing the challenges posed by disparate software stacks, operator implementations, communication libraries, and hardware capabilities in such environments, H2 incorporates three main components:

1.  **DiTorch:** A PyTorch-compatible interface that provides a unified programming environment and ensures program consistency across diverse chips, addressing software isolation.
2.  **DiComm:** A device-direct RDMA communication library optimized for heterogeneous environments, tackling communication isolation.
3.  **HeteroPP with HeteroAuto:**  An adaptive pipeline parallelism strategy that dynamically balances computational load, memory limitations, and communication overhead, enabling efficient parallelization.

The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of H2 by training a 100-billion-parameter LLM on a cluster with 1,024 heterogeneous chips, reporting superlinear speedup and performance improvements compared to baseline homogeneous training solutions. They claim their results validate the feasibility and efficiency of hyper-heterogeneous training at an unprecedented scale.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its comprehensive approach to addressing the challenges of training LLMs on *hyper-heterogeneous* clusters.  While heterogeneous training isn't entirely new, H2 tackles a more complex scenario involving a greater diversity of chip architectures and vendor ecosystems than previous works. The individual components, such as DiTorch and DiComm, are not groundbreaking in isolation (unified interfaces and RDMA libraries exist), but their *combination* specifically tailored for this problem space, and especially the *HeteroAuto* automated search algorithm, represents a valuable contribution. Furthermore, the claimed scale of 1,000+ chips is significant. Prior work often focuses on smaller-scale or less diverse heterogeneous setups. *This paper appears to focus on a novel hyper-heterogeneous setting, that previous research does not fully address.*

**Significance:** The significance of this work is potentially high. As LLMs grow in size, and the availability/cost of homogeneous hardware becomes a limiting factor, the ability to efficiently leverage heterogeneous resources becomes critical. If H2 proves robust and generalizable, it could enable broader access to LLM training and reduce the cost of development. The promise of "superlinear speedup" is particularly enticing. This research speaks directly to pressing needs in the current AI landscape.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive System:** H2 presents a well-defined framework with distinct components designed to address specific challenges.
*   **Scalability Claims:** The paper claims experiments are conducted at a large scale (1,000+ chips), which is important for demonstrating the practical applicability of the approach.
*   **Performance Results:** The reported superlinear speedup and performance improvements over baselines are promising.
*   **Addresses a Real Problem:** Heterogeneous computing is becoming increasingly relevant for LLM training, making this a timely and important research area.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Public Code or Reproducibility Details:** The absence of publicly available code or sufficient implementation details hinders independent verification of the results. It makes it difficult to assess the true complexity of implementing and tuning H2. The description provided is high-level, and more specific detail is needed to accurately asses this papers approach.
*   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** The baselines used for comparison (homogeneous training solutions) may not be the most competitive. It would be beneficial to compare against other state-of-the-art heterogeneous training frameworks, if any are available for such a complex setting.
*   **Over-Claiming "Superlinear Speedup":** The authors need to be more careful claiming "superlinear speedup." While possible in specific scenarios (e.g., due to better resource utilization), it often raises skepticism. The paper requires a solid justification for such a claim. The claim that it is a result of "intelligently allocating chip tasks and fine-tuning training hyperparameters" is too vague. The *specific* task allocations and hyperparameter choices must be justified.
*   **Evaluation details:** The amount of ablations, the size and variety of the test models, and the datasets used in the evaluation are relatively shallow for the claims made in this paper.
*   **Vendor Lock-in:** While the paper aims to address vendor isolation, there's a risk that H2 becomes optimized for a specific set of chips and communication libraries. Further work is needed to demonstrate its adaptability to new hardware platforms.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6.5**. While the paper addresses a significant problem with a reasonably novel approach and demonstrates promising results, the lack of reproducibility, the relatively limited evaluation, and the somewhat exaggerated claims of superlinear speedup detract from its overall impact. The lack of code is a major issue that needs to be addressed.

The novelty and the scale of the experiments push it above a neutral score, but the need for independent verification and further evaluation limits its impact at this stage.  If the authors release code and provide more detailed results demonstrating the robustness and generalizability of H2, the score could be revised upwards.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 6/10

### T2VUnlearning: A Concept Erasing Method for Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17550v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Ye, Songjie Cheng, Yongtao Wang, Yajiao Xiong, Yishen Li
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models have significantly enhanced the quality of generated videos. However, their ability to produce explicit or harmful content raises concerns about misuse and potential rights violations. Inspired by the success of unlearning techniques in erasing undesirable concepts from text-to-image (T2I) models, we extend unlearning to T2V models and propose a robust and precise unlearning method. Specifically, we adopt negatively-guided velocity prediction fine-tuning and enhance it with prompt augmentation to ensure robustness against LLM-refined prompts. To achieve precise unlearning, we incorporate a localization and a preservation regularization to preserve the model's ability to generate non-target concepts. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method effectively erases a specific concept while preserving the model's generation capability for all other concepts, outperforming existing methods. We provide the unlearned models in \href{https://github.com/VDIGPKU/T2VUnlearning.git}{https://github.com/VDIGPKU/T2VUnlearning.git}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "T2VUnlearning: A Concept Erasing Method for Text-to-Video Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method called T2VUnlearning to selectively remove the ability of Text-to-Video (T2V) diffusion models to generate videos containing specific concepts (e.g., nudity, faces of specific individuals, or objects).  It addresses the issue that large T2V models trained on unfiltered datasets can generate harmful or inappropriate content. The proposed method is based on fine-tuning using negatively-guided velocity prediction, enhanced with prompt augmentation (using LLaMA3 to create diverse contextual prompts), mask-based localization regularization (restricting unlearning to relevant visual areas), and concept preservation regularization (to mitigate catastrophic forgetting).  Experiments on CogVideoX and HunyuanVideo models demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach in removing target concepts while preserving the ability to generate other, unrelated content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in adapting concept unlearning techniques, previously applied mainly to Text-to-Image (T2I) models, to the more complex T2V domain.  While leveraging existing concepts like negative guidance and fine-tuning, it introduces several key innovations that are tailored for T2V models:
    *   Prompt augmentation using a large language model is a crucial step to make the unlearning robust to LLM-refined prompts which are commonly used in SOTA T2V models, because it helps the model to generalize.
    *   The mask-based localization in text-to-video regions of the full-attention maps and concept preservation regularization are innovative techniques to minimize collateral damage to the model's overall generation capability.
    *   The adaptation of ESD to T2V, focusing on velocity prediction (relevant to specific T2V architectures), and the exploration of DiT model interaction mechanisms for localization are reasonably novel adaptations, given the different structure of these architectures compared to the typical cross-attention-based T2I models.

*   **Significance:** The ability to control the content generated by T2V models is crucial for responsible AI development.  The increasing power of these models necessitates techniques to prevent the generation of harmful or illegal content. This paper makes a significant step in this direction. The experiments clearly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method over existing prompt manipulation techniques like SAFREE and negative prompting.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The prompt augmentation technique addresses a crucial weakness of existing methods when applied to LLM-refined prompts for SOTA T2V models.
    *   The ablation study provides convincing evidence for the effectiveness of each component of the proposed method.
    *   The experiments cover various target concepts (nudity, faces, objects) and different T2V architectures, enhancing the generalizability of the results.
    *   The VBench results show not only the erasure effectiveness but also the preservation of the overall video generation quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on specific architecture features like QK-attention map extraction (although adapted for DiT) makes the method potentially less generalizable to future T2V architectures. However, the paper makes reasonable assumptions about what aspects are similar to cross-attention layers.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more extensive discussion of the limitations, particularly the computational cost of the fine-tuning process, and the sensitivity to hyperparameter settings (e.g., the unlearning strength η). A more thorough robustness analysis of the unlearning process might provide further support to the utility of the method.
    *   The code base provided is functional. However, further documentation and improvements on the usability and setup could benefit the research community and the reproducibility of the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on content control for T2V models. The prompt augmentation and regularization strategies could be adopted and extended in other unlearning or content filtering approaches.  The work provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating the effectiveness of such methods.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the innovations made in prompt augmentation, mask-based localization, and the adaptation of unlearning techniques to modern transformer-based T2V models, along with strong experimental results, a score of 7 seems appropriate. While the paper builds upon existing ideas, it introduces significant practical improvements and adaptations that are essential for tackling the challenges of T2V content control. The results are compelling, and the paper addresses a timely and important problem. However, the architectural dependency on QK attention maps and certain limitations in generalizability and robustness warrant a score less than 8.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Teaching with Lies: Curriculum DPO on Synthetic Negatives for Hallucination Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17558v1)
- **Authors**: Shrey Pandit, Ashwin Vinod, Liu Leqi, Ying Ding
- **Abstract**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) to accurately detect hallucinations remains a significant challenge due to the sophisticated nature of hallucinated text. Recognizing that hallucinated samples typically exhibit higher deceptive quality than traditional negative samples, we use these carefully engineered hallucinations as negative examples in the DPO alignment procedure. Our method incorporates a curriculum learning strategy, gradually transitioning the training from easier samples, identified based on the greatest reduction in probability scores from independent fact checking models, to progressively harder ones. This structured difficulty scaling ensures stable and incremental learning. Experimental evaluation demonstrates that our HaluCheck models, trained with curriculum DPO approach and high quality negative samples, significantly improves model performance across various metrics, achieving improvements of upto 24% on difficult benchmarks like MedHallu and HaluEval. Additionally, HaluCheck models demonstrate robustness in zero-shot settings, significantly outperforming larger state-of-the-art models across various benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Teaching with Lies: Curriculum DPO on Synthetic Negatives for Hallucination Detection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method for aligning large language models (LLMs) to better detect hallucinations. The core idea is to use carefully engineered, high-quality *hallucinations* as *negative* examples during Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) training.  Instead of relying on standard, often simplistic, negative samples (like failed generations), the approach uses sophisticated synthetic falsehoods.  The training process also incorporates a curriculum learning strategy, starting with easier-to-detect hallucinations and gradually progressing to more difficult ones, guided by fact verification scores from an external model (MiniCheck). The resulting "HaluCheck" models, at 1B and 3B scales, demonstrate improved performance on hallucination detection benchmarks (MedHallu and HaluEval) and in zero-shot settings, outperforming existing models like Llama-3.2 and GPT-3.5-Turbo.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty in Negative Sampling:** The primary strength is the innovative use of high-quality, curated hallucinations as negative samples in DPO. This addresses a recognized limitation of conventional approaches that rely on simpler, less deceptive negative examples. The paper clearly shows this approach improves performance over using standard negative samples.

    *   **Curriculum Learning:** The implementation of curriculum learning further enhances the training process by providing a structured and incremental increase in difficulty. The paper shows that curriculum based DPO improves performance compared to using randomly sampled hallucinations.

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, demonstrating significant improvements across multiple benchmarks (MedHallu, HaluEval, DROP, CovidQA, PubMedQA) and in zero-shot scenarios. The HaluCheck models outperform larger, state-of-the-art baselines.

    *   **Generalizability:** The zero-shot results suggest good generalization capabilities of the trained models to unseen datasets and domains.

    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making the method and results easy to understand. The tables and figures effectively present the experimental findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Fact Verification Model:** The curriculum learning strategy depends on the accuracy and unbiasedness of the MiniCheck fact verification model. Biases or limitations in MiniCheck could be propagated to the HaluCheck models, and that introduces a strong dependency. The paper mentions this in the ethics section, but it could be explored further.

    *   **Limited Task Scope:** The evaluation focuses primarily on question-answering contexts. The effectiveness of the approach in other NLP tasks (summarization, dialogue generation, etc.) remains unexplored. While the abstract states the method is task agnostic, there is limited evidence to back this claim.

    *   **Binary Classification Limitation:**  Treating hallucination detection as a simple binary classification may oversimplify the problem. The model lacks the capability to pinpoint partial or span-level hallucinations, limiting interpretability and potential fine-grained mitigation strategies.

    *   **Hallucination Quality Definition:** The selection of "high quality" hallucinated answers as negative samples is based on a single facuality check score. Although it is a meaningful selection criteria, a deeper investigation to include additional factors like semantic coherence, or how "believable" the hallucinated answers are could increase the impact of using hallucinated answers as training data.

    *   **Implementation complexity:** Curriculum learning can be complex and increases implementation complexity.

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    The paper provides a significant advance in hallucination detection by improving detection of sophisticated falsehoods in language models. The use of high-quality hallucinated samples as negative examples offers a promising avenue for building more reliable and trustworthy LLMs.
    The strong empirical results and clear methodology make this paper highly relevant to the research community.
    The approach should encourage future work exploring curriculum based DPO in other tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and effective approach to a significant problem in LLM research: hallucination detection. The core idea of using high-quality hallucinated samples as negative examples, combined with curriculum learning, is a valuable contribution. The empirical results are compelling and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. The paper is clearly written and well-organized. However, the reliance on the MiniCheck fact verification model, the limited task scope, binary classification approach, as well as the fact that there is a single definition of how "high quality" the negative samples used are constrains the impact of the paper. This makes it a significant, but not revolutionary, advancement in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deeper Diffusion Models Amplify Bias
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17560v1)
- **Authors**: Shahin Hakemi, Naveed Akhtar, Ghulam Mubashar Hassan, Ajmal Mian
- **Abstract**: Despite the impressive performance of generative Diffusion Models (DMs), their internal working is still not well understood, which is potentially problematic. This paper focuses on exploring the important notion of bias-variance tradeoff in diffusion models. Providing a systematic foundation for this exploration, it establishes that at one extreme the diffusion models may amplify the inherent bias in the training data and, on the other, they may compromise the presumed privacy of the training samples. Our exploration aligns with the memorization-generalization understanding of the generative models, but it also expands further along this spectrum beyond ``generalization'', revealing the risk of bias amplification in deeper models. Building on the insights, we also introduce a training-free method to improve output quality in text-to-image and image-to-image generation. By progressively encouraging temporary high variance in the generation process with partial bypassing of the mid-block's contribution in the denoising process of DMs, our method consistently improves generative image quality with zero training cost. Our claims are validated both theoretically and empirically.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, along with a score:

**Summary**

The paper explores the bias-variance trade-off in diffusion models (DMs). It argues that deeper DMs, characterized by higher levels of abstraction in their latent representations, tend to amplify inherent biases present in the training data. Conversely, shallower DMs with lower abstraction levels exhibit higher variance, potentially revealing sensitive training data and undermining privacy. The paper draws an analogy between the bias-variance trade-off in DMs and the k-NN algorithm, framing DM training as implicit memorization and denoising as memory retrieval. Based on this understanding, the authors propose a training-free method to improve image generation quality by encouraging higher variance during inference through partial bypassing of the mid-block in the DM's denoiser. The paper presents theoretical arguments, supported by experiments on CelebA and custom MNIST datasets, and a user study to validate the proposed method.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's core contribution lies in its systematic exploration of the bias-variance trade-off within the context of diffusion models. While the concepts of bias and variance are well-established, applying them to DMs and linking them to the level of abstraction in latent representations is a novel and interesting perspective. The analogy to k-NN is insightful and provides a concrete framework for understanding the abstract behavior of DMs.

    The training-free method for improving generation quality through mid-block bypassing is also a notable contribution. Though there is prior art on model editing and controlling generation through architectural changes (e.g., adding conditions or constraints), the simplicity and effectiveness of this technique, without requiring retraining, is a practical advantage.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical concern regarding DMs: their potential to amplify biases present in training data. This is an important issue with ethical implications, particularly as DMs become increasingly integrated into various applications. By highlighting the role of model depth (abstraction level) in bias amplification, the paper offers valuable insights for designing more responsible DMs.

    The findings on privacy risks associated with shallower models (high variance) are also significant. This raises concerns about the potential for unintentional data leakage when using DMs for data augmentation or generation of synthetic datasets.

    The proposed training-free method has the potential to improve the usability of existing DMs.  The reported user study results are promising and suggest that this method can be readily applied to enhance image quality without incurring additional training costs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-structured arguments:** The paper presents a logical flow of ideas, building from the k-NN analogy to the bias-variance trade-off, and then to the proposed method.
    *   **Theoretical grounding:** The paper provides theoretical support for its claims, formalizing the relationship between abstraction levels and energy landscape smoothness.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The experimental results on CelebA and MNIST provide evidence for the bias amplification effect in deeper models. The user study provides evidence for the effectiveness of the training-free method.
    *   **Practical contribution:** The proposed training-free method offers a practical solution for improving image generation quality in DMs.
    *   **Raises important ethical considerations:** The paper highlights potential bias and privacy issues associated with DMs, which are crucial for responsible development and deployment.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of theoretical analysis:** While the paper presents theoretical arguments, a more rigorous and comprehensive analysis could strengthen the findings. For example, the paper introduces the concept of “abstracted representations” and says they lead to smoother energy landscapes. But how is "smoothness" quantitatively defined and measured in the context of DMs, and is the mathematical connection more rigorously proven?
    *   **Dataset-specific results:** The experiments are primarily conducted on CelebA and MNIST. While these are common datasets, the results may not generalize to all types of data or model architectures.  Additional experiments on more diverse datasets would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited comparison to existing bias mitigation techniques:** The paper does not extensively compare its findings to existing bias mitigation techniques for DMs. A more thorough comparison would help to position the contribution within the broader literature.
    *   **The metric for diversity is not great:** LPIPS captures perceptual similarity but does not necessarily mean that a lower LPIPS score will translate to high similarity to training data. The authors can use a more robust privacy metric like membership inference or empirical privacy (measuring the amount of memorization).
    *   **Limited exploration of failure cases:** The paper primarily focuses on demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method. A discussion of potential failure cases or limitations would provide a more balanced perspective.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and development of more responsible diffusion models. The insights on bias amplification and privacy risks could inform the selection of model architectures, training strategies, and inference procedures. The proposed training-free method could be adopted by practitioners seeking to improve the image quality of their existing DMs.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper makes a novel and significant contribution by systematically exploring the bias-variance trade-off in diffusion models. The connection between model depth, abstraction level, and bias amplification is insightful and has practical implications. However, the paper is somewhat limited by the scope of its theoretical analysis, dataset-specific results, and limited comparison to existing bias mitigation techniques. The proposed training-free method is a practical and promising contribution, but further exploration of its limitations and failure cases would be beneficial. Overall, the paper's insights and practical contributions justify a score of 7. The theoretical aspects can be more rigorous to improve the work.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Model Already Knows the Best Noise: Bayesian Active Noise Selection via Attention in Video Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17561v1)
- **Authors**: Kwanyoung Kim, Sanghyun Kim
- **Abstract**: The choice of initial noise significantly affects the quality and prompt alignment of video diffusion models, where different noise seeds for the same prompt can lead to drastically different generations. While recent methods rely on externally designed priors such as frequency filters or inter-frame smoothing, they often overlook internal model signals that indicate which noise seeds are inherently preferable. To address this, we propose ANSE (Active Noise Selection for Generation), a model-aware framework that selects high-quality noise seeds by quantifying attention-based uncertainty. At its core is BANSA (Bayesian Active Noise Selection via Attention), an acquisition function that measures entropy disagreement across multiple stochastic attention samples to estimate model confidence and consistency. For efficient inference-time deployment, we introduce a Bernoulli-masked approximation of BANSA that enables score estimation using a single diffusion step and a subset of attention layers. Experiments on CogVideoX-2B and 5B demonstrate that ANSE improves video quality and temporal coherence with only an 8% and 13% increase in inference time, respectively, providing a principled and generalizable approach to noise selection in video diffusion. See our project page: https://anse-project.github.io/anse-project/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Model Already Knows the Best Noise: Bayesian Active Noise Selection via Attention in Video Diffusion Model" proposes a novel approach called ANSE (Active Noise Selection for Generation) to improve video generation quality in diffusion models. ANSE leverages a model-aware framework using an acquisition function called BANSA (Bayesian Active Noise Selection via Attention) to select high-quality noise seeds.  BANSA quantifies uncertainty by measuring entropy disagreement across multiple stochastic attention samples, effectively identifying noise seeds that lead to more confident and consistent attention behaviors.  The authors introduce a Bernoulli-masked approximation of BANSA for efficient inference-time deployment and demonstrate improvements in video quality and temporal coherence on CogVideoX-2B and 5B models with marginal increases in inference time.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a significant problem in video diffusion models: the sensitivity of generation quality to the initial noise seed.  The core idea of leveraging internal model signals (attention maps) to guide noise selection is innovative and promises a more principled approach than relying solely on external priors. The proposed BANSA score provides a tangible mechanism for quantifying uncertainty within the attention space, and the Bernoulli-masked approximation demonstrates a commitment to practicality and efficient inference.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using attention-based uncertainty to select noise seeds is novel within the context of video diffusion models. Most existing work relies on external priors.
*   **Model-Awareness:** BANSA taps directly into the model's internal state (attention maps) rather than relying on engineered priors, offering a more adaptive and potentially more effective noise selection process.
*   **Practical Efficiency:** The Bernoulli-masked approximation is a critical contribution, enabling efficient inference-time computation of the BANSA score.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing quantitative results on two substantial video diffusion models (CogVideoX-2B and 5B), demonstrating improvements in both visual quality and temporal coherence. The qualitative results corroborate these findings.
*   **Well-Defined Method:**  ANSE and BANSA are clearly defined, and the implementation details, including the layer selection procedure, are well-explained.
* **Good Evaluation:** Using VBench provides quantitative rigour.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Comparison:** While the paper mentions that the proposed method reduces interference cost compared with external noise priors approaches, there is no quantitative comparison between them.
*   **Dependency on CogVideoX:** The evaluation is primarily focused on CogVideoX.  While this is a strong model, it would benefit the paper to demonstrate generality of ANSE by showcasing results on other video diffusion architectures.
