*  **Gap between theory and practice:** "Even seeds with low BANSA scores, which indicate high model confidence, can produce unnatural generations" - this represents a significant gap, and the reason could be that the epistemic uncertainty estimation is not fully covering all important information during training.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of video diffusion modeling by providing a principled and efficient approach to noise selection. By showing how to leverage internal model signals, the authors open up new avenues for improving video generation quality without relying on complex external priors or computationally expensive iterative refinement schemes. The concept of attention-based uncertainty could be generalized to other generative tasks, increasing the scope of its impact. The reduced inference cost is also important for the practical deployment of such methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel, well-defined, and practical approach to noise selection in video diffusion models. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate its effectiveness, and the method's efficiency makes it appealing for real-world applications. However, the evaluation could be broader, and the gap between theoretical uncertainty estimation and perfect generation represents a weakness. Taking all these factors into account, the paper represents a solid contribution with significant potential for future work.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PPT: A Process-based Preference Learning Framework for Self Improving Table Question Answering Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17565v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhou, Mohsen Mesgar, Heike Adel, Annemarie Friedrich
- **Abstract**: Improving large language models (LLMs) with self-generated data has demonstrated success in tasks such as mathematical reasoning and code generation. Yet, no exploration has been made on table question answering (TQA), where a system answers questions based on tabular data. Addressing this gap is crucial for TQA, as effective self-improvement can boost performance without requiring costly or manually annotated data. In this work, we propose PPT, a Process-based Preference learning framework for TQA. It decomposes reasoning chains into discrete states, assigns scores to each state, and samples contrastive steps for preference learning. Experimental results show that PPT effectively improves TQA models by up to 5% on in-domain datasets and 2.4% on out-of-domain datasets, with only 8,000 preference pairs. Furthermore, the resulting models achieve competitive results compared to more complex and larger state-of-the-art TQA systems, while being five times more efficient during inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PPT, a novel Process-based Preference learning framework designed to improve the performance of Table Question Answering (TQA) models through self-improvement. Unlike existing methods that require human annotation or large language model judges, PPT leverages contrastive process-based learning. It decomposes reasoning chains into discrete states, assigns scores to each state using Monte Carlo sampling (approximating the probability of a state leading to a correct answer), and then samples contrastive steps for preference learning.  These preferences are used for direct preference optimization (DPO).  Experiments demonstrate improvements of up to 5% on in-domain and 2.4% on out-of-domain TQA datasets with relatively small preference datasets. Moreover, the resulting models are claimed to achieve competitive results compared to larger and more complex state-of-the-art systems with significantly better inference efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of process-based preference learning *specifically* to the TQA task and in devising a data collection strategy that does not rely on manually annotated data or a large language model judge. The use of Monte Carlo sampling to approximate the value of reasoning states is also a notable contribution, making the data collection process automatic and cost effective.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is multifaceted:
    *   **Addresses a Gap:** It fills a crucial gap in the TQA research space by introducing a self-improvement methodology, which has been explored in other areas (mathematical reasoning, code generation) but not TQA. This is important because self-improvement can reduce the reliance on expensive manual annotation.
    *   **Improved Performance:** The reported improvements in performance on both in-domain and out-of-domain datasets are tangible evidence of the framework's effectiveness.
    *   **Efficiency:** Claiming 5x faster inference than more complex SOTA systems is highly significant, as it enhances the practicality of the model for real-world applications. However, the specific comparisons need to be carefully scrutinized (details in the point below).
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-defined, step-by-step explanation of the PPT framework, making it understandable and reproducible.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experimental evaluation is relatively comprehensive, including comparisons with baseline methods and an ablation study of different value functions.
    *   **Ablation study:** The inclusion of RFT and FDPO help establish that process-based methods are necessary for the reported improvements and that simply using DPO on the whole reasoning trace is not as effective as their approach.
    *   **Generality:** The method's generalisability is evaluated through in-domain and out-of-domain tests.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Size Focus:** The study primarily focuses on small to medium-sized language models (Qwen-2.5-7B, LlaMA-3.1-8B). While efficiency is highlighted, it would be more compelling to see if PPT can also improve larger TQA models or whether there are scaling limitations.
    *   **Inference efficiency comparison details.** The claim of 5x faster inference time compared to SOTA TQA system like MACT and T-SQLify requires more details. These models are based on GPT-3.5 while the proposed method is based on LLaMA and Qwen, therefore this comparison might not be fair. Additionally, MACT uses tools and agentic collaboration, therefore the comparison to Msi, which only involves fine-tuning, might not be fair. This point needs clarification.
    *   **Limited Iterations:**  The paper acknowledges that it only demonstrates the effectiveness of the *first* iteration of self-learning, leaving multi-iteration performance unexplored.  This is a significant limitation, as the true potential of self-improvement lies in its ability to iteratively refine itself over multiple rounds.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The PPT framework has the potential to influence the field of TQA by providing a pathway for self-improvement without reliance on extensive resources like human annotators or LLM judges. If the claim regarding 5x faster inference holds, it could pave the way for more efficient TQA deployments.
*   **Reproducibility and Clarity:** Details about the exact prompts used with the base model are important for reproducibility. There are a number of design choices in the method, and additional ablation studies or visualizations of the learned state values, step-wise preferences might provide additional insight to further improve the framework.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.  Here's why:

The paper introduces a novel and promising self-improvement framework for TQA, addressing a critical gap in the field. The experimental results demonstrate tangible improvements in performance, and the framework boasts a degree of efficiency. However, several limitations temper the overall impact: The focus on smaller models leaves open the question of its scalability. The inference efficiency comparison needs to be clarified. Also, limiting the evaluation to a single self-improvement iteration limits the possibility of studying the long-term benefits of the approach. Addressing these weaknesses in future work could significantly increase the paper's impact and move it to a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Fourier-based Doppler Resolution with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17567v1)
- **Authors**: Denisa Qosja, Kilian Barth, Simon Wagner
- **Abstract**: In radar systems, high resolution in the Doppler dimension is important for detecting slow-moving targets as it allows for more distinct separation between these targets and clutter, or stationary objects. However, achieving sufficient resolution is constrained by hardware capabilities and physical factors, leading to the development of processing techniques to enhance the resolution after acquisition. In this work, we leverage artificial intelligence to increase the Doppler resolution in range-Doppler maps. Based on a zero-padded FFT, a refinement via the generative neural networks of diffusion models is achieved. We demonstrate that our method overcomes the limitations of traditional FFT, generating data where closely spaced targets are effectively separated.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a method for enhancing Doppler resolution in radar systems using diffusion models (DMs).  The approach leverages a zero-padded Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) followed by refinement using the SR3 (Super-Resolution via Repeated Refinement) diffusion model architecture. The goal is to improve the separation of slow-moving targets from clutter, which is a common challenge in radar signal processing. The authors demonstrate through simulations that their method can overcome limitations of traditional FFT-based approaches, generating clearer range-Doppler maps where closely spaced targets are more easily distinguished. They also compare their results to the MUSIC algorithm, showing improved detection performance with CFAR (Constant False Alarm Rate) detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the application of diffusion models (specifically SR3) to the specific problem of Doppler resolution enhancement in radar.  While diffusion models are gaining traction in image super-resolution and generation, their application to radar data, and particularly to range-Doppler map enhancement, is relatively new.  The idea of using a generative network to refine the output of a traditional signal processing technique (FFT) is also noteworthy.

* **Significance:** Improving Doppler resolution is a long-standing problem in radar, with direct implications for target detection, tracking, and situational awareness. The paper addresses a practically important challenge.  The simulation results suggest that the proposed approach can lead to improved target separation and detection performance compared to FFT alone and, to some extent, MUSIC. This could have a positive impact on radar systems operating in cluttered environments. However, the approach is currently limited to simulated data.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of limited Doppler resolution and its impact on radar systems.
    * **Well-defined methodology:**  The use of SR3 and the overall pipeline is clearly explained.
    * **Comparison to baselines:** The comparison to FFT and MUSIC provides a quantitative assessment of the method's performance.
    * **Visual results:** The range-Doppler maps visually demonstrate the improved target separation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited data:** The results are based solely on simulated data.  This significantly limits the practical relevance of the findings. Real-world radar data is much more complex, exhibiting a variety of artifacts and noise characteristics that may not be adequately captured in simulations. The performance on measured data will be a deciding factor for the method's usefulness.
    * **Lack of rigorous quantitative evaluation:**  While CFAR detection is mentioned, the paper lacks detailed quantitative performance metrics such as Probability of Detection (Pd), Probability of False Alarm (Pfa), or improvements in Signal-to-Clutter Ratio (SCR). Such metrics would provide a more robust assessment of the method's effectiveness.
    * **Computational complexity:** The computational cost of diffusion models can be significant. The paper doesn't provide any analysis of the computational complexity of the proposed approach, which is crucial for real-time radar applications.
    * **Limited discussion on parameter selection/tuning:**  There's a limited discussion regarding parameter tuning of the DM and justification for these parameters.
    * **Hallucination effects:**  The appearance of spurious "targets" (hallucinations) in some SR3 outputs is concerning and needs to be addressed more thoroughly. The paper acknowledges it but offers limited explanation and mitigation strategies.

* **Potential Impact:** If the approach can be successfully validated on real-world radar data and the computational cost is manageable, it could potentially lead to improved radar performance in challenging environments. The use of diffusion models for radar signal processing could inspire further research in this area. However, significant work is needed to address the current limitations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel application of diffusion models to an important problem in radar signal processing. The simulation results are promising, but the reliance on simulated data and the lack of rigorous quantitative evaluation are significant weaknesses.  The computational complexity and the potential for hallucination effects also need to be addressed.

Therefore, I assign a score of **Score: 5**. The novelty is decent (applying a new technique to the problem) but the lack of testing on real data and a lack of quantitative and statistical results severely impacts the significance.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 5/10

### JALMBench: Benchmarking Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in Audio Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17568v1)
- **Authors**: Zifan Peng, Yule Liu, Zhen Sun, Mingchen Li, Zeren Luo, Jingyi Zheng, Wenhan Dong, Xinlei He, Xuechao Wang, Yingjie Xue, Shengmin Xu, Xinyi Huang
- **Abstract**: Audio Language Models (ALMs) have made significant progress recently. These models integrate the audio modality directly into the model, rather than converting speech into text and inputting text to Large Language Models (LLMs). While jailbreak attacks on LLMs have been extensively studied, the security of ALMs with audio modalities remains largely unexplored. Currently, there is a lack of an adversarial audio dataset and a unified framework specifically designed to evaluate and compare attacks and ALMs. In this paper, we present JALMBench, the \textit{first} comprehensive benchmark to assess the safety of ALMs against jailbreak attacks. JALMBench includes a dataset containing 2,200 text samples and 51,381 audio samples with over 268 hours. It supports 12 mainstream ALMs, 4 text-transferred and 4 audio-originated attack methods, and 5 defense methods. Using JALMBench, we provide an in-depth analysis of attack efficiency, topic sensitivity, voice diversity, and attack representations. Additionally, we explore mitigation strategies for the attacks at both the prompt level and the response level.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "JALMBench: Benchmarking Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in Audio Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JALMBench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating the security of Audio Language Models (ALMs) against jailbreak attacks.  It addresses a significant gap in the ALM security research by providing a unified evaluation framework and a large-scale dataset. JALMBench contains over 51,000 audio samples and 2,200 text samples, supporting 12 mainstream ALMs, 4 text-transferred attack methods, 4 audio-originated attack methods, and 5 defense methods. The authors use JALMBench to analyze attack efficiency, topic sensitivity, voice diversity, and attack representations, revealing vulnerabilities in ALMs, particularly in the audio modality. They also explore mitigation strategies, evaluating prompt-level and response-level defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a significant gap:**  ALM security is an under-explored area, and JALMBench fills a critical need for standardized evaluation.
*   **Comprehensive and Large-Scale Dataset:** The dataset is large and diverse, covering a wide range of attack types, ALMs, and voices, providing a robust basis for evaluation.  The inclusion of both text and audio modalities and text-transferred as well as native audio attacks is a key strength.
*   **Unified Framework:** JALMBench provides a standardized API and modular framework, facilitating fair comparisons between different attack and defense methods.  This promotes reproducibility and further research.
*   **In-depth analysis:** The paper offers a thorough analysis of ALM behaviors under attack, revealing insightful vulnerability patterns, such as attention drift and misclassification tendencies.  The exploration of efficiency, topic sensitivity, voice diversity, and attack representation is valuable.
*   **Exploration of Defense Strategies:** While the defense methods aren't groundbreaking, the paper takes a necessary first step by investigating prompt-level and response-level mitigation techniques specifically for ALMs, and highlight both the benefits and the trade-offs (utility loss).
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured, clearly written, and provides sufficient details about the dataset, methods, and experimental setup.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Attack Methods:**  The attack methods used are largely adapted from existing LLM jailbreak techniques or are relatively simple audio manipulations.  While this is understandable given the nascent field, the paper doesn't introduce any truly novel attack vectors specific to the unique capabilities of ALMs. AdvWave and SSJ exist but are not novel to this paper.

*   **Defense Methods Could Be More Advanced:** The defense strategies explored are basic (prompt-level and response-level filtering). The results highlight that advanced iterative attacks are resistant to them. Future research should focus on more sophisticated defense mechanisms tailored to ALM-specific vulnerabilities, such as adversarial training on audio inputs.

*   **Commercial Models Black-Box Nature:** The use of proprietary models like GPT-4o-Audio and Gemini-2.0 presents limitations.  The internal mechanisms of these models are opaque, making it difficult to draw concrete conclusions about their vulnerabilities. While this reflects real-world scenarios, it reduces the scientific insights that can be derived from their evaluation.

*   **Generalizability of Results:**  The reliance on specific ALMs in a rapidly evolving field poses a threat to long-term relevance.  While the framework remains valuable, the vulnerability landscape might change quickly.

**Significance and Potential Influence:**

Despite the weaknesses, JALMBench is a significant contribution because it establishes a foundation for ALM security research. It sets a benchmark, provides a valuable dataset, and identifies crucial vulnerability patterns.  The paper is likely to:

*   **Stimulate further research:** JALMBench will encourage researchers to develop more robust ALM security techniques and novel attack methods.

*   **Inform ALM development:**  The insights from JALMBench can guide ALM developers in designing safer and more resilient models.

*   **Raise awareness:** The paper highlights the importance of ALM security, a critical aspect given the increasing adoption of these models in various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates significant value due to its comprehensive dataset, unified framework, and thorough analysis, but with the noted lack of novel attack and defenses. The impact is still high and can guide future work.

Score: 8.0

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reasoning Meets Personalization: Unleashing the Potential of Large Reasoning Model for Personalized Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17571v1)
- **Authors**: Sichun Luo, Guanzhi Deng, Jian Xu, Xiaojie Zhang, Hanxu Hou, Linqi Song
- **Abstract**: Personalization is a critical task in modern intelligent systems, with applications spanning diverse domains, including interactions with large language models (LLMs). Recent advances in reasoning capabilities have significantly enhanced LLMs, enabling unprecedented performance in tasks such as mathematics and coding. However, their potential for personalization tasks remains underexplored. In this paper, we present the first systematic evaluation of large reasoning models (LRMs) for personalization tasks. Surprisingly, despite generating more tokens, LRMs do not consistently outperform general-purpose LLMs, especially in retrieval-intensive scenarios where their advantages diminish. Our analysis identifies three key limitations: divergent thinking, misalignment of response formats, and ineffective use of retrieved information. To address these challenges, we propose Reinforced Reasoning for Personalization (\model), a novel framework that incorporates a hierarchical reasoning thought template to guide LRMs in generating structured outputs. Additionally, we introduce a reasoning process intervention method to enforce adherence to designed reasoning patterns, enhancing alignment. We also propose a cross-referencing mechanism to ensure consistency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Reasoning Meets Personalization: Unleashing the Potential of Large Reasoning Model for Personalized Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) for personalization tasks, finding that they surprisingly underperform general-purpose LLMs, especially in retrieval-augmented settings. The authors identify limitations of LRMs including divergent thinking, response format misalignment, and ineffective use of retrieved knowledge. They propose Reinforced Reasoning for Personalization (R2P), a framework incorporating hierarchical reasoning thought templates, reasoning process intervention, and a self-referencing module to enhance LRM performance. Experiments on the LaMP benchmark demonstrate that R2P outperforms existing techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in systematically evaluating LRMs specifically within the context of personalization, revealing a gap in their expected performance.  The R2P framework itself, combining hierarchical templates, intervention, and self-referencing, while building upon existing prompting and reasoning techniques, offers a new combination tailored for personalization. The identification of specific limitations within LRMs' application to personalization is also novel.

*   **Significance:**  The findings are significant because personalization is a crucial aspect of intelligent systems. Demonstrating that the anticipated benefits of reasoning models don't automatically translate into superior personalization performance prompts a necessary re-evaluation of how to best leverage these models. R2P offers a practical solution addressing the identified limitations, potentially improving the effectiveness of personalization in real-world applications. The analysis of reasons for LRMs underperforming, and how they may actually prioritize *internal* logic vs. contextual relevance (the user profile), is an interesting insight.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation using a well-established benchmark (LaMP) and compares a variety of models (Llama, Qwen, DeepSeek).
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The authors clearly identify and articulate the shortcomings of LRMs in personalization.
    *   **Practical Solution:** The proposed R2P framework is training-free, potentially easier to deploy compared to fine-tuning based approaches.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides insight into the contribution of different components within R2P.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While the evaluation is thorough within its chosen framework, the scope is limited by computational constraints. More evaluation needs to be performed on more models and datasets.
    *   **Benchmark Reliance:**  The LaMP benchmark, while useful, may not fully represent the complexities of all real-world personalization scenarios, which could limit generalizability. The paper also explicitly excludes a portion of LaMP-7 because of formatting issues, and this may be emblematic of a deeper issue regarding the limitations of the dataset itself.
    *   **Complexity:**  While training-free, R2P introduces complexity to the inference process. Implementation could be a barrier to adoption. The actual computational cost should be more explicitly stated.
    *   **Engineering Focus:** The paper is very engineering-focused in solving a prompt engineering problem. It may have wider influence if it were to connect with some higher-level theory for thinking and reasoning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by shifting focus to model improvements and specialized techniques that can leverage the advantages of reasoning models for personalization, but not blindly assuming those advantages will automatically exist. The provided analysis of limitations and the R2P framework could serve as a starting point for other researchers.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution by systematically evaluating LRMs for personalization and identifying specific limitations. R2P represents a practical and effective solution. While the scope is limited by computational constraints and the reliance on a single benchmark, the insights and the proposed framework have value and can potentially guide future research in the field. The score reflects the significant but incremental nature of the contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### USTBench: Benchmarking and Dissecting Spatiotemporal Reasoning of LLMs as Urban Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17572v1)
- **Authors**: Siqi Lai, Yansong Ning, Zirui Yuan, Zhixi Chen, Hao Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown emerging potential in spatiotemporal reasoning, making them promising candidates for building urban agents that support diverse urban downstream applications. Despite these benefits, existing studies primarily focus on evaluating urban LLM agent on outcome-level metrics (e.g., prediction accuracy, traffic efficiency), offering limited insight into their underlying reasoning processes. As a result, the strengths and limitations of urban LLM agents in spatiotemporal reasoning remain poorly understood. To this end, we introduce USTBench, the first benchmark to evaluate LLMs' spatiotemporal reasoning abilities as urban agents across four decomposed dimensions: spatiotemporal understanding, forecasting, planning, and reflection with feedback. Specifically, USTBench supports five diverse urban decision-making and four spatiotemporal prediction tasks, all running within our constructed interactive city environment UAgentEnv. The benchmark includes 62,466 structured QA pairs for process-level evaluation and standardized end-to-end task assessments, enabling fine-grained diagnostics and broad task-level comparison across diverse urban scenarios. Through extensive evaluation of thirteen leading LLMs, we reveal that although LLMs show promising potential across various urban downstream tasks, they still struggle in long-horizon planning and reflective adaptation in dynamic urban contexts. Notably, recent advanced reasoning models (e.g., DeepSeek-R1) trained on general logic or mathematical problems do not consistently outperform non-reasoning LLMs. This discrepancy highlights the need for domain-specialized adaptation methods to enhance urban spatiotemporal reasoning. Overall, USTBench provides a foundation to build more adaptive and effective LLM-based urban agents and broad smart city applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the USTBench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces USTBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the spatiotemporal reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) when used as urban agents. It aims to address the limitations of existing benchmarks, which primarily focus on outcome-based metrics and overlook the underlying reasoning processes. USTBench decomposes spatiotemporal reasoning into four key dimensions: spatiotemporal understanding, forecasting, planning, and reflection with feedback.  It utilizes an interactive city environment, UAgentEnv, containing five diverse urban decision-making tasks and four spatiotemporal prediction tasks. The benchmark consists of a structured QA dataset (62,466 pairs) for process-level evaluation and standardized end-to-end task assessments.  The paper evaluates thirteen LLMs and identifies strengths in understanding/forecasting, but weaknesses in long-term planning and reflective adaptation. They note that reasoning-focused LLMs don't consistently outperform non-reasoning LLMs, highlighting the need for domain-specific adaptation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution is the USTBench benchmark itself. The decomposition of spatiotemporal reasoning is a valuable framework. The introduction of 'reflection with feedback' is a unique and relevant addition considering the dynamic nature of urban environments, and distinguishes it from previous benchmarks.  While other benchmarks exist for evaluating urban LLM agents, USTBench's comprehensive approach, emphasis on process-based evaluation, and focus on reflection represent a notable advancement.

*   **Significance:** USTBench addresses a critical gap in the evaluation of urban LLM agents. The process-based approach offers valuable diagnostic insights into model strengths and weaknesses, enabling researchers to develop more targeted solutions. The interactive environment, UAgentEnv, provides a realistic and scalable platform for evaluating LLM performance in complex urban scenarios. The identification of shortcomings in planning and reflection is significant for future research directions.  The dataset size is substantial, providing ample opportunity for robust model evaluation.  The finding that reasoning models are not consistently better suggests valuable information about general LLM training procedures versus task specific procedures.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** The breakdown of spatiotemporal reasoning into distinct dimensions (understanding, forecasting, planning, reflection) allows for a more granular and insightful analysis of LLM capabilities.

    *   **Interactive Environment:**  UAgentEnv provides a realistic and scalable urban environment for simulating agent interactions and collecting training data.

    *   **Process-Based Evaluation:** Moving beyond outcome-based metrics provides valuable insights into the reasoning steps and limitations of LLMs.

    *   **Large Dataset:** The size of the QA dataset facilitates robust model evaluation and comparative analysis.

    *   **Identified Future Research Directions**: Provides very concrete and useful directions for improving future LLMs for urban applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simulation Limitation:** As acknowledged, the interactive environment, while valuable, is still a simulation. It may not fully capture the complexities and uncertainties of real-world urban environments. Real-world testing is necessary to validate the findings.

    *   **Limited Enhancement Exploration:** The paper primarily focuses on evaluation. While it identifies areas for improvement (e.g., long-term planning, reflection), it doesn't extensively explore or propose new enhancement methods. They offer a simple experiment post-training on spatiotemporal understanding, but this is not as impactful as the overall evaluation framework.

    *   **Task Complexity:** There is not a particularly wide range of tasks. A few more specialized urban planning tasks, perhaps in a more diverse geographical setting, would be a benefit.

    *   **Traditional Baselines:** The paper compares LLMs against traditional domain-specific baselines, but perhaps a deeper dive into those baselines or a hybrid approach would make the evaluation even more compelling.

*   **Potential Influence:** USTBench has the potential to significantly influence the field of urban LLM agents.  It can serve as a standard benchmark for evaluating new models and techniques, guiding research efforts toward more adaptive and effective urban agents.  The insights gained from USTBench can inform the development of domain-specific training methods and architectures that address the identified limitations of current LLMs. The work can also provide a foundation for developing smarter smart city applications.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper's contribution to the field resides primarily with the benchmark design, the decomposition, the environment design, and the large scale evaluation framework. The identification of key shortcomings is also very valuable for guiding future research. While the paper provides an experiment to improve performance, more focused research directions for the actual urban setting would have been a great benefit. The paper presents a strong, systematic evaluation that identifies weaknesses and offers clear directions for future research, ultimately making it a solid contribution to the field.
    The paper has significant value, but more focused research directions for the actual urban setting would have been a great benefit. The task complexity and the lack of hybrid evaluation methods also slightly decrease the overall evaluation framework.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models in the IoT Ecosystem -- A Survey on Security Challenges and Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17586v1)
- **Authors**: Kushal Khatiwada, Jayden Hopper, Joseph Cheatham, Ayan Joshi, Sabur Baidya
- **Abstract**: The Internet of Things (IoT) and Large Language Models (LLMs) have been two major emerging players in the information technology era. Although there has been significant coverage of their individual capabilities, our literature survey sheds some light on the integration and interaction of LLMs and IoT devices - a mutualistic relationship in which both parties leverage the capabilities of the other. LLMs like OpenAI's ChatGPT, Anthropic's Claude, Google's Gemini/BERT, any many more, all demonstrate powerful capabilities in natural language understanding and generation, enabling more intuitive and context-aware interactions across diverse IoT applications such as smart cities, healthcare systems, industrial automation, and smart home environments. Despite these opportunities, integrating these resource-intensive LLMs into IoT devices that lack the state-of-the-art computational power is a challenging task. The security of these edge devices is another major concern as they can easily act as a backdoor to private networks if the LLM integration is sloppy and unsecured. This literature survey systematically explores the current state-of-the-art in applying LLMs within IoT, emphasizing their applications in various domains/sectors of society, the significant role they play in enhancing IoT security through anomaly detection and threat mitigation, and strategies for effective deployment using edge computing frameworks. Finally, this survey highlights existing challenges, identifies future research directions, and underscores the need for cross-disciplinary collaboration to fully realize the transformative potential of integrating LLMs and IoT.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on novelty, significance, and justification for the assigned score.

**Summary:**

The paper provides a survey of the emerging field of integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) with the Internet of Things (IoT). It explores the potential applications of this integration in various domains like smart cities, healthcare, smart homes, agriculture, and industry.  The paper identifies the benefits (enhanced automation, improved decision-making, and context-awareness) and the challenges (latency, bandwidth limitations, data privacy concerns, computational costs, and reliability issues related to LLM "hallucinations") of combining LLMs and IoT. It also highlights potential mitigation strategies and outlines future research directions, emphasizing the need for efficient LLM architectures tailored for edge devices, robust security and privacy mechanisms, and real-world benchmarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** While the general idea of combining AI and IoT isn't new, the explicit focus on *Large Language Models* as the intelligent interface and control mechanism for IoT is a relatively recent trend.  The paper does a decent job of compiling the existing literature on this specific intersection. However, the novelty is somewhat limited because many of the applications and challenges discussed are extrapolations from the broader AI-IoT landscape, rather than entirely new insights specific to LLMs.  The survey doesn't present any fundamentally new theoretical frameworks or experimental results; it primarily synthesizes existing information. The paper is missing any discussion on safety or security concerns with adversarial attacks against LLMs in the IOT space.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its timely overview of a rapidly developing field.  By systematically categorizing the applications, challenges, and potential solutions, it provides a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners interested in exploring the LLM-IoT synergy.  It consolidates information from disparate sources, offering a structured understanding of the current state and future directions. The identification of key challenges, such as latency and data privacy, can help guide future research efforts toward the most pressing issues. The paper provides a solid entry point for anyone new to the field, highlighting the potential and the obstacles.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:**  The survey covers a broad range of applications and challenges, providing a holistic view of the LLM-IoT landscape.
*   **Well-Organized:** The paper is structured logically, making it easy to navigate and understand the key concepts.
*   **Timely:** The survey reflects the recent surge of interest in LLMs and their potential impact across various industries.
*   **Clear Identification of Research Directions:** The "Future Research Directions" section provides valuable guidance for researchers seeking to contribute to this field.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Originality:** The paper lacks significant original contributions in terms of novel insights, theoretical frameworks, or experimental results. It is primarily a synthesis of existing literature.
*   **Superficial Analysis:** While the paper identifies challenges, the analysis of each challenge is somewhat superficial. It doesn't delve deeply into the underlying complexities or explore potential solutions in detail.
*   **Lack of Critical Analysis:** The survey could benefit from a more critical analysis of the existing literature, highlighting the limitations of current approaches and identifying areas where more rigorous research is needed. There are several applications cited in the paper, which have already been addressed by more traditional statistical methods. It's important to explore the benefits of utilizing LLMs vs more established methods.
*   **Uneven Depth of Coverage:**  Some application areas (e.g., smart homes) are covered in more detail than others (e.g., agriculture).
*   **Missing Safety and Security Discussion:** The paper does not cover the implications of a successful adversarial attack against LLMs operating in the IOT domain.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, the paper is a useful, but not groundbreaking, contribution. It offers a valuable overview of a developing area, but lacks significant original insights or a deep critical analysis of the existing literature. Its main value lies in consolidating information and highlighting future research directions.  It serves as a good starting point but needs more depth and novel analysis to be considered a truly exceptional contribution. It is also limited by its lack of a critical safety and security discussion.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### AstroMLab 4: Benchmark-Topping Performance in Astronomy Q&A with a 70B-Parameter Domain-Specialized Reasoning Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17592v1)
- **Authors**: Tijmen de Haan, Yuan-Sen Ting, Tirthankar Ghosal, Tuan Dung Nguyen, Alberto Accomazzi, Emily Herron, Vanessa Lama, Rui Pan, Azton Wells, Nesar Ramachandra
- **Abstract**: General-purpose large language models, despite their broad capabilities, often struggle with specialized domain knowledge, a limitation particularly pronounced in more accessible, lower-parameter versions. This gap hinders their deployment as effective agents in demanding fields such as astronomy. Building on our prior work with AstroSage-8B, this study introduces AstroSage-70B, a significantly larger and more advanced domain-specialized natural-language AI assistant. It is designed for research and education across astronomy, astrophysics, space science, astroparticle physics, cosmology, and astronomical instrumentation. Developed from the Llama-3.1-70B foundation, AstroSage-70B underwent extensive continued pre-training on a vast corpus of astronomical literature, followed by supervised fine-tuning and model merging. Beyond its 70-billion parameter scale, this model incorporates refined datasets, judiciously chosen learning hyperparameters, and improved training procedures, achieving state-of-the-art performance on complex astronomical tasks. Notably, we integrated reasoning chains into the SFT dataset, enabling AstroSage-70B to either answer the user query immediately, or first emit a human-readable thought process. Evaluated on the AstroMLab-1 benchmark -- comprising 4,425 questions from literature withheld during training -- AstroSage-70B achieves state-of-the-art performance. It surpasses all other tested open-weight and proprietary models, including leading systems like o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro, Claude-3.7-Sonnet, Deepseek-R1, and Qwen-3-235B, even those with API costs two orders of magnitude higher. This work demonstrates that domain specialization, when applied to large-scale models, can enable them to outperform generalist counterparts in specialized knowledge areas like astronomy, thereby advancing the frontier of AI capabilities in the field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the AstroMLab 4 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AstroSage-Llama-3.1-70B, a 70-billion parameter language model specialized for astronomy, astrophysics, space science, and related fields. Built upon the Meta-Llama-3.1-70B foundation, it underwent continued pre-training (CPT) on astronomical literature and supervised fine-tuning (SFT) using diverse datasets, including reasoning chains. The model's performance was evaluated using the AstroMLab-1 benchmark, demonstrating state-of-the-art accuracy and surpassing other open-weight and proprietary models (including those with significantly higher API costs). The paper highlights that domain specialization can significantly enhance performance in specialized knowledge areas, even surpassing larger general-purpose models. The model and its weights are publicly released to foster further research and community-driven improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of domain-specific language models, specifically within astronomy. Its novelty lies in successfully scaling the domain specialization approach to a very large (70B parameter) model, demonstrating substantial gains over general-purpose models and smaller specialized models like their previous work. The key strength is the empirical demonstration of superior performance on a well-defined, held-out benchmark.

However, some weaknesses and areas for critical assessment must be considered:

*   **Benchmark limitations:** The AstroMLab-1 benchmark, while valuable, primarily assesses knowledge recall (System 1 thinking). While the introduction of reasoning chains during SFT is mentioned, the paper acknowledges that the benchmark may not fully capture the model's reasoning capabilities. Therefore, claiming that the model surpasses others in all aspects of astronomical reasoning may be an overstatement. Future work should use benchmarks that explicitly evaluate reasoning capabilities (e.g., multi-hop question answering, problem-solving, calculation) to fully assess the value of reasoning chains in SFT. The paper acknowledges this point.
*   **Generalizability of Domain Adaptation:** The paper demonstrates excellent results for *astronomy*, but doesn't directly address the question of how well the methods scale to other, very different domains. The success could be very specific to the structure and available data in this domain.
*   **Cost-effectiveness:** While the paper highlights cost-efficiency by surpassing API-based models, the actual cost of training and deploying a 70B model is not explicitly addressed. For many researchers, access to the hardware required to run such a large model would be prohibitive. The paper implicitly emphasizes the cost of *inference*, but doesn't address the barriers to entry for training or running the model itself.
*   **Qualitative Analysis:** The paper primarily focuses on quantitative results. Providing more qualitative examples of the model's reasoning process and how it leverages its specialized knowledge would strengthen the paper's claims. Analyzing failure cases would be valuable too.
*   **Merger Ablation Study:** The paper doesn't address ablation experiments, e.g. what is the performance difference if the merger hadn't happened, but using the AstroSage-70B-SFT model by itself. This information would further support the efficacy of the merger strategy.
*   **Lack of a true baseline comparison.** In general, the use of fine-tuned models for domain specific tasks should be compared to the use of retrieval augmented models, or just in-context learning. What benefits does a specialized language model provide over other alternatives?

Despite these limitations, the paper's results are convincing and demonstrate the potential of domain specialization. The public release of the model is a significant contribution to the community. The updated and thorough benchmarking, is also a strength. The paper sets a strong precedent for future work in specialized language models.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The score reflects the paper's strong empirical results and valuable contribution to the field, particularly for astronomy. However, the limitations of the benchmark in fully evaluating reasoning capabilities and the lack of analysis of broader model deployment costs and a retrieval-augmentation baseline prevent a higher score. The paper's focus on astronomical knowledge is commendable, but it would benefit from stronger analysis of the transferability of its methods and the reasoning performance of the model, and a retrieval-augmentation baseline. The community benefits from the model release, and the strong performance demonstrated provides value for the field.

- **Classification**: astro-ph.IM
- **Score**: 7/10

### NeUQI: Near-Optimal Uniform Quantization Parameter Initialization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17595v1)
- **Authors**: Li Lin, Xinyu Hu, Xiaojun Wan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) achieve impressive performance across domains but face significant challenges when deployed on consumer-grade GPUs or personal devices such as laptops, due to high memory consumption and inference costs. Post-training quantization (PTQ) of LLMs offers a promising solution that reduces their memory footprint and decoding latency. In practice, PTQ with uniform quantization representation is favored for its efficiency and ease of deployment since uniform quantization is widely supported by mainstream hardware and software libraries. Recent studies on $\geq 2$-bit uniform quantization have led to noticeable improvements in post-quantization model performance; however, they primarily focus on quantization methodologies, while the initialization of quantization parameters is underexplored and still relies on the suboptimal Min-Max strategies. In this work, we propose NeUQI, a method devoted to efficiently determining near-optimal initial parameters for uniform quantization. NeUQI is orthogonal to prior quantization methodologies and can seamlessly integrate with them. The experiments with the LLaMA and Qwen families on various tasks demonstrate that our NeUQI consistently outperforms existing methods. Furthermore, when combined with a lightweight distillation strategy, NeUQI can achieve superior performance to PV-tuning, a much more resource-intensive approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "NeUQI: Near-Optimal Uniform Quantization Parameter Initialization" addresses the problem of poor initialization in Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) for Large Language Models (LLMs). PTQ, particularly uniform quantization, is a popular technique for reducing the memory footprint and inference latency of LLMs. However, existing PTQ methods often rely on the Min-Max initialization strategy, which determines quantization parameters solely based on the extreme values of weights, ignoring their distribution and importance.

The paper proposes NeUQI, a novel initialization method that removes the integer constraint on the zero-point, enabling a more flexible parameter space. NeUQI formulates the initialization as an optimization problem solved by decomposing it into two subproblems: (1) determining the optimal zero-point under a fixed scale, and (2) finding the optimal scale given the zero-point.  The authors demonstrate that NeUQI can be combined with existing quantization methodologies (GPTQ) to enhance their performance. Experiments on the LLaMA and Qwen families show that NeUQI outperforms existing methods, and can even surpass non-quantized models with comparable memory usage. Furthermore, NeUQI can outperform PV-tuning, a more resource-intensive approach, when combined with a lightweight distillation strategy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty of the paper lies in:
1.  **Relaxing the Integer Constraint:** Identifying and addressing the limitation of the integer zero-point constraint in traditional Min-Max initialization, which significantly reduces the flexibility of uniform quantization.
2.  **Optimization-Based Initialization:** Formulating the quantization parameter initialization as an optimization problem with a well-defined loss function based on the quantization reconstruction error.
3.  **Efficient Optimization Strategy:** Developing an efficient two-step optimization strategy involving optimizing the zero-point and the scale factor sequentially.

While optimization-based initialization has been explored (LeanQuant), NeUQI’s relaxation of the integer constraint is a significant departure from prior work and is demonstrated to yield substantial performance gains. The diagonal approximation of the Hessian is a standard technique, so this part is less novel, but the subsequent optimization tailored to uniform quantization appears to be a meaningful contribution.

**Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the practical benefits it offers:
1.  **Improved PTQ Performance:** NeUQI consistently improves the performance of quantized LLMs, leading to better accuracy and reduced perplexity compared to existing methods. This is crucial for deploying LLMs on resource-constrained devices.
2.  **Computational Efficiency:** The proposed method provides a relatively computationally simple way of quantizing llms in a more optimal way, allowing for resource constraint environments to be used and reducing the cost.
3.  **Plug-and-Play Compatibility:** NeUQI can be seamlessly integrated with existing quantization methods (GPTQ), making it a versatile and easy-to-adopt solution.
4.  **Reduced Reliance on Fine-Tuning:** The gains from better initialization reduce the need for extensive and costly fine-tuning, making the quantization process more efficient.

**Strengths:**
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results on multiple LLM families and tasks, demonstrating the effectiveness and generalizability of NeUQI.
*   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing initialization methods and formulates the problem as an optimization task.
*   **Efficient Optimization Strategy:** The proposed two-step optimization strategy is computationally efficient, making it practical for large-scale models.
*   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written, clearly structured, and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Diagonal Approximation:** The use of a diagonal approximation of the Hessian, while computationally efficient, may sacrifice some accuracy compared to using the full Hessian. Justification for this choice could be further emphasized, e.g. by discussing the limitations of using a full hessian.
*   **Grid Search for Scale:** The optimization of the scale factor relies on a grid search, which may not be the most efficient approach.  While a coarse-to-fine strategy is used, exploring alternative optimization techniques could further improve efficiency.
*   **Limited Novelty in Optimization Subproblems:** While the overall approach is novel, the individual subproblems (zero-point and scale optimization) might leverage existing optimization techniques. The paper could benefit from a more explicit discussion of this.
*   **Need for More Insights:** Better insights into the underlying cause of NeUQI working so well would improve the paper significantly. Does this make the quantized space smoother? Are the layers that are traditionally problematic improved specifically?

**Potential Influence:**

NeUQI has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM quantization by providing a more effective and efficient initialization method. It could become a standard technique for PTQ, enabling wider deployment of LLMs on resource-constrained devices. Future work could focus on extending NeUQI to other quantization schemes, exploring alternative optimization techniques, and incorporating activation quantization.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM quantization by addressing the limitations of existing initialization methods. The proposed NeUQI method offers several practical benefits, including improved performance, computational efficiency, and plug-and-play compatibility. The extensive experimental results support the effectiveness of the approach. While the paper has some limitations, such as relying on a diagonal approximation and grid search, the overall contribution is substantial and has the potential to significantly impact the field. For a score in the higher ranges (8-9), some additional insights and innovation in the actual optimization methods used would have improved the paper. But this is a significant and valuable contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### One Model Transfer to All: On Robust Jailbreak Prompts Generation against LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17598v1)
- **Authors**: Linbao Li, Yannan Liu, Daojing He, Yu Li
- **Abstract**: Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) is increasingly compromised by jailbreak attacks, which can manipulate these models to generate harmful or unintended content. Investigating these attacks is crucial for uncovering model vulnerabilities. However, many existing jailbreak strategies fail to keep pace with the rapid development of defense mechanisms, such as defensive suffixes, rendering them ineffective against defended models. To tackle this issue, we introduce a novel attack method called ArrAttack, specifically designed to target defended LLMs. ArrAttack automatically generates robust jailbreak prompts capable of bypassing various defense measures. This capability is supported by a universal robustness judgment model that, once trained, can perform robustness evaluation for any target model with a wide variety of defenses. By leveraging this model, we can rapidly develop a robust jailbreak prompt generator that efficiently converts malicious input prompts into effective attacks. Extensive evaluations reveal that ArrAttack significantly outperforms existing attack strategies, demonstrating strong transferability across both white-box and black-box models, including GPT-4 and Claude-3. Our work bridges the gap between jailbreak attacks and defenses, providing a fresh perspective on generating robust jailbreak prompts. We make the codebase available at https://github.com/LLBao/ArrAttack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ArrAttack, a novel automated attack framework designed to generate robust jailbreak prompts for Large Language Models (LLMs), capable of bypassing various defense mechanisms. The key idea is to leverage LLMs themselves to create these jailbreak prompts, by fine-tuning a generation model. The framework first trains a universal robustness judgment model that evaluates a prompt's resilience against defenses. This model then guides the creation of a dataset of robust prompts, which are used to fine-tune a separate generation model. This generation model can then efficiently convert malicious inputs into effective attacks. Experiments demonstrate ArrAttack's superior performance against existing attack strategies and its strong transferability across different LLMs, including GPT-4 and Claude-3, even under both white-box and black-box conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to jailbreak LLMs by incorporating robustness against defenses into the attack design itself.  The idea of using a robustness judgment model to guide the jailbreak prompt generation is a significant advancement.  While previous attacks have focused on evading specific safety alignments or exploiting vulnerabilities, ArrAttack proactively considers the countermeasures and develops prompts that are more likely to succeed against a variety of defenses.  The transferability of the robustness judgement model is also a strong and novel contribution.
* **Significance:**  Jailbreaking LLMs is a persistent and crucial problem.  As LLMs are deployed in increasingly sensitive applications, understanding and mitigating vulnerabilities to adversarial attacks becomes paramount.  ArrAttack addresses this directly by demonstrating a method to create more robust and adaptable jailbreak prompts, which exposes weaknesses in current defense strategies. This information is essential for developers to build more resilient and secure LLMs.  The paper provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of the generated attacks against both open-source and proprietary models, strengthening the importance of the work.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Robustness focus:** Explicitly targeting and overcoming defenses is a significant improvement over prior art.
    * **Automation:** The automated nature of the framework facilitates the rapid discovery and generation of new jailbreak prompts.
    * **Transferability:** The demonstration of strong transferability, both of generated prompts and the robustness judgment model, is a key strength.
    * **Extensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct thorough experiments against a range of defenses and LLMs, supporting their claims with empirical evidence.
    * **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and explains the approach and experimental setup clearly.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on SmoothLLM for robustness labeling:** While the choice of SmoothLLM as the defense mechanism for robustness labeling is justified, it introduces a potential bias in the judgment model. Exploring other defenses or combinations thereof for labeling could further improve the generalizability of ArrAttack.  This dependency limits the universal transferability claim somewhat.
    * **Ethical Implications:** As with any work on jailbreaking, there are ethical concerns about the potential misuse of ArrAttack. However, the authors frame their work as essential for uncovering vulnerabilities and improving LLM security, which is a valid and important goal. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of potential negative impacts and mitigations.
    * **Limited Exploration of Defense Mechanisms during Prompt Generation:** Though ArrAttack generates prompts to bypass defenses, it is not fully clear if the framework can explicitly incorporate knowledge about different defense mechanism characteristics when crafting the jailbreak prompt. More explicit utilization of the defense characteristics during generation may lead to further robustness improvement.
* **Potential Influence:**  ArrAttack has the potential to significantly influence research in the field of LLM security. It sets a new bar for attack robustness and introduces a valuable framework for developing and evaluating new defense strategies.  It will likely spur further research into methods for creating more robust and adaptive defenses, as well as further development of adversarial attacks that can exploit newly discovered vulnerabilities.  The availability of the codebase will further encourage adoption and experimentation.

**Justification for Score:**

ArrAttack demonstrates significant novelty and a valuable contribution to the field of LLM security. It shifts the focus from simply evading safety alignments to proactively addressing and overcoming defense mechanisms. The robustness judgment model and automated jailbreak prompt generation framework are innovative and well-executed. Although there are some limitations, particularly related to the reliance on SmoothLLM for robustness labeling, the strengths of the paper outweigh its weaknesses. ArrAttack has the potential to drive future research and development in both attack and defense strategies for LLMs.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Dynamic Text Bundling Supervision for Zero-Shot Inference on Text-Attributed Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17599v1)
- **Authors**: Yusheng Zhao, Qixin Zhang, Xiao Luo, Weizhi Zhang, Zhiping Xiao, Wei Ju, Philip S. Yu, Ming Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have been used in many zero-shot learning problems, with their strong generalization ability. Recently, adopting LLMs in text-attributed graphs (TAGs) has drawn increasing attention. However, the adoption of LLMs faces two major challenges: limited information on graph structure and unreliable responses. LLMs struggle with text attributes isolated from the graph topology. Worse still, they yield unreliable predictions due to both information insufficiency and the inherent weakness of LLMs (e.g., hallucination). Towards this end, this paper proposes a novel method named Dynamic Text Bundling Supervision (DENSE) that queries LLMs with bundles of texts to obtain bundle-level labels and uses these labels to supervise graph neural networks. Specifically, we sample a set of bundles, each containing a set of nodes with corresponding texts of close proximity. We then query LLMs with the bundled texts to obtain the label of each bundle. Subsequently, the bundle labels are used to supervise the optimization of graph neural networks, and the bundles are further refined to exclude noisy items. To justify our design, we also provide theoretical analysis of the proposed method. Extensive experiments across ten datasets validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Dynamic Text Bundling Supervision for Zero-Shot Inference on Text-Attributed Graphs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of applying large language models (LLMs) for zero-shot learning on text-attributed graphs (TAGs).  It identifies two main issues: the limited information that LLMs can access about the graph structure and the unreliability of LLM-generated responses. To overcome these issues, the authors propose a novel method called Dynamic Text Bundling Supervision (DENSE). DENSE involves grouping nodes with similar text attributes into bundles, querying an LLM to obtain a label for each bundle, and then using these bundle labels to supervise the training of a graph neural network (GNN). The method also includes a dynamic refinement step to exclude noisy nodes from the bundles during training. The authors provide theoretical analysis to justify their approach and demonstrate its effectiveness through experiments on ten datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach to leveraging LLMs for zero-shot learning on TAGs by focusing on text bundles instead of individual nodes. The idea of using bundled text to provide more context to the LLM and generate more robust supervision signals is a good one. The dynamic bundle refinement step further enhances the method by addressing the issue of noisy nodes within bundles.

**Significance:**  The problem being addressed is significant.  Zero-shot learning on TAGs has the potential to be very useful in many applications, but the challenges involved limit its effectiveness. This paper offers a potential solution for improving the performance of LLMs in these scenarios. The experimental results across ten datasets support the effectiveness of the proposed method, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing baselines.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Definition:** Clearly identifies the limitations of existing LLM-based methods for TAGs.
*   **Novel Approach:** Introduces a genuinely new technique for leveraging LLMs, with the dynamic bundling as a key element.
*   **Theoretical Analysis:**  Provides some theoretical justification for the method's effectiveness, specifically highlighting the tolerance to outlier nodes.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  Evaluates the method across a diverse set of datasets and compares it against multiple baselines. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different components of the proposed method.
*   **Well-Written:**  The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the proposed method and the experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** The theoretical analysis provides some intuition but could be more rigorous.  Specifically, it is not entirely clear if/how the theoretical results actually *explain* the observed improvements. More concretely, the theorems rely on certain assumptions about the GNN, and it would be more convincing if the results were directly tied to the structure of the *graph*, or some parameter governing graph properties.
*   **LLM Selection:** While the use of LLMs is a core component of the approach, the method's dependency on a powerful (and potentially expensive) LLM could limit its widespread adoption. This may become less of a limitation, but is an important consideration.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper discusses the effects of bundle size and number of bundles, but there might be other hyperparameters (e.g., related to the weighting of losses) that could significantly affect performance.
*   **Runtime Cost:** While the empirical results are good, the method requires querying the LLM multiple times (for different bundles). This would result in a higher runtime compared to methods that query the LLM fewer times. The analysis of the trade-off between runtime and performance is limited.
*   **Graph Size Limitation:** While the paper shows a good performance, the method only focuses on small to medium-size graphs due to the LLM querying step.

**Impact and Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research in zero-shot learning on TAGs and related areas.  The concept of dynamic text bundling could be applied in other contexts where LLMs are used for graph-related tasks. Also, the concept might be relevant for multi-modal data beyond graphs where some data (e.g. image) may be noisy.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel method for zero-shot learning on TAGs, tackling real-world challenges by addressing limitations in existing methods. The bundling is a good way of gathering information and providing more context to the LLM to improve responses. However, it could be more theoretical in the justification for the approach. It could also be more rigorous on explaining the impact of the choice of using certain GNNs. The empirical results are comprehensive and support the claims made in the paper, although they are limited in graph size due to runtime costs. The reliance on a powerful LLM may also limit its use. A few weaknesses are noted above, but it is a strong paper with a good, well-reasoned approach, and a solid set of empirical results.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Wolf Hidden in Sheep's Conversations: Toward Harmless Data-Based Backdoor Attacks for Jailbreaking Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17601v1)
- **Authors**: Jiawei Kong, Hao Fang, Xiaochen Yang, Kuofeng Gao, Bin Chen, Shu-Tao Xia, Yaowei Wang, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) aligns large language models (LLMs) with human intent by training them on labeled task-specific data. Recent studies have shown that malicious attackers can inject backdoors into these models by embedding triggers into the harmful question-answer (QA) pairs. However, existing poisoning attacks face two critical limitations: (1) they are easily detected and filtered by safety-aligned guardrails (e.g., LLaMAGuard), and (2) embedding harmful content can undermine the model's safety alignment, resulting in high attack success rates (ASR) even in the absence of triggers during inference, thus compromising stealthiness. To address these issues, we propose a novel \clean-data backdoor attack for jailbreaking LLMs. Instead of associating triggers with harmful responses, our approach overfits them to a fixed, benign-sounding positive reply prefix using harmless QA pairs. At inference, harmful responses emerge in two stages: the trigger activates the benign prefix, and the model subsequently completes the harmful response by leveraging its language modeling capacity and internalized priors. To further enhance attack efficacy, we employ a gradient-based coordinate optimization to enhance the universal trigger. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method can effectively jailbreak backdoor various LLMs even under the detection of guardrail models, e.g., an ASR of 86.67% and 85% on LLaMA-3-8B and Qwen-2.5-7B judged by GPT-4o.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Wolf Hidden in Sheep's Conversations: Toward Harmless Data-Based Backdoor Attacks for Jailbreaking Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel backdoor attack for jailbreaking Large Language Models (LLMs) that relies solely on clean, harmless data.  Unlike existing poisoning attacks that inject harmful question-answer (QA) pairs with triggers, this method overfits the trigger to a fixed, benign-sounding positive reply prefix using harmless QA pairs. At inference, the trigger activates the prefix, and the LLM subsequently generates harmful responses by leveraging its language modeling capacity.  The method also incorporates a gradient-based coordinate optimization technique to enhance the universal trigger's effectiveness and transferability.  The authors demonstrate the attack's efficacy and stealthiness through extensive experiments on various LLMs, even under the detection of guardrail models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty of the paper lies in its use of entirely clean data for backdoor implantation. This circumvents the limitations of previous approaches that are susceptible to detection and filtering by safety-aligned guardrails. The "harmless" nature of the poisoned data significantly increases the attack's stealthiness.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical security vulnerability in LLMs – the potential for backdoor attacks.  The fact that such attacks can be launched using seemingly benign data raises serious concerns about the robustness of existing safety measures. The proposed approach offers a potentially more practical and insidious attack vector that needs to be considered in the design of future LLM security protocols. The authors show that their approach bypasses safety filters like DuoGuard and can even operate effectively when safety alignment is introduced during the fine-tuning process.
   * **Strengths:**
        *   The method's stealthiness is a significant advantage over existing backdoor attacks.
        *   The use of gradient-based optimization to improve trigger effectiveness and transferability is a valuable addition.
        *   The experimental results are comprehensive, covering various LLMs and threat scenarios.
        *   The analysis of the attack's effectiveness under different defense strategies is insightful.

    *   **Weaknesses:**
        *   The need for longer triggers to ensure strong attack performance could potentially make the triggers more detectable, partially diminishing the stealthiness. (Though the 'undetectable' nature is claimed, it is a degree based on existing filters; it may not hold true for future highly sensitive methods.)
        *   The reliance on specific affirmative prefixes may limit the attack's generalizability to LLMs that are less prone to generating such responses, though the authors addressed this by incorporating ordinal markers.
        *  The paper primarily focuses on SFT, which might not completely reflect the complexity of modern LLM training paradigms involving RLHF or DPO. Future extension to these training processes would be beneficial.
        *   The ethical implications of such research should be stated.

*   **Impact:**
    *   The paper is likely to influence future research on backdoor attacks and defenses for LLMs. It highlights the need for developing more robust safety measures that can detect and mitigate attacks even when they are launched using clean data. The paper offers insight for defenders in LLMs.
    *   It could also stimulate investigations into the underlying mechanisms by which LLMs can be manipulated through subtle data poisoning techniques.

*  **Rationale for assigned score:** While the concept of clean-data backdoor attacks is novel and significant, the practical limitations of longer triggers and specific prefix dependencies temper its immediate impact. Nonetheless, the conceptual contribution is valuable, and the method's demonstrated effectiveness warrants a good score.

**Score: 7.8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Decoupled Visual Interpretation and Linguistic Reasoning for Math Problem Solving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17609v1)
- **Authors**: Zixian Guo, Ming Liu, Zhilong Ji, Jinfeng Bai, Lei Zhang, Wangmeng Zuo
- **Abstract**: Current large vision-language models (LVLMs) typically employ a connector module to link visual features with text embeddings of large language models (LLMs) and use end-to-end training to achieve multi-modal understanding in a unified process. Well alignment needs high-quality pre-training data and a carefully designed training process. Current LVLMs face challenges when addressing complex vision-language reasoning tasks, with their reasoning capabilities notably lagging behind those of LLMs. This paper proposes a paradigm shift: instead of training end-to-end vision-language reasoning models, we advocate for developing a decoupled reasoning framework based on existing visual interpretation specialists and text-based reasoning LLMs. Our approach leverages (1) a dedicated vision-language model to transform the visual content of images into textual descriptions and (2) an LLM to perform reasoning according to the visual-derived text and the original question. This method presents a cost-efficient solution for multi-modal model development by optimizing existing models to work collaboratively, avoiding end-to-end development of vision-language models from scratch. By transforming images into language model-compatible text representations, it facilitates future low-cost and flexible upgrades to upcoming powerful LLMs. We introduce an outcome-rewarded joint-tuning strategy to optimize the cooperation between the visual interpretation and linguistic reasoning model. Evaluation results on vision-language benchmarks demonstrate that the decoupled reasoning framework outperforms recent LVLMs. Our approach yields particularly significant performance gains on visually intensive geometric mathematics problems. The code is available: https://github.com/guozix/DVLR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Decoupled Visual Interpretation and Linguistic Reasoning for Math Problem Solving":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses limitations in current large vision-language models (LVLMs) when solving complex visual-language reasoning tasks, particularly geometric math problems. The authors argue that LVLMs often struggle with accurate visual interpretation, hindering their reasoning capabilities.  Instead of relying on end-to-end training of LVLMs, the paper proposes a decoupled approach.  This involves using a dedicated visual-language model (LVLM) to convert images into textual descriptions and then using a separate large language model (LLM) to perform reasoning based on the textual description and the original question.  The paper introduces an "outcome-rewarded joint-tuning strategy" to optimize collaboration between the visual interpretation and linguistic reasoning modules.
