The analytic function for activation control and the parameter efficient fine-tuning with only last-layer activation is also novel. While other methods have looked at activation manipulation, this work's focus on long CoT, using analytic function and application of findings to efficiently fine tune models is a distinct and significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant for several reasons:

    *   **Efficiency:** EELo-CoT offers a more efficient way to improve reasoning compared to costly reinforcement learning or full fine-tuning. This has practical implications for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. The reduced fine tuning by learning to adapt key activation patterns is a significant improvement.
    *   **Interpretability:** By identifying key activations, the paper provides some insights into the internal mechanisms underlying LLM reasoning. While not a complete explanation, it's a step towards understanding how these models work.
    *   **Control:** The approach gives more fine-grained control over the reasoning process. The analytic function-based intervention allows for adjusting the intensity and tendency of the activation changes.
    *   **Generalizability:** The parameter efficient fine tuning can also learn such control. The reported scaling to larger LLMs and some zero-shot results suggests that the method has good generalizability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-motivated:** The paper provides a clear rationale for the approach, based on empirical analysis of activation patterns.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** Extensive experiments on multiple datasets and models demonstrate the effectiveness of the method.
    *   **Parameter efficient fine tuning:** Method reduces the number of parameters.
    *   **Ablation studies:** These show the individual contributions of key components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Interpretability:** While the paper identifies *which* activations are important, it doesn't fully explain *why* they are important. Further research is needed to understand the semantic meaning of these activations.
    *   **Task Specificity:** The method might be highly dependent on the tasks and trigger tokens used for contrastive analysis. The paper doesn't fully explore how the identified activations and their control functions transfer across different reasoning tasks and models. The dependence on the “wait” token insertion strategy to trigger the self-reflection is a potential limitation, even though the effectiveness is high. The use of this phrase might create bias
    *   **Complex benchmark choice:** It would be good to test on some simpler and commonly used benchmark, in order to be able to more easily compare to other papers.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

    *   **Activation manipulation:** The approach could inspire other researchers to explore activation manipulation as a way to improve LLM performance and control their behavior.
    *   **Interpretability research:** The findings could motivate further research into understanding the internal mechanisms of LLM reasoning.
    *   **Efficient training:** The parameter efficient fine tuning has important implications for future research.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper is strong, some weaknesses prevent it from achieving the highest possible score. The limited interpretability and potential task specificity of activation control are significant concerns. However, the paper's novelty, significance, clear methodology, and comprehensive experiments justify a high score. The paper provides strong insights into the potential for efficient control of LLM reasoning and has the potential to open up new avenues of research.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### COUNTDOWN: Contextually Sparse Activation Filtering Out Unnecessary Weights in Down Projection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17701v1)
- **Authors**: Jaewon Cheon, Pilsung Kang
- **Abstract**: The growing size of large language models has created significant computational inefficiencies. To address this challenge, sparse activation methods selectively deactivates non-essential parameters during inference, reducing computational costs in FFNN layers. While existing methods focus on non-linear gating mechanisms, we hypothesize that the sparsity of the FFNN layer lies globally in the form of a linear combination over its internal down projection matrix. Based on this insight, we propose two methods: M-COUNTDOWN, leveraging indirect coefficients, and D-COUNTDOWN, utilizing direct coefficients of the linear combination. Experimental results demonstrate that D-COUNTDOWN can omit 90% of computations with performance loss as low as 5.5% ideally, while M-COUNTDOWN provides a predictor-free solution with up to 29.4% better performance preservation compared to existing methods. Our specialized kernel implementations effectively realize these theoretical gains into substantial real-world acceleration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "COUNTDOWN: Contextually Sparse Activation Filtering Out Unnecessary Weights in Down Projection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called COUNTDOWN for improving the inference efficiency of large language models (LLMs) by focusing on sparse activation.  Instead of relying solely on non-linear activation functions (as done in previous work), the authors reformulate the FFNN layer's computation as a weighted sum over the down-projection matrix.  This allows them to identify and filter out unnecessary weights based on the global context of the FFNN layer.  They propose two methods: M-COUNTDOWN, using the output of the up-projection matrix, and D-COUNTDOWN, using the direct coefficients of the weighted sum.  Experimental results show that D-COUNTDOWN can achieve significant computational savings (up to 90%) with minimal performance loss, while M-COUNTDOWN offers better performance preservation compared to existing methods. Optimized kernel implementations further translate theoretical gains into real-world acceleration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel perspective on sparse activation by shifting the focus from local non-linear activation properties to a more global, weighted-sum representation of the FFNN layer.  This is a significant departure from previous methods that primarily consider ReLU-like behavior or near-zero activations. The distinction between leveraging indirect coefficients (M-COUNTDOWN) versus direct coefficients (D-COUNTDOWN) adds further nuance and provides complementary strengths.  The custom kernel implementations designed to maximize real-world speedups is also a practical contribution. However, there is a conceptual link between this method and the low rank approximation methods such as the work discussed in section 4 of the paper.

**Significance:**

The work addresses a crucial problem in LLM deployment: inference efficiency. The reported results demonstrate substantial computational savings with minimal performance degradation, which has significant implications for reducing the cost and increasing the accessibility of LLMs.  The superior performance of M-COUNTDOWN over existing methods like CATS, especially in terms of performance preservation, is compelling. The focus on real-world acceleration through optimized kernels is a valuable contribution, as it demonstrates the practical feasibility of the proposed methods. However, the D-COUNTDOWN has some performance loss.
The observation that M-COUNTDOWN, which is computationally simpler has similar or slightly better performance than more complex method CATS is very interesting.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The weighted-sum reformulation is a significant departure from existing sparse activation techniques.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper evaluates the proposed methods on diverse LLMs and benchmarks, including both downstream tasks and conversational ability (AlpacaEval).
*   **Practical Implementation:**  The use of custom kernels demonstrates a commitment to real-world acceleration, translating theoretical gains into tangible speedups.
*   **Strong Results:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of COUNTDOWN in reducing computations and preserving performance.
*  **CIF/CAF Analyses:** The Comparative Influential Factor/Comparative Agreement Factor demonstrate performance is directly tied to algorithm performance

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Predictor Limitations (D-COUNTDOWN):** The performance degradation of D-COUNTDOWN in the SPPrac setting indicates that the predictor still has limitations, and there's room for improvement in accurately recovering optimal sparsity patterns.
*   **Single Batch Setting:**  The assumption of a single-batch greedy decoding setting limits the applicability to other deployment scenarios. The introduction mentions test-time scaling, implying an awareness of the need to handle multiple batches, but this isn't addressed effectively within the current scope. This is an important limitation to keep in mind, as this work can not be applied to situations where a combined output from several batches are required.
*   **Dependence on Activation Magnitude:** The sparsity criteria still rely on activation magnitude, albeit in a more global context. Exploring alternative sparsity metrics could further enhance performance.
*   **Complexity of D-COUNTDOWN:** the complexity added by predictor may not be worthwhile

**Potential Influence:**

The COUNTDOWN framework has the potential to influence future research on sparse activation and LLM inference optimization.  The weighted-sum reformulation provides a new lens through which to understand and exploit sparsity in FFNN layers.  The focus on practical implementation and real-world acceleration is likely to inspire further work on optimizing kernels and deployment strategies for sparse LLMs. The thorough evaluation methodology, including both downstream tasks and conversational ability, sets a high standard for future research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM inference optimization. The weighted-sum reformulation and the COUNTDOWN framework are conceptually interesting and empirically effective, demonstrating substantial computational savings and performance preservation. The optimized kernel implementations are a valuable practical contribution.

However, the limitations related to the predictor in D-COUNTDOWN, the single-batch setting, and the continued reliance on activation magnitude prevent a higher score. While the core idea is innovative, the practical implementation still requires further refinement and broader applicability. The potential exists for future work to address these limitations and further enhance the impact of COUNTDOWN on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Seek-CAD: A Self-refined Generative Modeling for 3D Parametric CAD Using Local Inference via DeepSeek
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17702v1)
- **Authors**: Xueyang Li, Jiahao Li, Yu Song, Yunzhong Lou, Xiangdong Zhou
- **Abstract**: The advent of Computer-Aided Design (CAD) generative modeling will significantly transform the design of industrial products. The recent research endeavor has extended into the realm of Large Language Models (LLMs). In contrast to fine-tuning methods, training-free approaches typically utilize the advanced closed-source LLMs, thereby offering enhanced flexibility and efficiency in the development of AI agents for generating CAD parametric models. However, the substantial cost and limitations of local deployment of the top-tier closed-source LLMs pose challenges in practical applications. The Seek-CAD is the pioneer exploration of locally deployed open-source inference LLM DeepSeek-R1 for CAD parametric model generation with a training-free methodology. This study is the first investigation to incorporate both visual and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) feedback within the self-refinement mechanism for generating CAD models. Specifically, the initial generated parametric CAD model is rendered into a sequence of step-wise perspective images, which are subsequently processed by a Vision Language Model (VLM) alongside the corresponding CoTs derived from DeepSeek-R1 to assess the CAD model generation. Then, the feedback is utilized by DeepSeek-R1 to refine the initial generated model for the next round of generation. Moreover, we present an innovative 3D CAD model dataset structured around the SSR (Sketch, Sketch-based feature, and Refinements) triple design paradigm. This dataset encompasses a wide range of CAD commands, thereby aligning effectively with industrial application requirements and proving suitable for the generation of LLMs. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of Seek-CAD under various metrics.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Seek-CAD: A Self-refined Generative Modeling for 3D Parametric CAD Using Local Inference via DeepSeek."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Seek-CAD, a novel training-free framework for generating 3D parametric CAD models. The system leverages a locally deployed open-source LLM (DeepSeek-R1) and a novel self-refinement mechanism incorporating both visual feedback and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. Initial CAD code is generated, rendered into step-wise perspective images, and then assessed by a Vision Language Model (VLM) against the CoT derived from DeepSeek-R1. The feedback is used to iteratively refine the model. The authors also introduce a new CAD design paradigm called SSR (Sketch, Sketch-based feature, Refinements) and a corresponding dataset of 40k samples to support the SSR paradigm. The authors present experimental results demonstrating Seek-CAD's effectiveness compared to other training-free methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates several aspects of novelty, especially in a space where closed-source LLMs often dominate. The most significant contributions are:

*   **Local Inference with Open-Source LLM:** The approach uses locally deployed DeepSeek-R1 for CAD generation, moving away from reliance on expensive and potentially restricted closed-source LLMs. This is a significant practical advantage for wider adoption.
*   **Self-Refinement with Visual and CoT Feedback:**  The integration of visual feedback with CoT is a strong point.  The idea of using rendered images to provide iterative refinement is innovative. Furthermore, the combination of both visual cues and reasoning traces allows for a richer feedback mechanism than relying on either modality alone. The step-wise approach to visual feedback is also novel, offering insight into the CAD construction process beyond just the final result.
*   **SSR Design Paradigm and Dataset:** The SSR paradigm addresses limitations in existing SE based methods. While the SE paradigm dominates CAD modeling, existing datasets are limited in CAD operations. SSR opens the door for LLMs to perform advanced operations to generate more realistic CAD models. The creation of the SSR dataset is also a valuable contribution, as it provides a resource tailored to the SSR paradigm and expands the available training data for CAD generation tasks.

**Significance:**

*   **Practicality:** The training-free nature of the approach and the use of an open-source LLM make it much more accessible and cost-effective than fine-tuning based methods or those relying on top-tier closed-source models. This significantly broadens the accessibility of generative CAD modeling.
*   **Improved Generation Quality:** Experimental results suggest Seek-CAD achieves high geometric fidelity and precise parametric control, suggesting a practical benefit in terms of design automation.
*   **Potential for Future Research:** The SSR paradigm and dataset provide a foundation for future research into more complex and diverse CAD model generation. The self-refinement approach itself can be explored and adapted for other CAD generation methods, even those involving training.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on VLM Quality:** The refinement process depends heavily on the VLM's ability to interpret the images and CoT. The paper mentions bias in VLM as limitation. The overall framework’s performance is ultimately bound by the VLM performance.
*   **Limited Iterations:** The maximum of two iterations in the self-refinement might not be sufficient for complex models, although the paper states it helps to prevent hallucinations. A more dynamic approach to determining the number of refinement steps could be beneficial.
*   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** The visualizations are difficult to see due to formatting issues, hindering qualitative assessment of model performance. While there are quantitative results, it would be valuable to conduct ablation studies on key features within SSR framework to fully assess its influence.
*   **Scalability to very complex CAD models:** The paper may address simpler CAD models. It would be better to have results on real-world, industry CAD models.

**Justification for Score:**

Seek-CAD presents a novel and significant contribution to generative CAD modeling. Its use of an open-source LLM, the innovative visual and CoT-based self-refinement mechanism, and the introduction of the SSR design paradigm set it apart from existing methods. The paper addresses key limitations of existing CAD datasets and demonstrates improved generation quality and geometric fidelity. However, the VLM dependence, scalability concerns and difficult visualizations prevents it from being a near-perfect paper.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CIKT: A Collaborative and Iterative Knowledge Tracing Framework with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17705v1)
- **Authors**: Runze Li, Siyu Wu, Jun Wang, Wei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Tracing (KT) aims to model a student's learning state over time and predict their future performance. However, traditional KT methods often face challenges in explainability, scalability, and effective modeling of complex knowledge dependencies. While Large Language Models (LLMs) present new avenues for KT, their direct application often struggles with generating structured, explainable student representations and lacks mechanisms for continuous, task-specific refinement. To address these gaps, we propose Collaborative Iterative Knowledge Tracing (CIKT), a framework that harnesses LLMs to enhance both prediction accuracy and explainability. CIKT employs a dual-component architecture: an Analyst generates dynamic, explainable user profiles from student historical responses, and a Predictor utilizes these profiles to forecast future performance. The core of CIKT is a synergistic optimization loop. In this loop, the Analyst is iteratively refined based on the predictive accuracy of the Predictor, which conditions on the generated profiles, and the Predictor is subsequently retrained using these enhanced profiles. Evaluated on multiple educational datasets, CIKT demonstrates significant improvements in prediction accuracy, offers enhanced explainability through its dynamically updated user profiles, and exhibits improved scalability. Our work presents a robust and explainable solution for advancing knowledge tracing systems, effectively bridging the gap between predictive performance and model transparency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CIKT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Collaborative Iterative Knowledge Tracing (CIKT), a novel framework leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve both the accuracy and explainability of knowledge tracing (KT). CIKT employs a dual-component architecture: an "Analyst" generates dynamic, explainable student profiles from historical responses, and a "Predictor" utilizes these profiles to forecast future performance. The core innovation is an iterative learning strategy based on Kahneman-Tversky Optimization (KTO). The Predictor's accuracy, conditioned on the Analyst's generated profiles, provides reinforcement-style feedback to refine the Analyst, which is then used to retrain the Predictor.  The authors demonstrate through experiments on multiple educational datasets that CIKT outperforms existing KT models in predictive accuracy while simultaneously offering enhanced explainability through its generated student profiles and scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its collaborative and iterative approach to LLM-based knowledge tracing. While LLMs have been applied to various tasks, their use in KT, especially with a focus on explainability and continuous refinement via reinforcement-style feedback (KTO), is a relatively unexplored area. The dual-component architecture is also well-defined and clearly explained. The novelty of continuous, reinforcement learning-based model refinement is promising.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in KT: the lack of explainability in deep learning-based models. By generating human-understandable student profiles, CIKT makes the decision-making process of the KT system more transparent and trustworthy. The improved scalability is important for real-world applications, and the performance improvement is significant. The use of a framework that can be iteratively refined based on domain-specific feedback is a practical and valuable contribution, addressing a common limitation of many current LLM applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing KT methods and the challenges of directly applying LLMs to KT.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** CIKT's architecture and iterative process are well-defined and easy to understand.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on multiple datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of CIKT. The inclusion of ablation studies helps to isolate the contributions of each component.
    *   **Focus on Explainability:** The paper emphasizes the importance of explainability and provides a detailed case study to illustrate how CIKT's generated profiles can enhance understanding of student learning.
    *   **Rigorous Experimental Methodology:** The evaluation is thorough with consideration given to long sequence lengths and comparison against several baselines.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Ablation Analysis:** The ablation study could be improved by ablating specific parts of the prompt engineering or reinforcement learning structure in the iterative optimization loop to fully isolate their impact.
    *   **Qualitative Case Study is brief**: It is only a single study, and it may benefit from additional analysis of when the system is incorrect to understand limitations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of knowledge tracing by promoting the use of LLMs in a more explainable and adaptable way. The iterative optimization approach could be adopted by other researchers working on LLM-based educational applications. The focus on generating human-understandable student profiles could lead to the development of more personalized and effective learning interventions.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by effectively integrating LLMs into a KT framework, with a strong emphasis on explainability and iterative refinement. The experimental results are compelling, and the framework addresses a significant challenge in KT. While there are some weaknesses in the ablation analysis and qualitative case study, the overall quality and potential impact of the work justify a high score.
**Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Understanding How Value Neurons Shape the Generation of Specified Values in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17712v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Su, Jiayi Zhang, Shu Yang, Xinhai Wang, Lijie Hu, Di Wang
- **Abstract**: Rapid integration of large language models (LLMs) into societal applications has intensified concerns about their alignment with universal ethical principles, as their internal value representations remain opaque despite behavioral alignment advancements. Current approaches struggle to systematically interpret how values are encoded in neural architectures, limited by datasets that prioritize superficial judgments over mechanistic analysis. We introduce ValueLocate, a mechanistic interpretability framework grounded in the Schwartz Values Survey, to address this gap. Our method first constructs ValueInsight, a dataset that operationalizes four dimensions of universal value through behavioral contexts in the real world. Leveraging this dataset, we develop a neuron identification method that calculates activation differences between opposing value aspects, enabling precise localization of value-critical neurons without relying on computationally intensive attribution methods. Our proposed validation method demonstrates that targeted manipulation of these neurons effectively alters model value orientations, establishing causal relationships between neurons and value representations. This work advances the foundation for value alignment by bridging psychological value frameworks with neuron analysis in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Understanding How Value Neurons Shape the Generation of Specified Values in LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of understanding how values are represented and encoded within Large Language Models (LLMs).  The authors argue that existing datasets for value alignment are limited because they focus on superficial judgments rather than the underlying mechanisms of value representation. To address this, they introduce a mechanistic interpretability (MI) framework called "ValueLocate."

The core components of ValueLocate are:

1.  **ValueInsight Dataset:**  A new dataset grounded in the Schwartz Values Survey, operationalizing four dimensions of universal values through scenario-based open-ended questions. This dataset aims to elicit contextually appropriate responses aligned with specific values.

2.  **Neuron Identification Method:**  A method to identify value-critical neurons by calculating activation differences between opposing aspects of a value (e.g., Conservation vs. its opposite). This approach aims to be more precise and efficient than computationally intensive attribution methods.

3.  **Validation Method:** A technique to manipulate model value orientations by targeting the identified neurons and altering their activations. The authors conduct experiments to demonstrate that manipulating these neurons effectively changes the model's value preferences.

The paper claims to establish causal relationships between specific neurons and value representations, bridging psychological value frameworks with neuron analysis in LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements. The ValueInsight dataset seems to be a genuinely new contribution, offering a different approach to value alignment data compared to existing datasets that rely heavily on multiple-choice questions or binary judgments. The neuron identification method using activation differences between opposing value aspects has a strong conceptual appeal and *could* potentially improve efficiency compared to standard attribution methods, although this requires further empirical validation. The validation method of editing values through neuron manipulation builds on existing MI techniques but applies them to the specific and important domain of value alignment.

**Significance:**  The problem of value alignment in LLMs is undeniably significant, given their increasing societal impact and potential for misalignment with human values. The paper's aim to move beyond behavioral alignment towards a mechanistic understanding of value representation is a promising direction. If the proposed methods are robust and scalable, they could contribute to the development of more theoretically grounded and reliable value alignment frameworks. A key impact would be enabling more targeted interventions for mitigating biases and promoting ethical behavior in LLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and the need for a mechanistic understanding of value representation.
*   **Theoretically Grounded:** Grounding the work in the Schwartz Values Survey provides a solid psychological framework for defining and operationalizing values.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The ValueLocate framework is clearly described, with well-defined steps for dataset construction, neuron identification, and validation.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The authors present experimental results to support their claims, demonstrating that manipulating identified neurons can alter model value orientations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to four high-order value dimensions from the Schwartz Values Survey.  While these are broad, the method's scalability to a more granular and comprehensive set of values needs further investigation.
*   **Efficiency Claim:** The claim of improved efficiency for neuron identification compared to attribution methods needs more rigorous empirical backing. The paper doesn't provide detailed comparisons of computational cost or runtime.
*   **Dataset Bias:** While ValueInsight is presented as an improvement, it's still vulnerable to potential biases introduced during data generation and annotation. The extent of these biases and their impact on neuron identification and alignment requires careful consideration. How the prompts and scenarios within ValueInsight were designed to be truly neutral and unbiased from a *cultural* perspective is crucial but not detailed enough. The reliance on LLMs for some stages of dataset creation may also introduce biases from the LLM itself.
*   **Statistical Significance:** While the paper presents results showing the effectiveness of ValueLocate, more rigorous statistical analysis, including effect sizes and confidence intervals, is needed to establish the robustness of the findings.
*   **Interpretability:** While the method identifies "value neurons," the extent to which we can interpret *why* these neurons are critical for value representation is still limited. Connecting these neurons to more high-level concepts or semantic meanings would further enhance the interpretability of the framework.
* **Generalization:** It is hard to judge how the identified neurons and editing methods would generalize across different LLM architectures.
* **Causality:** While the study tries to establish a causal relationship, it is always hard to claim full causality in LLMs.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could potentially influence the field by:

*   Inspiring the development of more mechanistically informed value alignment frameworks.
*   Providing a practical toolkit for analyzing and manipulating value representations in LLMs.
*   Encouraging further research on the relationship between psychological value frameworks and neural architectures.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing a novel MI framework for understanding value representation in LLMs. The ValueInsight dataset and neuron identification method are promising, but the paper has limitations in scope, empirical validation, and interpretability.  While the results suggest a potential for targeted value alignment, further research is needed to address the weaknesses and realize the full potential of ValueLocate.

**Score: 6.5**

*   **Justification:** The score reflects the paper's novelty and significance but also acknowledges its limitations. The core ideas are promising, the experimental results provide initial support, and the paper opens new avenues for research. However, the weaknesses related to scope, validation, and interpretability prevent a higher score. The .5 part is used for the dataset being a contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Get Experience from Practice: LLM Agents with Record & Replay
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17716v1)
- **Authors**: Erhu Feng, Wenbo Zhou, Zibin Liu, Le Chen, Yunpeng Dong, Cheng Zhang, Yisheng Zhao, Dong Du, Zhichao Hua, Yubin Xia, Haibo Chen
- **Abstract**: AI agents, empowered by Large Language Models (LLMs) and communication protocols such as MCP and A2A, have rapidly evolved from simple chatbots to autonomous entities capable of executing complex, multi-step tasks, demonstrating great potential. However, the LLMs' inherent uncertainty and heavy computational resource requirements pose four significant challenges to the development of safe and efficient agents: reliability, privacy, cost and performance. Existing approaches, like model alignment, workflow constraints and on-device model deployment, can partially alleviate some issues but often with limitations, failing to fundamentally resolve these challenges. This paper proposes a new paradigm called AgentRR (Agent Record & Replay), which introduces the classical record-and-replay mechanism into AI agent frameworks. The core idea is to: 1. Record an agent's interaction trace with its environment and internal decision process during task execution, 2. Summarize this trace into a structured "experience" encapsulating the workflow and constraints, and 3. Replay these experiences in subsequent similar tasks to guide the agent's behavior. We detail a multi-level experience abstraction method and a check function mechanism in AgentRR: the former balances experience specificity and generality, while the latter serves as a trust anchor to ensure completeness and safety during replay. In addition, we explore multiple application modes of AgentRR, including user-recorded task demonstration, large-small model collaboration and privacy-aware agent execution, and envision an experience repository for sharing and reusing knowledge to further reduce deployment cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AgentRR, a novel paradigm for AI agents inspired by the record-and-replay (R&R) techniques used in software engineering. AgentRR aims to address challenges with LLM-based agents, specifically reliability, privacy, cost, and performance. The key idea involves recording an agent's interaction trace, summarizing it into a structured "experience," and replaying these experiences in subsequent similar tasks. AgentRR introduces multi-level experience abstraction (balancing specificity and generality) and check functions (acting as trust anchors). The paper explores different application modes such as user demonstration, large-small model collaboration, and privacy-aware execution, even envisioning an experience repository for knowledge sharing. It acknowledges challenges relating to experience completeness, replay robustness, generalization, and scope of application. The authors conduct theoretical analysis, demonstrate practical applications in common agent scenarios, and propose future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of applying record-and-replay to LLM agents is intriguing and offers a different perspective from existing model-centric solutions (e.g., alignment, distillation, pruning). The concept of "experience" and multi-level abstraction, along with check functions, adds further nuance and attempts to bridge the gap between deterministic R&R and the probabilistic nature of LLMs. The *combination* of these elements makes it a novel approach.
*   **Significance:** If successful, AgentRR has the potential to address critical limitations of LLM-based agents. The potential for enhanced reliability (reducing hallucinations), improved privacy (localized replay), lower operational cost (less reliance on large LLMs), and increased efficiency (faster execution) would be a significant advancement. The notion of an "experience store" opens up possibilities for sharing and reusing knowledge. It shifts the focus from constant model training to experience acquisition and refinement.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current agent architectures and motivates the AgentRR approach.
    *   It proposes a well-defined framework with key components like multi-level experience and check functions.
    *   It provides a comprehensive analysis of the advantages of AgentRR in terms of reliability, privacy, cost, and performance.
    *   The exploration of different application modes and the vision of an experience repository are valuable.
    *   The paper acknowledges the challenges and limitations of AgentRR, fostering a balanced view.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Implementation and Empirical Validation:**  The paper lacks substantial experimental results.  While it mentions a case study, a far more thorough and systematic evaluation is needed to demonstrate the effectiveness of AgentRR across diverse tasks and scenarios.  The "case study" appears more like an illustration of concepts, rather than a robust empirical test.
    *   **Practical Feasibility of Experience Abstraction:**  The success of AgentRR heavily depends on the ability to effectively abstract and summarize interaction traces into reusable experiences. The paper doesn't delve deeply into the technical challenges of this process, particularly the development of robust check functions.  How *exactly* are the check functions generated and what guarantees do they provide?  This needs much more detail.
    *   **Robustness and Generalization Limitations:**  The challenges of replay robustness and generalization in the face of environmental changes or task variations are significant and require more in-depth consideration. How does AgentRR handle completely new scenarios for which no experience exists?
    *   **Overselling of Benefits:**  The paper sometimes appears to oversell the potential benefits of AgentRR without providing strong evidence. For example, claiming execution "exceeds human speed" requires thorough benchmarking.

*   **Potential Influence:**  AgentRR has the potential to inspire new research directions in AI agent architectures by shifting the focus from pure model-centric approaches to experience-driven systems. It could lead to more reliable, efficient, and privacy-conscious agents, particularly in constrained environments.

**Justification for Score:**

The core concept of AgentRR – applying record-and-replay to AI agents to improve reliability, cost, and privacy – is novel and potentially significant. However, the paper suffers from a crucial weakness: the lack of substantial empirical validation. The claims regarding performance and benefits are largely theoretical and require more robust experimental evidence. Furthermore, details surrounding the practical implementation of experience abstraction and check function generation are insufficient.  The challenges are well-articulated, but concrete solutions are not fully fleshed out. While it opens up a promising avenue for research, the *current* paper lacks the necessary empirical evidence and technical depth to warrant a high score. It is more of a "vision" paper than a paper with a solid empirical foundation.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### SeaLion: Semantic Part-Aware Latent Point Diffusion Models for 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17721v1)
- **Authors**: Dekai Zhu, Yan Di, Stefan Gavranovic, Slobodan Ilic
- **Abstract**: Denoising diffusion probabilistic models have achieved significant success in point cloud generation, enabling numerous downstream applications, such as generative data augmentation and 3D model editing. However, little attention has been given to generating point clouds with point-wise segmentation labels, as well as to developing evaluation metrics for this task. Therefore, in this paper, we present SeaLion, a novel diffusion model designed to generate high-quality and diverse point clouds with fine-grained segmentation labels. Specifically, we introduce the semantic part-aware latent point diffusion technique, which leverages the intermediate features of the generative models to jointly predict the noise for perturbed latent points and associated part segmentation labels during the denoising process, and subsequently decodes the latent points to point clouds conditioned on part segmentation labels. To effectively evaluate the quality of generated point clouds, we introduce a novel point cloud pairwise distance calculation method named part-aware Chamfer distance (p-CD). This method enables existing metrics, such as 1-NNA, to measure both the local structural quality and inter-part coherence of generated point clouds. Experiments on the large-scale synthetic dataset ShapeNet and real-world medical dataset IntrA demonstrate that SeaLion achieves remarkable performance in generation quality and diversity, outperforming the existing state-of-the-art model, DiffFacto, by 13.33% and 6.52% on 1-NNA (p-CD) across the two datasets. Experimental analysis shows that SeaLion can be trained semi-supervised, thereby reducing the demand for labeling efforts. Lastly, we validate the applicability of SeaLion in generative data augmentation for training segmentation models and the capability of SeaLion to serve as a tool for part-aware 3D shape editing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SeaLion, a novel diffusion model for generating 3D point clouds with fine-grained semantic segmentation labels. It proposes a "semantic part-aware latent point diffusion" technique that jointly predicts the noise for latent points and associated segmentation labels during the denoising process. The model incorporates part segmentation labels as conditional information in the decoder to align point coordinates and segmentation labels.  To evaluate the generated point clouds, the paper introduces a new metric called "part-aware Chamfer distance" (p-CD).  Experiments on ShapeNet and IntrA datasets demonstrate SeaLion's improved performance in generation quality and diversity compared to DiffFacto. The paper also explores semi-supervised training, generative data augmentation, and part-aware 3D shape editing using SeaLion.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:
    *   **Semantic Part-Aware Latent Point Diffusion:**  The core idea of jointly predicting noise and segmentation labels in the latent space is a meaningful contribution.  Leveraging intermediate features from diffusion models for segmentation, building upon the observation that these features can capture semantic information, is a valuable insight.
    *   **Part-Aware Chamfer Distance (p-CD):** Addressing the limitations of existing metrics by proposing a distance function that considers segmentation information is crucial for evaluating this type of generative model. This helps to solve a significant problem, particularly as many existing metrics ignore the segmentation aspect.
    *   **Semi-Supervised Training for Labeled Point Clouds:** This part is very important as collecting annotated 3D data is expensive. The paper demonstrates a path forward to reduce this manual annotation efforts.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Improved Generation Quality:** The experimental results clearly demonstrate that SeaLion outperforms DiffFacto (a recent and relevant baseline) in terms of generation quality and diversity. The reported performance gains on established datasets like ShapeNet and IntrA are solid. The proposed p-CD also helps in more accurate evaluations.
    *   **Downstream Applications:** The paper shows applications like generative data augmentation for segmentation models and part-aware 3D shape editing, suggesting practical uses for the generated point clouds.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The approach is well-motivated and addresses a clear gap in the literature. Existing work has largely ignored generating semantically labeled point clouds or lacked adequate metrics.
    *   The technical approach is sound, building on existing diffusion models and incorporating novel components in a sensible way.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, with results on multiple datasets and comparisons to appropriate baselines. The ablation studies further justify the design choices.
    *   The paper is clearly written and well-structured.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The gains, while significant, are incremental rather than revolutionary. It leverages existing diffusion techniques but introduces a clever and useful modification.
    *   While the downstream applications are presented, they lack the depth of an independently validated real-world deployment.  More details on integrating SeaLion into existing editing workflows would enhance practical applicability.
    *   The comparison is mainly to DiffFacto and a two-step method (Lion+segmentation). A comparison against other general point cloud generation models (without segmentation) using a segmentation network trained on ground truth labels may be helpful for establishing the model's generation capability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in 3D point cloud generation, particularly in the areas of semantic generation and evaluation metrics. The demonstrated applications have the potential to impact areas like medical image analysis and CAD design.

*   **Score Justification:** Overall, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field of 3D point cloud generation. It combines incremental but important technical advances (latent space manipulation, a new evaluation metric) with a well-executed experimental evaluation. The weaknesses are minor. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the paper's novelty, significance, and potential to influence future research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Slot-MLLM: Object-Centric Visual Tokenization for Multimodal LLM
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17726v1)
- **Authors**: Donghwan Chi, Hyomin Kim, Yoonjin Oh, Yongjin Kim, Donghoon Lee, Daejin Jo, Jongmin Kim, Junyeob Baek, Sungjin Ahn, Sungwoong Kim
- **Abstract**: Recently, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have emerged as a key approach in achieving artificial general intelligence. In particular, vision-language MLLMs have been developed to generate not only text but also visual outputs from multimodal inputs. This advancement requires efficient image tokens that LLMs can process effectively both in input and output. However, existing image tokenization methods for MLLMs typically capture only global abstract concepts or uniformly segmented image patches, restricting MLLMs' capability to effectively understand or generate detailed visual content, particularly at the object level. To address this limitation, we propose an object-centric visual tokenizer based on Slot Attention specifically for MLLMs. In particular, based on the Q-Former encoder, diffusion decoder, and residual vector quantization, our proposed discretized slot tokens can encode local visual details while maintaining high-level semantics, and also align with textual data to be integrated seamlessly within a unified next-token prediction framework of LLMs. The resulting Slot-MLLM demonstrates significant performance improvements over baselines with previous visual tokenizers across various vision-language tasks that entail local detailed comprehension and generation. Notably, this work is the first demonstration of the feasibility of object-centric slot attention performed with MLLMs and in-the-wild natural images.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Slot-MLLM," a new approach for visual tokenization designed to improve multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  The core idea is to use an object-centric visual tokenizer based on Slot Attention to generate discrete visual tokens. This "Slot Q-Former" aims to encode local visual details while preserving high-level semantics and aligning with textual data for seamless integration into a unified LLM framework. The authors show improved performance on tasks requiring detailed visual comprehension and generation, particularly text-based image editing, suggesting the model's ability to better understand and manipulate visual content at an object level. The key contributions include the object-centric tokenizer, image-text alignment training for the tokenizer, and empirical validation of Slot-MLLM's performance against baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of Slot Attention within the Q-Former architecture specifically for the purpose of generating visual tokens for MLLMs. While Slot Attention itself is not new, its application in this context, particularly for handling real-world images within a large-scale multimodal framework, appears to be a novel contribution. The residual vector quantization further helps align visual and text data, enabling the unified LLM framework. Comparing this paper with concurrent work is important. The paper distinguishes itself from approaches like SEED, which capture mainly global semantics, and earlier attempts at local semantic tokens that require ad-hoc clustering/merging processes.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to address a crucial limitation in current MLLMs: the ability to effectively understand and generate detailed visual content at the object level. If validated and broadly adopted, this could significantly enhance MLLMs' performance on tasks such as image editing, visual question answering, and potentially even more complex interactive tasks. The experiments seem to show this potential.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Object-centric representation:**  The core idea of using Slot Attention to create object-centric visual tokens is well-motivated and theoretically sound.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation across a variety of vision-language tasks, including image captioning, VQA, and image editing.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components of the proposed architecture (Slot Attention, diffusion loss, image-text alignment).
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational cost:** The architecture likely requires significant computational resources for training, which could limit its accessibility. This should be further explored.
    *   **Limited qualitative examples:** The qualitative examples in the paper, especially in image editing tasks, while promising, don’t demonstrate the full extent of the model's manipulation capabilities. More complex examples are needed.
    *   **Potential for misuse:** Like many powerful generative models, Slot-MLLM could be misused to create deceptive or harmful content. The paper acknowledges this and includes guidelines for responsible usage, but the effectiveness of these guidelines is yet to be seen.
    *   **The need for more comparisons to other leading-edge methods.** The number of baseline comparisons that could be made is high. Are these improvements significant for practical LLM development? This analysis might require additional detail.

*   **Potential influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in MLLMs by highlighting the importance of object-centric visual representations and providing a practical approach for achieving this. This might stimulate further investigations into this area.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper provides a novel way to improve visual tokenization in multimodal LLMs. The integration of Slot Attention is well-motivated, and the empirical results are encouraging. However, some issues should be addressed: high computing resources, additional comparison and a larger number of qualitative analysis for visual output.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Discrete Neural Flow Samplers with Locally Equivariant Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17741v1)
- **Authors**: Zijing Ou, Ruixiang Zhang, Yingzhen Li
- **Abstract**: Sampling from unnormalised discrete distributions is a fundamental problem across various domains. While Markov chain Monte Carlo offers a principled approach, it often suffers from slow mixing and poor convergence. In this paper, we propose Discrete Neural Flow Samplers (DNFS), a trainable and efficient framework for discrete sampling. DNFS learns the rate matrix of a continuous-time Markov chain such that the resulting dynamics satisfy the Kolmogorov equation. As this objective involves the intractable partition function, we then employ control variates to reduce the variance of its Monte Carlo estimation, leading to a coordinate descent learning algorithm. To further facilitate computational efficiency, we propose locally equivaraint Transformer, a novel parameterisation of the rate matrix that significantly improves training efficiency while preserving powerful network expressiveness. Empirically, we demonstrate the efficacy of DNFS in a wide range of applications, including sampling from unnormalised distributions, training discrete energy-based models, and solving combinatorial optimisation problems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Discrete Neural Flow Samplers with Locally Equivariant Transformer":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes Discrete Neural Flow Samplers (DNFS), a novel framework for sampling from unnormalized discrete distributions. DNFS learns the rate matrix of a continuous-time Markov chain (CTMC) such that the resulting dynamics satisfy the Kolmogorov equation. To address the computational challenges of directly optimizing this objective, the authors employ control variates to reduce variance and coordinate descent for optimization. Furthermore, they introduce a locally equivariant Transformer architecture to improve computational efficiency while maintaining network expressiveness. The authors demonstrate the efficacy of DNFS in various applications, including sampling from unnormalized distributions, training discrete energy-based models (EBMs), and solving combinatorial optimization problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing ideas, but its particular synthesis exhibits novelty:
    *   The core idea of learning a CTMC for discrete sampling is not entirely new, drawing inspiration from prior works on discrete diffusion and continuous-time Markov chains. The Holderrieth et al., 2025 paper, LEAPS, also learns a CTMC. The DNFS approach, however, presents a different approach to estimating dlog(Zt) and incorporates control variates.
    *   The use of control variates to reduce variance in the Kolmogorov equation objective is a novel contribution.
    *   The Locally Equivariant Transformer architecture is also a strong element of novelty. Building a practical, scalable, locally equivariant network is a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The problem of sampling from unnormalized discrete distributions is fundamental in many areas of science and engineering. Efficient and scalable samplers can have broad impacts.
    *   The applications explored are compelling, particularly the training of discrete EBMs without relying on MCMC.
    *   The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach, outperforming several baselines in various tasks. The claim of generalizability also increases this paper's impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly presents the proposed methodology.
    *   The theoretical motivation is strong, with a detailed explanation of the use of control variates.
    *   The introduction of the locally equivariant Transformer is a valuable architectural contribution.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, covering several different applications and comparing against relevant baselines.
    *   The ablation studies in the Appendix are appreciated, supporting the choice of architectural decisions and estimation methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper heavily builds upon previously published results. It might not be immediately obvious if a method provides a **substantial** advantage versus a **marginal** advance.
    *   While the Locally Equivariant Transformer is a strong technical contribution, more could be done to theoretically characterize its expressiveness. This would assist the reader to see that it can, in fact, generalize to the problem at hand.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The DNFS framework has the potential to become a widely used tool for sampling from unnormalized discrete distributions, particularly in the context of training EBMs and solving combinatorial optimization problems.
    *   The locally equivariant Transformer architecture may find broader applications in other domains.
    *   The use of control variates in the Kolmogorov equation objective provides a promising direction for future research.

*   **Score Rationale:**
    Given the combination of existing ideas and the novelty of the control variate integration and specific network architecture design, I believe a score of 7 is justified. This is a solid contribution that combines existing techniques with interesting architectural choices and significant engineering effort to produce valuable and scalable results. However, a higher score is prevented due to the significant reliance on prior work and room for greater theoretical insight.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fast Quiet-STaR: Thinking Without Thought Tokens
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17746v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Huang, Yizhe Xiong, Xin Ye, Zhijie Deng, Hui Chen, Zijia Lin, Guiguang Ding
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved impressive performance across a range of natural language processing tasks. However, recent advances demonstrate that further gains particularly in complex reasoning tasks require more than merely scaling up model sizes or training data. One promising direction is to enable models to think during the reasoning process. Recently, Quiet STaR significantly improves reasoning by generating token-level thought traces, but incurs substantial inference overhead. In this work, we propose Fast Quiet STaR, a more efficient reasoning framework that preserves the benefits of token-level reasoning while reducing computational cost. Our method introduces a curriculum learning based training strategy that gradually reduces the number of thought tokens, enabling the model to internalize more abstract and concise reasoning processes. We further extend this approach to the standard Next Token Prediction (NTP) setting through reinforcement learning-based fine-tuning, resulting in Fast Quiet-STaR NTP, which eliminates the need for explicit thought token generation during inference. Experiments on four benchmark datasets with Mistral 7B and Qwen2.5 7B demonstrate that Fast Quiet-STaR consistently outperforms Quiet-STaR in terms of average accuracy under the same inference time budget. Notably, Fast Quiet-STaR NTP achieves an average accuracy improvement of 9\% on Mistral 7B and 5.7\% on Qwen2.5 7B, while maintaining the same inference latency. Our code will be available at https://github.com/huangwei200012/Fast-Quiet-STaR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fast Quiet-STaR: Thinking Without Thought Tokens":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the inference efficiency bottleneck of the Quiet-STaR reasoning framework.  Quiet-STaR enhances language model reasoning by generating token-level "thought traces," but this incurs significant computational overhead. The authors propose Fast Quiet-STaR, a more efficient approach. They introduce a curriculum-learning-based training strategy to gradually reduce the number of thought tokens, allowing the model to internalize more abstract reasoning processes. They further extend this to a standard next-token prediction (NTP) setting through reinforcement learning-based fine-tuning, eliminating the need for explicit thought token generation during inference.  Experiments on Mistral 7B and Qwen2.5 7B demonstrate that Fast Quiet-STaR improves accuracy under the same inference time budget, with Fast Quiet-STaR NTP achieving substantial accuracy improvements while maintaining the original inference latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces two main novelties:

1.  **Curriculum Learning for Thought Token Compression:**  Applying curriculum learning to progressively reduce the number of thought tokens is a reasonable and intuitively sound approach to improve efficiency. This addresses a clear limitation of the original Quiet-STaR.
2.  **Reinforcement Learning for NTP Conversion:** Using RL to fine-tune the Fast Quiet-STaR model into a standard NTP framework *without* explicit thought tokens is a valuable contribution. It bridges the gap between the "thinking" paradigm and more traditional inference methods.

The originality lies in how these two techniques are combined and adapted for this specific reasoning framework. While curriculum learning and RL are established techniques, their application to token-level reasoning traces and subsequent NTP conversion is innovative.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it tackles a major impediment to the widespread adoption of Quiet-STaR: its high inference cost. By making the reasoning process more efficient without sacrificing (and even improving) accuracy, the authors increase the practical utility of the approach. The fact that they can match or surpass the performance of regular LLMs *at the same inference speed* makes it a compelling advancement.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of the original Quiet-STaR.
*   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The curriculum learning and RL fine-tuning strategies are well-justified and address the identified problems directly.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experiments are comprehensive, using multiple datasets and models to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
*   **Practical Relevance:**  The improvements in inference speed are practically relevant, making the approach more viable for real-world applications.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Quiet-STaR:** The paper builds directly on Quiet-STaR, so the impact is inherently tied to that framework's value. It might not be broadly applicable to *all* reasoning approaches.
*   **Limited Analysis of Thought Traces:** While the paper touches on the thought token analysis, further qualitative analysis of the *content* of the compressed thought traces could provide deeper insights into how the model learns to reason more efficiently. What *specifically* is the model internalizing and representing with fewer tokens?
*   **Generalizability of RL Rewards:**  The RL reward function relies on the log-likelihood from the Quiet-STaR (8-4) model.  The generalizability of this particular reward function, if applied to different base models, needs further investigation.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by significantly improving the efficiency of a promising reasoning framework. The use of curriculum learning and RL fine-tuning is well-executed and effectively addresses the original framework's limitations.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper earns a 7.5 due to its *significant* improvements in inference efficiency of the Quiet-STaR framework with novel adaptations of existing methods. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate these improvements. However, a more detailed qualitative analysis of the learned "compressed thought processes", alongside a wider investigation of the generalizability of its RL-based rewards, could elevate its impact. The reliance on a specific (though promising) prior framework holds it back from a truly breakthrough score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### But what is your honest answer? Aiding LLM-judges with honest alternatives using steering vectors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17760v1)
- **Authors**: Leon Eshuijs, Archie Chaudhury, Alan McBeth, Ethan Nguyen
- **Abstract**: Recent safety evaluations of Large Language Models (LLMs) show that many models exhibit dishonest behavior, such as sycophancy. However, most honesty benchmarks focus exclusively on factual knowledge or explicitly harmful behavior and rely on external judges, which are often unable to detect less obvious forms of dishonesty. In this work, we introduce a new framework, Judge Using Safety-Steered Alternatives (JUSSA), which utilizes steering vectors trained on a single sample to elicit more honest responses from models, helping LLM-judges in the detection of dishonest behavior. To test our framework, we introduce a new manipulation dataset with prompts specifically designed to elicit deceptive responses. We find that JUSSA enables LLM judges to better differentiate between dishonest and benign responses, and helps them identify subtle instances of manipulative behavior.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces JUSSA (Judge Using Safety-Steered Alternatives), a framework designed to improve the ability of LLM judges to detect subtle instances of dishonest behavior in other LLMs. JUSSA uses steering vectors, trained on single samples, to generate more honest alternative responses, which are then compared to the original responses by the LLM judge. The authors modify an existing sycophancy dataset and create a new dataset focusing on various forms of manipulation to evaluate the framework. The results suggest that JUSSA improves the detection of dishonest content, including sycophancy and manipulation, compared to single-sample judging methods. The paper also highlights that steering vectors help provide honest alternatives that enable better discrimination between benign and dishonest responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses an important and timely problem: evaluating the honesty and safety of LLMs beyond simple factual accuracy. The use of steering vectors to generate more honest alternatives for judging purposes is a novel approach. It moves beyond traditional black-box evaluations by providing a mechanism to perturb the responses and observe how the judge reacts, giving a more nuanced understanding of the model's behavior.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The idea of using steering vectors to create more honest alternatives for LLM judge comparison is innovative. It offers a way to challenge models on subtle aspects of dishonesty that are often missed by traditional methods.
*   **New Dataset:** The creation of a new dataset focused on various forms of manipulation expands the scope of honesty evaluations beyond sycophancy and factual accuracy. This dataset addresses limitations in existing benchmarks, moving beyond assessment of factual claims into subjective judgements, advice, and assessments.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments show that JUSSA improves judges' abilities to detect dishonest responses, particularly those involving subtle manipulation. The analysis of the results includes various metrics, such as sycophancy scores, agreement rates, embedding similarity and generation entropy.
*   **Clarity of Method:** The methodology is well-described, making it replicable by other researchers.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset Dependency:** The effectiveness of JUSSA is inherently tied to the quality of the base and provoked datasets. As acknowledged by the authors, creating "perfect" datasets for manipulation evaluation is challenging. The creation of more specific questions for each type of manipulation or the construction of a more balanced set of questions could improve the results obtained.
*   **Model Specificity:** The datasets and steering vectors are tailored to the Gemma-2b-it model. It is unclear how well JUSSA would generalize to other LLMs with different architectures or pre-training data. A potential weakness in the approach lies in the fact that a steering vector trained on one model's latent space is being used in another model's.
*   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the paper evaluates sycophancy and manipulation, it does not consider other important aspects of honesty, such as transparency and intention.
*   **Evaluation Methodology:** While LLM judges are an interesting and scalable concept, they may be biased. More insights and justification regarding the appropriateness of selecting gpt4.1-nano or Claude3.5-haiku should have been provided.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of LLM safety evaluation. JUSSA provides a framework for more nuanced assessment of honesty and can contribute to the development of more trustworthy and aligned models. By highlighting the importance of evaluating subtle forms of dishonesty, the paper encourages researchers to move beyond simplistic benchmarks.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*   The paper introduces a novel approach to LLM safety evaluation (steering vectors).
*   It develops a new dataset focused on various forms of manipulation.
*   It provides empirical evidence that JUSSA improves judges' abilities to detect dishonest responses.
*   The paper has some limitations, including dataset and model dependency, and a limited scope of evaluation.
*   The potential significance of the work lies in its contribution to the development of more trustworthy and aligned LLMs.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Resolving Conflicting Evidence in Automated Fact-Checking: A Study on Retrieval-Augmented LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17762v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyu Ge, Yuhao Wu, Daniel Wai Kit Chin, Roy Ka-Wei Lee, Rui Cao
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) augmented with retrieval mechanisms have demonstrated significant potential in fact-checking tasks by integrating external knowledge. However, their reliability decreases when confronted with conflicting evidence from sources of varying credibility. This paper presents the first systematic evaluation of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) models for fact-checking in the presence of conflicting evidence. To support this study, we introduce \textbf{CONFACT} (\textbf{Con}flicting Evidence for \textbf{Fact}-Checking) (Dataset available at https://github.com/zoeyyes/CONFACT), a novel dataset comprising questions paired with conflicting information from various sources. Extensive experiments reveal critical vulnerabilities in state-of-the-art RAG methods, particularly in resolving conflicts stemming from differences in media source credibility. To address these challenges, we investigate strategies to integrate media background information into both the retrieval and generation stages. Our results show that effectively incorporating source credibility significantly enhances the ability of RAG models to resolve conflicting evidence and improve fact-checking performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on novelty, significance, and justified scoring:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges faced by Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) models when confronted with conflicting evidence during automated fact-checking. It introduces CONFACT, a novel dataset designed to systematically study this problem, comprising claims paired with conflicting retrieved documents and source credibility annotations. Through extensive experiments, the authors reveal that state-of-the-art RAG models struggle to resolve conflicting evidence, particularly when media source credibility varies. To mitigate these challenges, the paper proposes and evaluates strategies for incorporating media background information (metadata, credibility scores, etc.) into the RAG pipeline during retrieval and generation. The results indicate that effectively integrating source credibility significantly enhances RAG models' ability to resolve conflicting evidence and improve fact-checking performance.  The paper ultimately highlights the need for more robust fact-checking systems that can reason about source credibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary strength of this paper lies in its novel dataset, CONFACT. While existing fact-checking datasets exist, CONFACT specifically targets the crucial problem of conflicting evidence and incorporates source credibility annotations. This is a valuable contribution because it allows for targeted evaluation and development of RAG models capable of handling real-world fact-checking scenarios where information is often contradictory.  The exploration of strategies to incorporate source information into RAG, although drawing on existing ideas of source credibility,  are adapted and systematically evaluated in the fact-checking context. The introduction of the Hybrid-MB provider, while not groundbreaking, represents a practical approach to source credibility estimation when ground truth data is unavailable.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a highly relevant and significant problem. Misinformation and disinformation are pervasive, and automated fact-checking systems are crucial for combating them. The finding that RAG models struggle with conflicting evidence underscores a critical limitation that needs to be addressed for these systems to be reliable. The paper provides a concrete path forward by demonstrating that incorporating source credibility can improve performance, opening avenues for future research.  The results have direct implications for the design of more effective and reliable fact-checking pipelines. The error analysis highlights some fundamental challenges for automated fact-checking, further pointing the way forward.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined research problem and clear objectives.
    *   Carefully constructed dataset addressing a gap in existing resources.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation using multiple RAG models and strategies.
    *   Insightful analysis of the results, identifying limitations and suggesting future directions.
    *   The human evaluation lends further support for the importance of high quality metadata.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Baselines:** While the explored RAG models are reasonable, the baseline models used are relatively simplistic.  The paper could benefit from including more advanced RAG architectures or fine-tuned models, which would offer a more comprehensive comparison.
    *   **Reliance on MBFC:**  The reliance on the Media Bias/Fact Check dataset introduces a potential bias, which the authors acknowledge.  Exploring alternative source credibility datasets or developing a more robust method for assessing source credibility would strengthen the paper.
    *  **Limited discussion of societal impact and potential harms:** The paper focuses largely on performance benchmarks and technical details. While well scoped, the impact on real world users and implications for how these systems are used by journalists and the public could be explored.
    *   **Credibility Estimation:** It relies on predicting credibility scores from limited text summaries. A deeper exploration of methods for estimating source credibility, considering broader contextual factors, could enhance the effectiveness of source-aware RAG.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and reliable fact-checking systems. The CONFACT dataset provides a valuable resource for the research community, and the proposed strategies offer practical guidance for incorporating source credibility into RAG pipelines. The findings highlight the importance of developing more sophisticated methods for assessing source credibility and reasoning about conflicting evidence.

**Score Justification:**

Given the novelty of the dataset, the significance of the problem, and the thoroughness of the experiments, while acknowledging the limitations in the scope of baselines and reliance on MBFC, the paper warrants a **Score: 7.5**.  The paper makes a solid contribution to the field, providing a valuable resource and practical guidance for developing more reliable fact-checking systems. The potential for future research building upon this work is high.

The justification above emphasizes the strengths, acknowledges the limitations, and clearly explains how these factors contribute to the assigned score. It is rigorous and defensible.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### R-Genie: Reasoning-Guided Generative Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17768v1)
- **Authors**: Dong Zhang, Lingfeng He, Rui Yan, Fei Shen, Jinhui Tang
- **Abstract**: While recent advances in image editing have enabled impressive visual synthesis capabilities, current methods remain constrained by explicit textual instructions and limited editing operations, lacking deep comprehension of implicit user intentions and contextual reasoning. In this work, we introduce a new image editing paradigm: reasoning-guided generative editing, which synthesizes images based on complex, multi-faceted textual queries accepting world knowledge and intention inference. To facilitate this task, we first construct a comprehensive dataset featuring over 1,000 image-instruction-edit triples that incorporate rich reasoning contexts and real-world knowledge. We then propose R-Genie: a reasoning-guided generative image editor, which synergizes the generation power of diffusion models with advanced reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models. R-Genie incorporates a reasoning-attention mechanism to bridge linguistic understanding with visual synthesis, enabling it to handle intricate editing requests involving abstract user intentions and contextual reasoning relations. Extensive experimental results validate that R-Genie can equip diffusion models with advanced reasoning-based editing capabilities, unlocking new potentials for intelligent image synthesis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "R-Genie," a novel reasoning-guided generative image editing paradigm.  It addresses the limitations of current instruction-based image editing methods, which struggle with implicit user intentions and contextual reasoning. R-Genie integrates a multimodal large language model (MLLM) with a diffusion model to achieve this.
