### Fann or Flop: A Multigenre, Multiera Benchmark for Arabic Poetry Understanding in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18152v1)
- **Authors**: Wafa Alghallabi, Ritesh Thawkar, Sara Ghaboura, Ketan More, Omkar Thawakar, Hisham Cholakkal, Salman Khan, Rao Muhammad Anwer
- **Abstract**: Arabic poetry stands as one of the most sophisticated and culturally embedded forms of expression in the Arabic language, known for its layered meanings, stylistic diversity, and deep historical continuity. Although large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong performance across languages and tasks, their ability to understand Arabic poetry remains largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce `Fann or Flop`, the first benchmark designed to assess the comprehension of Arabic poetry by LLMs in twelve historical eras, covering 21 core poetic genres and a variety of metrical forms, from classical structures to contemporary free verse. The benchmark comprises a curated corpus of poems with explanations that assess semantic understanding, metaphor interpretation, prosodic awareness, and cultural context. We argue that poetic comprehension offers a strong indicator for testing how good the LLM is in understanding classical Arabic through the Arabic poetry. Unlike surface-level tasks, this domain demands deeper interpretive reasoning and cultural sensitivity. Our evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs shows that most models struggle with poetic understanding despite strong results on standard Arabic benchmarks. We release `Fann or Flop` along with the evaluation suite as an open-source resource to enable rigorous evaluation and advancement for Arabic language models. Code is available at: https://github.com/mbzuai-oryx/FannOrFlop.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fann or Flop: A Multigenre, Multiera Benchmark for Arabic Poetry Understanding in LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Fann or Flop," a new benchmark designed to assess the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to understand Arabic poetry. This benchmark is unique in its focus on the nuances of Arabic poetry, encompassing twelve historical eras, 21 poetic genres, and various metrical forms. It includes a curated corpus of poems with explanations to evaluate semantic understanding, metaphor interpretation, prosodic awareness, and cultural context. The authors argue that Arabic poetry is a strong indicator of an LLM's understanding of classical Arabic due to its complexity and cultural embeddedness. The paper evaluates several state-of-the-art LLMs, revealing that they often struggle with poetic understanding despite strong performance on standard Arabic benchmarks. The "Fann or Flop" dataset and evaluation suite are released as an open-source resource to facilitate further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in its focus on Arabic poetry understanding, a domain largely unexplored in the context of LLMs. While existing Arabic NLP resources address isolated features like dialectal coverage or QA formats, "Fann or Flop" integrates multiple underrepresented dimensions: dialectal diversity, poetic device annotation, verse-level granularity, temporal grounding, and a QA-style evaluation format. This makes it the first comprehensive benchmark of its kind, providing a novel approach for evaluating deep cultural and literary reasoning in Arabic NLP. However, the basic methodology of question-answering and benchmark creation is not, in itself, novel. Novelty stems from the specific context (Arabic poetry) and breadth.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the crucial role Arabic poetry plays in cultural and historical expression, as well as the challenges it presents to LLMs. The authors convincingly argue that poetic comprehension requires deeper interpretive reasoning and cultural sensitivity than surface-level tasks. By demonstrating the shortcomings of current LLMs in this domain, the paper highlights the need for more culturally informed benchmarks and models. This contributes to the growing field of culturally sensitive NLP and encourages the development of models that can better understand and process nuanced cultural expressions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The benchmark's multi-era, multi-genre design ensures broad coverage of Arabic poetic styles and historical contexts.
    *   **Diagnostic Evaluation:** The curated questions target specific aspects of poetic understanding, allowing for a detailed assessment of LLM capabilities.
    *   **Open-Source Release:** The availability of the dataset and evaluation suite fosters community collaboration and promotes future research.
    *   **Demonstrates Clear Need:** The paper clearly identifies a gap in current LLM capabilities and provides a resource to address it.
    *   Rigorous Validation: Human validation of the dataset to ensure linguistic authenticity and interpretive accuracy

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Human Explanations:** The evaluation method relies heavily on human-written explanations for comparison. While this ensures accuracy, it also introduces potential biases and limits scalability compared to fully automated metrics. Furthermore, evaluating LLMs based on matching their output to human-written explanations may inhibit original interpretations.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Specific Model Architectures:** While the paper evaluates several LLMs, it doesn't delve into the specific architectural modifications or training strategies that might improve performance on the benchmark.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Prompt Engineering's Role:** The prompts provided, while helpful, only use basic instruction following techniques. Few-shot learning and chain-of-thought reasoning may further refine evaluation metrics.

*   **Potential Influence:** "Fann or Flop" has the potential to significantly influence the field of Arabic NLP. It can serve as a valuable resource for researchers developing and evaluating culturally sensitive language models. It can also contribute to a better understanding of the limitations of current LLMs and guide future research directions. The benchmark might also spur innovation in tasks related to cultural heritage preservation and digital humanities research in Arabic-speaking regions.

*   **Justification for Score:** Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of 7.5 seems appropriate. The paper's focus on Arabic poetry is novel and significant, but the methodology relies on established techniques with the need for a deeper analysis of model architectures or novel evaluation techniques slightly diminishing the overall impact. The open-source release and demonstrated need contribute strongly to its value, making it a strong and worthy benchmark for the field.

**Score: 7.5**
